L=\omega_{C}(-D)
of such a presentation.

Lemma 2.1
.

(cf. e.g.
[
31
, Lemme 2.6 and pp.101–102]
or
[
11
, Lemma 3.4]
) Let
C
be any smooth, irreducible, projective curve of genus
g
. For any integer
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4
and
i>0
there is no irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{i}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
whose general member
[\mathcal{F}]
corresponds to rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
of speciality
j:=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})>i
.

Proof.

The reasoning is as follows: if
[\mathcal{F}]\in B_{d}^{k_{i}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
is such that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=j>i

then
h^{0}(\mathcal{F})=d-2g+2+j=k_{j}>k_{i}
, so
[\mathcal{F}]\in{\rm Sing}(B_{d}^{k_{i}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d))
(cf. e.g.
[
2
, p. 189]
). Therefore the statement follows from
[
31
, Lemme 2.6]
, from which one deduces that no component
of
B_{d}^{k_{i}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
can be entirely contained or coincide with a component of
B_{d}^{k_{j}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
, for
j>i
(the proof is identical to that in
[
31
, pp.101-102]
for
B^{0}_{d}
,
1\leq d\leq 2g-2
, which uses
elementary transformations
of vector bundles).
∎

Thus, Lemmas
1.12
and
2.1
imply that a general point
[\mathcal{F}]
of any possible irreducible component
\mathcal{B}
of the Brill-Noether locus
B_{d}^{k_{i}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
corresponds therefore to a rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
of degree
d
, presented as (
1.22
) via an effective
L
s.t.
1\leq h^{1}(L)\leq i=i(\mathcal{F})
. The proofs of Theorems
0.2
and
0.5
will therefore split into subsections §
3
, for
i=2
, and §
4
, respectively for
i=3
, each of them dealing with all possible cases for the speciality of such a quotient line bundle
L
.

When
i=2
, it is clear that possibilities are
h^{1}(L)=1,2
, namely the components
\mathcal{B}
of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
will be constructed just in terms of presentations of the bundle
\mathcal{F}
as either of
first type
or of
second type
in the sense of Definition
1.15
, where
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
.

When otherwise
i=3
, according to Theorem
1.14
with
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
one can limit to cases
h^{1}(L)=1,3
in order to find possible obstructions for a component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
to exist (cf. e.g. Propositions
4.8
,

4.9
,
4.13
,
4.32
). Nevertheless, to explicitely construct irreducible components
\mathcal{B}
of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
via
parametric approach
as well as to control the infinitesimal behavior at its general point
[\mathcal{F}]
(namely, the dimension of the kernel of the associated
Petri map

\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
as in Remark
1.10
) we will sometimes allow quotients
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
s.t.
h^{1}(L)=2
, when such a variation to the main subdivision
among
first type
or
second type bundles
as in Definition
1.15
makes it easier to deal with the
parametric construction
of such a component
\mathcal{B}
as well as with the study of its local behavior (cf. e.g. Proposition
4.22
below).

3.
Brill-Noether loci in speciality 2: proof of Theorem
0.2

Here we focus on the Brill-Noether locus
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
for which, from Remark
1.10
, the expected dimension of any of its irreducible component is
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
. In what follows we will show in particular that, for any
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4
as in (
1.17
), the case
h^{1}(L)=1
(resp.,
h^{1}(L)=2
) only produces either the empty set or the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
(resp.,
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
) as in Theorem
0.2
, extending our previous results in
[
10
,
11
]
to the whole range
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4
of interest for
d
(cf. Proposition
1.8
and (
1.17
) above). Thus, when
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)\neq\emptyset
it turns out that its irreducible components contain dense open subsets filled up by bundles either of
first
or of
second type
, as in Definition
1.15
, the former case for
superabundant
components, the latter for
regular
ones.

To do this, recall that for any exact sequence
(u)
as in (
1.22
), if one sets
H^{0}(L)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{u}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(N)
the associated
coboundary map
then, for any integer
t>0
, one can define the
degeneracy locus
:

(3.1)

\mathcal{W}_{t}:=\{u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N)\ |\ {\rm corank}(\partial_{u})\geq t\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N),

which has a natural structure of (locally) determinantal scheme (cf.
[
12
, § 5.2]
for details) and, as such, it has an
expected codimension
, which is

(3.2)

c(t):={\rm max}\{0,\;t(h^{0}(L)-h^{1}(N)+t)\}

(cf.
[
12
, (5.5)]
). In this set–up, one has:

Theorem 3.1
.

(
[
12
, Thm. 5.8, Cor. 5.9]
) Let
C
be a smooth, irreducible, projective curve of genus
g\geq 3
.
Let
0\leq\delta\leq d
be integers and let
L\in\hbox{\rm Pic}^{\delta}(C)
be special and effective and
N\in\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-\delta}(C)
.
Set
l:=h^{0}(L)
,
r:=h^{1}(N)
,
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N))
and assume
r\geq 1,\,l\geq\operatorname{max}\{1,r-1\},\,m\geq l+1
. Then:

(i)
\mathcal{W}_{1}
as in (
3.1
) is irreducible of the expected dimension

m-c(1)=m-(l-r+1)
;

(ii) if
l\geq r
, then
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N)
and, for general
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N)
, the associated coboundary map
\partial_{u}
is surjective whereas, for general
w\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
, one has
{\rm corank}(\partial_{w})=1
.

3.1.
Regular components in speciality 2

In this subsection we prove that, for any
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4
as in (
1.17
), the Brill-Noether locus
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
is either empty or it contains only one irreducible component whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
correspond to a rank-
2
stable bundle
\mathcal{F}
of degree
d
, of
first type
in the sense of Definition
1.15
, namely presented via an extension as in (
1.22
) with the quotient line bundle
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
effective, of speciality
1
.

When not empty, this component will turn out to be exactly the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
as described in Theorem
0.2
; we moreover deduce several properties of the bundle
\mathcal{F}
corresponding to
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
general (e.g., speciality, bounds on its Segre invariant, minimal presentation of
\mathcal{F}
as an extension (
1.22
), etcetera) as well as information on the birational structure of the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
. For values of
d
near the upper-bound
4g-4
, some of these properties have already been discussed in
[
10
,
11
]
. Nevertheless, in order to completely present all possible cases in the whole range of interest for
d
, namely
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4
as in (
1.17
), for reader’s convenience we will also briefly remind main content of the results in
[
10
,
11
]
.

\boxed{4g-6\leq d\leq 4g-4}
In this range the naive count
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d\geq 0
(which holds true iff
d\leq 4g-\frac{11}{2}
) suggests that
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\emptyset
and this is exactly what occurs as stated in Theorem
0.2
-(i). The following is an easy motivation of this fact (cf. also
[
43
, end of page 123]
and
[
11
, Rem. 1.2]
).

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
C
be any smooth, irreducible, projective curve of genus
g\geq 4
. Then, for any
4g-6\leq d\leq 4g-4
one has
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

For
d=4g-4
there is no stable and special vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
of degree
d=4g-4
otherwise
i(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})>0
would imply
h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})>0
, so there would exist an injective morphism
\mathcal{O}_{C}\hookrightarrow\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
, contradicting the stability of
\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
, which has slope
0
in this case.

When otherwise
d=4g-5,4g-6
, if by contradiction
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)\neq\emptyset
and if
[\mathcal{F}]
were one of its element, we could consider the stable and effective residual bundle
\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
, which is of degree
1
and
2
. One concludes as in
[
43
, end of page 123]
.
∎

\boxed{4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7}
This range for
d
was partially considered in
[
10
, Rem. 3.14-(ii)]
and
[
11
, Rem. 3.1]
where, as a direct consequence
of Theorem
0.1
-
(i)
, we observed that
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
is irreducible, consisting only of the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, which is of the expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
and which coincides with the component

\mathcal{B}
mentioned in Teixidor I Bigas’ Theorem
0.1
-(i), existing for any smooth, irreducible, projective curve
C
of genus
g
.

Here we improve our results in
[
10
,
11
]
, by showing the
regularity
of such a component when
C
is a general
\nu
-gonal curve (recall that Theorem
0.1
-(ii) shows the regularity of her component
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
only when
C
is with
general moduli
) and also further properties concerning the birational structure of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
as well as a suitable
parametric representation
of such a component. Precisely, we prove (cf. Theorem
0.2
-(ii)):

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
g\geq 4
,
3\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
and
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then one has:

(i)

B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
is irreducible, consisting only of one component, denoted
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, which is of the expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
;

(ii)

\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is uniruled and generically smooth, so in particular it is regular;

(iii)

if
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
denotes a general point, the corresponding rank-
2
, degree-
d
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
on
C
is of second type (as in Definition
1.15
), arising as an extension of line bundles of the following type:

(iii-1)
for either
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7
, when
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{2}
, or for
3g-4\leq d\leq 4g-7
, when
\frac{g+1}{2}\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, the bundle
\mathcal{F}
fits in an exact sequence of the form:

0\to\omega_{C}(-D)\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0,

where

D\in C^{(4g-5-d)}
and
p\in C
are general;

(iii-2)
for
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-5
, when
\frac{g+1}{2}\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, the bundle
\mathcal{F}
fits in an exact sequence of the form:

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0,

where
p\in C
is general and
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+3}(C)
is general, i.e. special and non-effective.

Proof.

(i) This is an easy consequence of Theorem
0.1
-
(i)
, where for any smooth, irreducible, projective curve
C
of genus
g
, it is proved the existence of a component
\mathcal{B}
, of expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
. Moreover conditions therein, guaranteeing reducibility of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, are:

2n<4g-4-d,\;\;W^{1}_{n}(C)\neq\emptyset\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\operatorname{dim}(W^{1}_{n}(C))\geq 2g+2n-d-5.

Since on a general
\nu
-gonal curve one must have
\nu\leq n
(cf. e.g.
[
1
]
), then
2\nu\leq 2n<4g-4-d
forces
d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, which explains why
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
must be irreducible for
d\geq 4g-4-2\nu
.

(ii)-(iii) In order to give an explicit parametric representation of the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, as in
[
43
, Proofs of
(1)
and of Claim 2]
related to the “Serre dual” version of these cases, the bundle
\mathcal{F}
corresponding to a general point in
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is proved to have a
presentation
of the form

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0,

where
N
of degree
2g-2-2\nu\leq\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2\leq 2g-5
, necessarily special, as
i(\mathcal{F})=2
. We will use this fact when
C
is a general
\nu
-gonal curve to prove (ii) and (iii).

Notice that, in the above exact sequence, one may take the kernel line bundle
N
as
N:=K_{C}-D_{t}
, with
D_{t}\in C^{(t)}
a general effective divisor such that
t=4g-4-d
,
h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t})=1
and
h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{t})=g-t\geq 1
, if and only if
1\leq t\leq g-1
. This is therefore possible if
t=4g-4-d\leq g-1
, namely
d\geq 3g-3
. When otherwise
d=3g-4
and
N
, of degree
d-2g-2
, is general of its degree then
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2=g-2
so
h^{1}(N)=1
by generality, which gives
N=K_{C}-D_{g}
, with
D_{g}\in C^{(g)}
general. On the other hand, since
d\geq 4g-4-2\nu
, notice that
4g-4-2\nu\geq 3g-3
iff
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-1}{2}
.

To sum-up, to study the cases as in
(iii-1)
in the statement, we take
N:=K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}
, where
D_{4g-4-d}\in C^{(4g-4-d)}
is general (cases as in
(iii-2)
will be treated later on). Thus
h^{0}(N)=h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d})=d+4-3g\geq 1
. Using notation as in Theorem
3.1
, one has
r:=h^{1}(N)=h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d})=1
. Moreover
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}))=h^{0}(K_{C}+D_{4g-4-d})=5g-5-d
and, since
h^{1}(K_{C})=1
, similarly as in
[
11
, Proof of Lemma 3.5]
one has that
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d})
, defined as in (
3.1
), turns out to be

(3.3)

{\small{\mathcal{W}}_{1}=\left(\mathrm{coker}\left(H^{0}(K_{C}+D_{4g-4-d})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi^{\vee}}}{{\leftarrow}}H^{0}(K_{C})\otimes H^{0}(D_{4g-4-d})\right)\right)^{\vee},}

i.e.
\mathcal{W}_{1}=\left(\mathrm{im}\;\Phi^{\vee}\right)^{\perp}
is a sub-vector space of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d})
. Since

h^{0}(D_{4g-4-d})=1
, the map
\Phi^{\vee}
is injective and so
\mathcal{W}_{1}
has codimension
g
, namely
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=m-g=4g-5-d\geq 2
; moreover, for
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general, the corresponding rank-two vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
has speciality
2
. We let
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
denote the corresponding linear sub-space in
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-D_{t}))
. The stability of the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, for
D_{4g-4-d}\in C^{(4g-4-d)}
and
[u]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
general, can be proved using its “Serre dual version”

\mathcal{E}_{u}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
as in
[
43
, proof of Claim 2]
.

Since for
D_{4g-4-d}
varying in a suitable dense subset
S\subseteq C^{(4g-4-d)}
each corresponding
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
is a linear space of (constant) dimension
4g-6-d
, these linear spaces fill-up an irreducible scheme, denoted by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, which is ruled over
S
by the linear spaces
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
’s as
D_{4g-4-d}
varies in
S
and which is of dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=(4g-4-d)+(4g-6-d)=8g-10-2d
. By stability and speciality
2
of the general bundle,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
is endowed with a natural (rational) modular map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U^{s}_{C}(d)\\
(D_{4g-4-d},[u])&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\end{array}

for which
\mathrm{im}(\pi)\subseteq B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
.

Claim 3.4
.

The general fiber of
\pi
is
1
-dimensional, isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

proof of Claim
3.4
.

Take
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
general, so
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for

(D_{4g-4-d},[u])\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
general. Then one has that

\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])=\left\{(D_{4g-4-d}^{\prime},u^{\prime})\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}\,|\;\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}\right\}
. Since
2K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}=\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})\simeq\det(\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}})=2K_{C}-D^{\prime}_{4g-4-d}
and since both bundles are presented via the canonical quotient, one gets that
D_{4g-4-d}=D^{\prime}_{4g-4-d}
. For
[u^{\prime}]\in\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])\setminus\{[u]\}
, taking into account that

\mathcal{F}_{u}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}
and that both of them are stable (so simple), there exists therefore an isomorphism
\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{F}_{u}
(which is not an endomorphism) giving rise to the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}0\to&K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}&\to&K_{C}&\to 0\\
&&&\downarrow^{\varphi}&&&\\
0\to&K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\to&K_{C}&\to 0.\end{array}

Thus the maps
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
determine, respectively, two non-zero global sections
s_{1}\neq s_{2}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-K_{C}+D_{4g-4-d}))
. If these sections are linearly independent, they give two non-proportional ways to injects

K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}
as a sub-line bundle of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
; in other words the fiber
\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])
is determined by the family of possible injections (up to scalar multiplication) of
K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}
as a sub-line bundle of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. Thus, if
F_{u}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
denotes the surface scroll associated to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and if
\Gamma
denotes a canonical section of
F_{u}
corresponding to the canonical quotient of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, from (
1.14
) one has therefore
\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])=\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(\mathcal{F}(-K_{C}+D_{4g-4-d}))\simeq|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|
. From the fact that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is of rank two, with
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2K_{C}-D_{t}
and
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2
, by Serre duality one has

\operatorname{dim}(|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|)=h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-K_{C}+D_{4g-4-d}))-1=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})-1=1
. If otherwise the two sections were linearly dependent, one would get a contradiction by
[
12
, Lemma 4.5]
.
∎

Hence
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
has dimension
8g-11-2d=\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
so it is dense in the unique component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, namely the component
\mathcal{B}
as in Theorem
0.1
-(i) existing for any curve
C
, which is of expected dimension and also the only component by (i). Since
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
is dominated by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, which is
ruled
over
S
by the linear spaces
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
’s, it is clear that
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
and so also
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
are
uniruled
. Moreover, since the general fiber of
\pi
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
by Claim
3.4
, it also follows that the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
corresponding to
[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
general is not rigidly specially presented (not rsp), as in Definition
1.6
, via the exact sequence

0\to\omega_{C}(-D_{4g-4-d})\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\omega_{C}\to 0
.

To prove the generic smoothness of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, we need to study the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
for general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
; one can apply arguments similar to those in
[
10
, Prop. 3.9]
. Indeed, from the fact that
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
, for some
[u]
in some fiber
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
over
S
of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, it follows that the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{u}
is the zero-map; in other words

H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\cong H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d})\oplus H^{0}(K_{C})\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;H^{1}(\mathcal{F})\cong H^{1}(K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d})\oplus H^{1}(K_{C}).

This means that, for any such bundle, the domain of the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
coincides with that
of
\mu_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}
, where the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{0}:=(K_{C}-D)\oplus(K_{C})
corresponds to the zero vector in
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d})
. We will concentrate in computing
\mu_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}
. To ease notation, we will set

D:=D_{4g-4-d}
. Observe that

\begin{array}[]{ccl}H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}^{\vee})&\cong&\left(H^{0}(K_{C}-D)\otimes H^{0}(D)\right)\oplus\left(H^{0}(K_{C}-D)\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\right)\oplus\\
&&\left(H^{0}(K_{C})\otimes H^{0}(D)\right)\oplus\left(H^{0}(K_{C})\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\right).\end{array}

Moreover we have
\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}^{\vee}\cong K_{C}\oplus(K_{C}-D)\oplus(K_{C}+D)\oplus K_{C}
. Therefore, for Chern classes reason,

\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}=\mu_{D}\oplus\mu_{K_{C}-D,\mathcal{O}_{C}}\oplus\mu_{K_{C},D}\oplus\mu_{K_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C}}

where the maps

\displaystyle\mu_{D}:H^{0}(D)\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-D)\to H^{0}(K_{C})

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\mu_{K_{C}-D,\mathcal{O}_{c}}:H^{0}(K_{C}-D)\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{0}(K_{C}-D)

\displaystyle\mu_{K_{C},D}:H^{0}(K_{C})\otimes H^{0}(D)\to H^{0}(K_{C}+D)

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\mu_{K_{C},\mathcal{O}_{C}}:H^{0}(K_{C})\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{0}(K_{C})

are natural multiplication maps. Since
h^{0}(D)=1
, all the maps are injective, so it is
\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}
. By semicontinuity on
\mathcal{W}_{1}
, it then follows that also
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective, proving that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is
regular
and
uniruled
in the above cases.

We now focus on the left cases, namely

4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-5,\;{\rm when}\;\frac{g+1}{2}\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor.

In this situation we take the kernel line bundle
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general of its degree; from the bounds on
d
one has
g-4\leq 2g-2-2\nu\leq\mbox{deg}(N)\leq g-2
, so
N
general is non-effective with speciality
h^{1}(N):=r\in\{1,2,3\}
. More precisely, we have

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cccc}{\rm for}\;\nu=\frac{g+1}{2}&&3g-5=4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4\;\;\mbox{and}&1\leq r\leq 2\\
{\rm for}\;\nu=\frac{g+2}{2}&&3g-6=4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4\;\;\mbox{and}&1\leq r\leq 3\end{array}\right.

On the other hand, the case
\nu=\frac{g+2}{2}
cannot occur as
g
, being necessarily even, gives
\frac{g+2}{2}=\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
and, by assumption we have
\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
. Moreover, when
\nu=\frac{g+1}{2}
, then
3g-5=4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4
, neverthless the case
d=3g-4
(so
\mbox{deg}(N)=g-2
) has been already studied in cases
(iii-1)
. Therefore we are left with
d=3g-5
, with
N
general of degree
\mbox{deg}(N)=g-3
as in
(iii-2)
, and by generality
r:=h^{1}(N)=2
. Using notation as in Theorem
3.1
, one has
l:=h^{0}(K_{C})=g
,
m:=5g-5-d
and it is easy to observe that all the numerical assumptions therein are satisfied. Therefore,
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N)
is irreducible of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=7g-9-d
and, for general
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
, the corresponding
coboundary map
\partial_{u}
has corank
1
, so
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2
. As above, we let
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
be the projectification
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
in
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N))
and, for
N
varying in a suitable open dense subset
S\subseteq{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}
, these
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
’s are all of (constant) dimension
7g-10-2d
. Thus, they fill-up an irreducible scheme, denoted once again by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, as
N
varies in
S
, which is of dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=g+(7g-10-2d)=8g-10-2d
.

Furthermore
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
turns out to be
uniruled
indeed, from the proof of
[
12
, Thm. 5.8]
, any
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
is
unirational
, being dominated by an incidence variety
\mathcal{J}
which is a projective bundle, over
\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(K_{C}-N))
, whose fibers are projective spaces of dimension
m-1-l>0
.

The stability of the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, for
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
and
[u]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
general, can be proved once again via the “Serre dual version”

\mathcal{E}_{u}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
using
[
43
, proof of Claim 2]
. By stability and speciality
2
of the general bundle in it,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
is once again endowed with a (rational) modular map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U^{s}_{C}(d)\\
(N,[u])&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\end{array}

and
\mathrm{im}(\pi)\subseteq B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
. Similarly as in Claim
3.4
, one shows that the general fiber of
\pi
is
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, being isomorphic to the complete linear system
|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|
of the canonical section
\Gamma
on the surface scroll
F_{u}
corresponding to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
general in
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
. Hence, as above,
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
has dimension
8g-11-2d=\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
so it is dense in the unique component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, which is therefore
uniruled
as
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
is dominated by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
(which is unirational from above); moreover
[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
general is not rigidly specially presented (not rsp), as in Definition
1.6
, via

(3.4)

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\omega_{C}\to 0.

To study the Petri map, consider the cohomology sequences arising from (
3.4
), namely:

{\small 0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\to H^{0}(K_{C})\to{\rm Im}(\partial_{u})\to 0\;\;{\rm and}\;\;0\to{\rm Coker}(\partial_{u})\simeq\mathbb{C}\to H^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\to H^{1}(K_{C})\cong\mathbb{C}\to 0.}

Set
W\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
the image inside
H^{0}(K_{C})
via the above inclusion

H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(K_{C})
; thus
W
is of codimension
r-1=1
in
H^{0}(K_{C})
. If we take the dual of the second exact sequence and tensor it by
W
, we get:

(3.5)

\begin{array}[]{ccccll}\\[4.30554pt]
&0&&&&\\
&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
0\to&W\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{W,\mathcal{O}_{C}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&W&\subset&H^{0}(K_{C})\\[4.30554pt]
&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&W\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(2K_{C}))&\simeq&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(K_{C}-N))\\[4.30554pt]
&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
0\to&W\otimes{\rm Coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mu_{K_{C},K_{C}-N}}_{|}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)&=&H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)\\[4.30554pt]
&\downarrow&&&&\\
&0&&&&\end{array}

where the first map
\mu_{W,\mathcal{O}_{C}}
is the natural multiplication map, which is injective, the isomorphism in the second row follows from
\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}(-\det(\mathcal{F}_{u}))
and
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})\simeq K_{C}+N
, whereas the latter horizontal map is given by the restriction
{\mu_{K_{C},K_{C}-N}}_{|}
to the vector space
W\otimes{\rm Coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}
of the natural multiplication map
H^{0}(K_{C})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-N)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mu_{K_{C},K_{C}-N}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)
, since one clearly has inclusions
W\subset H^{0}(K_{C})
and
{\rm Coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}\subset H^{0}(K_{C}-N)
as proper sub-vector spaces. This restricted map is also injective as

{\rm Coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb{C}
and because of the composition of
injections
W\hookrightarrow H^{0}(K_{C})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)
, the latter inclusion following from the effectiveness of
K_{C}-N
. Observe that one can complete the right-most column of the previous diagram; indeed, tensoring (
3.4
) by its dual sequence and then tensoring by
K_{C}
gives the following exact diagram:

(3.6)

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\rightarrow&K_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(N+K_{C})\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}&\rightarrow&\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(2K_{C})\cong\mathcal{F}_{u}(K_{C}-N)&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&K_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}(K_{C}-N)&\rightarrow&2K_{C}-N&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&\end{array}

From the cohomology sequence of the right-most column of the previous diagram, we get that (
3.5
) completes to the following commutative diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccll}\\[4.30554pt]
&0&&&&0\\
&\downarrow&&&&\downarrow\\[4.30554pt]
0\to&W\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{W,\mathcal{O}_{C}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&W&\subset&H^{0}(K_{C})\\[4.30554pt]
&\downarrow&&&&\downarrow\\[4.30554pt]
&W\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(2K_{C}))&\simeq&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(K_{C}-N))\\[4.30554pt]
&\downarrow&&&&\downarrow\\[4.30554pt]
0\to&W\otimes{\rm Coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mu_{K_{C},K_{C}-N}}_{|}}}{{\rightarrow}}&H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)&=&H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)\\[4.30554pt]
&\downarrow&&&&\downarrow\\
&0&&&&0\end{array}

which proves the injectivity of the map
\alpha
.
Taking into account the cohomology sequence obtained from the middle column in (
3.6
), the fact that
W\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}
and the map
\alpha
previoulsy defined, one gets the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\\[4.30554pt]
&&&0\\
&&&\downarrow\\[4.30554pt]
&&&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\\[4.30554pt]
&&&\downarrow\\[4.30554pt]
&W\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\omega_{S}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}))\\[4.30554pt]
&||&&\downarrow^{\pi}\\[4.30554pt]
0\to&W\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\rightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(K_{C}-N))\end{array}

Take
x\in{\rm Ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}})
so that
\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(x)=0
; since

\pi\circ\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}=\alpha
, one must have

0=\pi(0)=\pi(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}(x))=\alpha(x)
, namely
x\in\operatorname{ker}(\alpha)=\{0\}
as
\alpha
was injective. Therefore
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is
regular
and
uniruled
also in these cases.

To conclude with the description of a
minimal presentation
of
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{{\rm reg},2}
general as stated, we observed that Teixidor’s presentation of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
general in such a component was not rigidly special (not rp), since the map
\pi
has a
1
-dimensional general fiber. Nevertheless, since this general fiber is isomorphic to the linear pencil of canonical sections
\Gamma
on the corresponding surface scroll
F_{u}
(recall the proof of Claim
3.4
) and since
\Gamma^{2}=4g-4-d>0
, this linear pencil certainly has some base points. If
p
is any of such base points, as in Proposition
1.7
-(2-i), one can split off the fiber
f_{p}
and gets a section
\Gamma^{\prime}
, of degree
2g-3
and of speciality
1
, since it corresponds to the quotient line bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0
. In such a case,
\mathcal{F}_{u}
turns out to be rigidly specially presented (rsp), as in Definition
1.6
, via the exact sequence
0\to N^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0
where by determinantal reasons in cases
(iii-1)
, where
N=K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}
, one has
N^{\prime}=K_{C}-D_{4g-4-d}+p
,
whereas in cases
(iii-2)
one has
N^{\prime}=N+p
.
∎

\boxed{3g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu}
. These cases occur when
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{2}
(otherwise, for
\frac{g}{2}<\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, we are in the previous case where
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
) and have been studied in
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(i)]
where we precisely proved:

Proposition 3.5
.

(cf.
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(i)]
) Let
g\geq 6
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{2}
and
3g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
contains a unique component which is regular, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
. Such a component is moreover uniruled and a general element
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
corresponds to a bundle fitting in an exact sequence

(3.7)

0\to\omega_{C}(-D)\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0,

where
p\in C
and
D\in C^{(4g-5-d)}
are general. Moreover,
omega_{C}(-p)
is of minimal degree among special quotient line bundles of
\mathcal{F}
.

Sketch of the proof of Proposition
3.5
.

The strategy used in
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(i) and § 3.2]
is the following: take a general effective divisor
D\in C^{4g-5-d}
, where
\mbox{deg}(D)=4g-5-d\leq g
, and
p\in C
general and consider
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-D)
as in (
3.1
), which has been proved to be a sub-vector space of dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=4g-6-d
. We showed that, for
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general, the corresponding
rank-two vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
as in (
3.7
) is stable (more precisely
s(\mathcal{F}_{u})\geq 1
– resp.
2
– if
d
is odd–resp. even), of speciality
i=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=2
; this has been done by using Proposition
1.16
and Theorem
3.1
.

To show that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is actually a component of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
, we proved there exists a natural projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\to S
, where
S\subseteq W^{0}_{4g-5-d}(C)\times C
an open dense subset: namely,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})
is the family of

\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-D))
’s as
(D,p)\in S
varies. Since, for any such
(D,p)\in S
,

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
is a linear sub-space of (constant) dimension
4g-7-d
, one has therefore an irreducible projective variety

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}:=\left\{(D,p,[u])\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\;|\;H^{0}(K_{C}-p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{u}=0}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(K_{C}-D)\right\}\subsetneq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})

which, from above, is
ruled
over
S
and of
dimension

\operatorname{dim}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}\right)=\operatorname{dim}(S)+4g-7-d=4g-d-4+4g-7-d=8g-2d-11=\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}.

From stability and speciality of the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
arising from the general triple
(D,p,u)\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, we deduced a natural (rational) modular map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U^{s}_{C}(d)\\
(D,p,[u])&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\end{array}

s.t.
\mathrm{im}(\pi)\subseteq B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
. In
[
11
, Prop. 3.6]
, we proved that the closure
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
of
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
in
U_{C}(d)
is a generically smooth component of
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
with (expected) dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
, i.e.
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is
regular
. Moreover, by the parametric description of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, we deduced that it is also
uniruled
, being finitely dominated by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, whose general fiber over
S
is a projective space, and the general point of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
arises as an extension in (
3.7
) with corank-one coboundary map.

Finally, in
[
11
, Prop. 3.8]
, we showed the uniqueness of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
among possible components of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, whose general point represents a bundle
\mathcal{F}
of speciality
2
and presented via an extension
(
1.22
) with
h^{1}(L)=1
. As observed in
[
11
, Rem. 3.9]
, the proof of the uniqueness of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
among such type of components also shows that, for
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
general, the line bundle
\omega_{C}(-p)
is minimal among special quotient line bundles for
\mathcal{F}
general in
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
and that it admits also a
presentation
via a canonical quotient, i.e. it fits in
0\to\omega_{C}(-D-p)\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0
, whose residual presentation coincides with that in the proof of
[
43
, Theorem]
. In other words, the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
coincides with the component
\mathcal{B}
stated in
[
43
, Theorem]
(which therein turns out to be also the only component when
C
is with general moduli).

The proof of the generic smoothness of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
was done in
[
44
, Thm. 1]
(in her “Serre dual notation”
W^{1}_{2,d}
) but only under the assumption on
C
with general moduli. We instead proved its generic smoothness for
C
a general
\nu
-gonal curve in
[
11
, Prop. 3.6]
.
∎

\boxed{2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-6}
These cases are brand new and they appear in the statement of Theorem
0.2
. To approach these new cases, we start with the following preliminary results.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
d=3g-5-j
be an integer, with
1\leq j\leq g-3
. Let
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+3}(C)
be general and let
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N)
be the degeneracy locus as in (
3.1
).

Then
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is irreducible, of dimension
g+2j-1
and unirational. Moreover, for
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general, the corresponding rank-two, degree-
d
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2
, with Segre invariant
s(\mathcal{F}_{u})\geq 1
(resp.,
2
) if
d
is odd (resp., even). In particular
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable.

Proof.

We first prove the assertions on
\mathcal{W}_{1}
for any
d\leq 3g-6
. Notice that
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+3=g-2-j\leq g-3
so, by generality,
N
is special and non-effective. Thus we have the following set-up:

(3.8)

\begin{CD}&(u)&:\;\;\;0\to&N&\to&\ \ \mathcal{F}_{u}\ \ &\to\ &\ \ K_{C}-p\ \ &\to 0\\
&\mbox{deg}&&g-2-j&&d&&2g-3&\\
&h^{0}&&0&&&&g-1&\\
&h^{1}&&j+1&&&&1&.\end{CD}

Since
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-p)=2g-3>g-2-j=\mbox{deg}(N)
, then

K_{C}-p\ncong N
so, using (
1.24
) and notation as in Theorem
3.1
,
one has
l:=h^{0}(K_{C}-p)=g-1
,
r:=h^{1}(N)=j+1
and
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N))=2g-2+j
;
therefore
l=g-1>r=j+1
and
m=2g-2+j\geq l+1=g
so we can apply Theorem
3.1
to get that
\mathcal{W}_{1}=\{u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N)\ |\ \operatorname{corank}(\partial_{u})\geq 1\}
is irreducible, of (expected) dimension

(3.9)

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=m-1(l-r+1)=g+2j-1.

Set
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N)\right)
and

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
; from the proof of
[
12
, Thm. 5.8]
,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
is
unirational
, being dominated by an incidence variety
\mathcal{J}
, which turns out to be a projective bundle over
\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(K_{C}-N))
, whose fibers are projective spaces of dimension
m-1-l>0
. This proves the assertions on
\mathcal{W}_{1}
.

Concerning the speciality of the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
corresponding to
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general, by Theorem
3.1
-(ii) and by (
3.8
), one has
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(K_{C}-p)+{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=2
, and we are done. Concerning the bounds on the Segre invariant (and so the stability, by (
1.23
)) of the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general, recall that
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
has dimension
g+2j-2
; posing
\delta:=2g-3
and considering that
d=3g-5-j
, for
1\leq j\leq 2g-3
, one has
2\delta-d=g-1+j\geq g\geq 4
. We are therefore in position to apply Theorem
1.16
and consider the natural morphism
C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}
, given by the complete linear system
|2K_{C}-p-N|
.
Set
X:=\varphi\left(C\right)
and let
\sigma
be an integer such that

\sigma\equiv 2\delta-d\ {\rm{(mod\;2)\ \ and}}

0\leq\sigma\leq 2\delta-d
; then we have

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(2\delta-d+\sigma-2)}(X)\right)=2\delta-d+\sigma-3=g-1+j+\sigma-3\leq g-2+2j=\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1})

if and only if

\sigma\leq 2+j
, where the equality holds if and only if
\sigma=2+j
.
Thus, since
j\geq 1
, we may take
\sigma=1
for
d
odd (resp.,
\sigma=2
for
d
even) which shows in particular the stability of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
.
∎

To construct the locus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
and to show that it actually is an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
as in Theorem
0.2
, notice that, as in
[
10
, § 3.2]
, one has a projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\to S
over
S\subseteq{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+3}(C)\times C
, an open dense subset of dimension
g+1=4g-4-j-d
; namely,

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})
is the family of
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N))
’s as
(N,p)\in S
varies. Since,
for any such
(N,p)\in S
,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
is irreducible,
unirational
and of (constant) dimension
g-2+2j=4g-7+j-d
,
one has an irreducible projective variety

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}:=\left\{(N,p,u)\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\;|\;H^{0}(K_{C}-p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{u}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(N),\ \ \operatorname{codim}(\partial_{u})=1\right\},

which is therefore
uniruled
over
S
and of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=(4g-4-j-d)+(4g-7+j-d)=8g-2d-11=\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}.

From stability, proved in Lemma
3.6
for
(N,p,u)\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
general, one has a natural (rational) modular map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U^{s}_{C}(d)\\
(N,p,[u])&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\end{array}

and
\mathrm{im}(\pi)\subseteq B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
.

Lemma 3.7
.

The map
\pi:\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}{\dashrightarrow}U^{s}_{C}(d)
is birational. In particular,
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
is uniruled and

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi))=\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=8g-2d-11=\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
.

Proof.

Once we show that
\pi
is birational onto its image, the uniruledness and the stated dimension of
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
will follow from the fact that it is birational
to
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, which is uniruled and of computed dimension.

To prove that
\pi
is birational onto its image, let
\mathcal{F}_{u}
be the bundle corresponding to the general point of
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
. If
F_{u}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
denotes the associated surface scroll and if
\Gamma
denotes the section of
F_{u}
corresponding to the quotient line bundle
K_{C}-p
of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, from (
1.12
) one has that
\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma/F_{u}}\simeq K_{C}-p-N
is general, since
N
is, and of degree
g-1+j
. Therefore
h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma/F_{u}})=h^{0}(K_{C}-p-N)=j>0
and
h^{1}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma/F_{u}})=0
, i.e. the Hilbert scheme
{\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}}
is unobstructed at
[\Gamma]
and of dimension
j
.

We will now prove that
[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
general is rigidly specially presented (rsp) via (
3.8
) (recall Definition

1.6
) more precisely that the section
\Gamma\subset F_{u}
is algebraically specially unique (asu).

Claim 3.8
.

\Gamma\subset F_{u}
is linearly isolated (li) and algebraically specially unique (asu), but not of minimal degree on
F_{u}
.

Proof of Claim
3.8
.

First of all we show that
\Gamma
is linearly isolated (li) in
F_{u}
which, by (
1.14
), is equivalent to proving that
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})=1
. From Serre duality, the facts
\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}(-{\rm det}(\mathcal{F}_{u}))

and
{\rm det}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=K_{C}-p+N
and (
3.8
), this is equivalent to proving that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(p))=1
. To do this, we tensor by
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p)
the exact sequence (
3.8
) to get

(3.10)

0\ \to\ N+p\ \to\ \mathcal{F}_{v}:=\mathcal{F}_{u}(p)\ \to\ K_{C}\ \to\ 0,

where
v\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
in (
3.8
) arises from a general pair
(N,p)\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+3}(C)\times C
and for
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N)
general, where
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=g-1+2j
by (
3.9
), and since
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N)
(being both isomorphic to
H^{1}(N+p-K_{C})
), it follows that
v
is a general point in an irreducible, unirational and
(g-1+2j)
-dimensional locus in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)
which, by small abuse of notation, we will always denote by
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)
.

Now we consider the relevant degeneracy sub-locus in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)
as in (
3.1
), namely:

(3.11)

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}}:=\{u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)\ |\ {\rm corank}(\partial_{u})\geq 1\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p).

We check the validity of conditions in Theorem
3.1
; note that from (
3.10
) we have
l:=h^{0}(K_{C})=g
and
r:=h^{1}(N+p)=j
, as we had
h^{1}(N)=j+1
and
N
general of
\mbox{deg}(N)\leq g-3
.
Since
1\leq j\leq g-3
, we get therefore
l>j
; moreover, from above
m=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p))=2g-2+j\geq l+1=g+1
. Hence, by Theorem
3.1
, a general element
w\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}}
gives corank(
\partial_{w})=1
and

\operatorname{dim}(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}})=m-(g-j+1)=g-3+2j.

Since
v
in (
3.10
) was general in
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)
such that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=g-1+2j>g-3+2j=\operatorname{dim}(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}})
, then a general
v\in\mathcal{W}_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+p)
gives rise to a surjective coboundary map
\partial_{v}
and we get

(3.12)

h^{1}(\mathcal{F}(p))=h^{1}(K_{C})=1,

so
\Gamma
is linearly isolated (li) in
F_{u}
as desired.

Now we will show that, for
[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
general, the section
\Gamma
is even algebraically specially unique (asu) on the scroll
F_{u}
. Assume by contradiction that
\Gamma
is (not asu) on
F_{u}
then, by Proposition
1.7
-
(1)
, we have
the following diagram for some point
p\neq q\in C
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&&&0&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&N^{\prime}&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\rightarrow&K_{C}-p&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&K_{C}-q&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&0&&&&\end{array}

where
N^{\prime}:=N+q-p
since
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=K_{C}+N-p\simeq K_{C}-q+N^{\prime}
. Tensoring with the line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p)
, we get

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&&&0&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&N+q&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N+p&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{v}:=\mathcal{F}(p)&\rightarrow&K_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&K_{C}+p-q&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&0&&&&\end{array}

As above, the extension
v\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}+p-q,\;N+q)
is a general point in the irreducible sub-locus
\mathcal{W}_{1}
of dimension
g-1+2j
, with
N
and
p
general. On the other hand, consider the relevant degeneracy
sub-locus in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}+p-q,N+q)
:

\widetilde{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}}}:=\{u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}+p-q,N+q)\ |\ {\rm corank}(\partial_{u})\geq 1\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}+p-q,N+q).

Once again we check the validity of conditions as in Theorem
3.1
;
in this case we have
l:=h^{0}(K_{C}+p-q)=g-1
, since
p\neq q
because
\Gamma
is linearly isolated (cf. Prop.
1.7
-(1)), and
r:=h^{1}(N+q)=j
, as

N
was general of its degree
\mbox{deg}(N)\leq g-3
with speciality
j+1
. Thus,
from
1\leq j\leq g-3
, we get
l=g-1>r=j
. Now set

m:=\operatorname{dim}({\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}+q-p,N+q))=h^{0}(2K_{C}+p-N-2q)
; since

\mbox{deg}(2K_{C}+p-N-2q)=3g-3+j>2g-2
, one has
m=2g-2+j
, certainly greater than
l+1=g
. Hence, from Theorem
3.1
, it follows that a general element
w\in\widetilde{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}}}
gives
corank(
\partial_{w})=1
. Notice that
\operatorname{dim}(\widetilde{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}}})=m-(g-i)=g-2+2j
.
Since
v
was general in
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}+p-q,N+q)
of dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=g-1+2j
, which is bigger
than
\operatorname{dim}(\widetilde{\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{1}}})
, we deduce that a general
v\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
gives rise to a surjective coboundary map
\partial_{v}
. This in turn gives
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(p))=h^{1}(K_{C}+p-q)=0
, which contradicts
(
3.12
). Therefore,
\Gamma
is (asu) on
F_{u}
.

Finally, the fact that
\Gamma
is not of minimal degree among sections of
F_{u}
follows from
h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma/F_{u}})=\operatorname{dim}_{[\Gamma]}({\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}})=j>0
, from
F_{u}
being not decomposable and from Proposition
1.7
-(2).
∎

We now show how Claim
3.8
implies that the map
\pi
is birational; we argue as in the proof of Claim
3.4
. Let
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
be general, so
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
(N,p,[u])\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
general. By definition of the map
\pi
we have that
\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])=\left\{(N^{\prime},p^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}\,|\;\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}\right\}
.

Assume by contradiction
(N^{\prime},p^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])\setminus\{(N,p,[u])\}
; thus
N^{\prime}\simeq N+p^{\prime}-p
. If
p=p^{\prime}
, then
N\simeq N^{\prime}
; thus
[u]\neq[u^{\prime}]
belong to the same
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,N))
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable (so simple), this implies the existence of an isomorphism
\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{F}_{u}
(which is not an endomorphism) giving rise to the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}&\to&K_{C}-p&\to 0\\
&&&\downarrow^{\varphi}&&&\\
0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\to&K_{C}-p&\to 0.\end{array}

The maps
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
determine, respectively, two non-zero global sections
s_{1}\neq s_{2}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})
.
They must be linearly dependent, otherwise one would have
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})>1
, contradicting Claim
3.8
as
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})-1=\operatorname{dim}(|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|)
by (
1.14
). On the other hand, if they were linearly dependent, then
[
12
, Lemma 4.5]
would give
[u]=[u^{\prime}]
, a contradiction. If on the contrary
p\neq p^{\prime}
, the sections
\Gamma
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
, respectively corresponding to the quotient line bundles
\omega_{C}(-p)
and
\omega_{C}(-p^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{F}_{u}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}
, would be such that
\Gamma\sim_{\rm alg}\Gamma^{\prime}
, contradicting Claim
3.8

This completes the proof of Lemma
3.7
.
∎

Let
\mathcal{B}
be any component of
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
containing
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
. From (
3.7
), one must have therefore that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\geq\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi))=\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
. The next result will show that there exists only one of such a component, which turns out to be also
regular
and
uniruled
.

Proposition 3.9
.

The dimension of the tangent space to
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
at a general point

[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
equals the expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
.

Hence there exists a unique irreducible component of
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, which contains
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
as an open dense subset. Moreover,
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is generically smooth, of the expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
, i.e. it is
regular. In particular, the general point of

\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
corresponds to a stable bundle, arising as in Lemma
3.6
, and
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
is uniruled.

Proof.

The proof of the first part of the statement is similar to that in
[
10
, Prop. 3.9]
and uses computations of the kernel of the Petri map as in (
1.21
) of the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
via suitable degenerations. The second part directly follows from Lemma
3.7
and from the computation of the Petri map.
∎

Proposition
3.9
shows that a general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
certainly has a
special presentation
as in (
3.8
), with

N
and
p
general. The next results will show in particular that
\omega_{C}(-p)
is of
minimal degree
among special quotient line bundles of
\mathcal{F}
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a rank-
2
vector bundle of degree
d
, speciality
i=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=2
and of second type in the sense of Definition
1.15
. Let
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
, be an effective divisor of degree
s>0

and
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+s}(C)
, such that

(i)
1\leq h^{1}(N)\leq g-s
,

(ii)
h^{0}(2K_{C}-(N+D_{s}))\geq g-s+2
,

(iii)
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset Ext^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}),\;N)
general.

Then
\mathcal{F}
fits also in the exact sequence:
0\to N(-D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0
.

Proof.

Notice that from the assumptions on
\mathcal{F}
, being of speciality
2
and of
second type
, one must have
h^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))=1
, so we have
\ell:=h^{0}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))=g-s
and
h^{1}(N)\geq 1
, as in (i). Notice further that assumption (i) implies
\ell=h^{0}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))\geq h^{1}(N)
whereas assumption (ii) implies that
m:=\operatorname{dim}({\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N))\geq\ell+2
. Therefore, from Theorem
3.1
, we have that
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset{\rm Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}),\;N)
as in (
3.1
) is non-empty, irreducible, of the expected codimension
c(1)=\ell-r+1
and
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general is such that
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=1
. So the associated bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
has degree
d
, speciality
2
and it is of
second type
, which explains assumption (iii).

By assumption (iii),
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits in a sequence of the form (
1.25
), namely
0\to N\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\omega_{C}(-D_{s})\to 0
. Composing the line-bundle injection
N(-D_{s})\hookrightarrow N
with the above exact sequence shows that
N(-D_{s})
is also a sub-line bundle of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. To prove the statement, we need to show that
\frac{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{N(-D_{s})}\simeq\omega_{C}
.

Since by (
1.25
) one has
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})\simeq\omega_{C}\otimes N\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(-D_{s})
, by determinantal reasons it is enough to show that the quotient sheaf
\frac{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{N(-D_{s})}
is torsion-free on
C
, namely that the sub-line bundle
N(-D_{s})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{u}
is
saturated
in
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. Notice that the injection
N(-D_{s})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{u}
gives rise to a global section of the rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})\simeq\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
, where the isomorphism follows from the fact that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is of rank
2
with determinant
N\otimes\omega_{C}(-D_{s})
. Thus, to prove that the injection
N(-D_{s})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{u}
is saturated it is enough to show that the general global section of
\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
is nowhere vanishing, namely that
the intersections of the zero-loci of global sections of

\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
is empty, i.e.
h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(-q))=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(q))\leq 1,\;\;\mbox{for every point}\;q\in C
.

Tensoring the exact sequence (
1.25
) by
N^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})
gives

0\ \to\ \mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}\ \omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}=N^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\ \to\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}\ \ \to\ \ 0

and the injection
\sigma
gives rise to a global section
\tilde{\sigma}\in H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})
which is not zero at any
q\notin D_{s}
.

We claim that, for any
q\in D_{s}
, there also exists a global section in
H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})
which is not zero at
q
. Thus, assume
q\in D_{s}
and tensor (
1.25
) by
\mathcal{O}_{C}(q)
to get

(3.13)

0\to N(q)\to\mathcal{F}_{v}:=\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(q)\to K_{C}-(D_{s}-q)\to 0.

Since
q\in D_{s}
, then
\widetilde{\ell}:=h^{0}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q))=\ell+1=g-s+1
, whereas
\widetilde{r}:=h^{1}(N(q))
is either
h^{1}(N)
or
h^{1}(N)-1
and, in any case, assumption (i) implies
\widetilde{\ell}\geq\widetilde{r}
. Notice that
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q)),N(q))\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N)
since they both are isomorphic to
H^{1}(N+D_{s}-K_{C})
, whose dimension is
m:=h^{0}(2K_{C}-(N+D_{s}))
which, from assumption (ii), is such that

m\geq\widetilde{\ell}+1=h^{0}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q))+1=g-s+2
. Therefore, from Theorem
3.1
, the relevant degeneracy locus

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{1}\>=\left\{w\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(D-q),N(q))\;|\;{\rm corank}(\partial_{w})\geq 1\right\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(D-q),N(q))\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N)

is not empty,
irreducible, of the expected codimension
\widetilde{c(1)}:=\widetilde{\ell}-\widetilde{r}+1
and
w\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
general is such that
{\rm corank}(\partial_{w})=1
. Since both
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{1},\mathcal{W}_{1}
are contained in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N)
and
\widetilde{c(1)}>c(1)
, it follows that
\operatorname{dim}(\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{1})<\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
. From the generality assumption of
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
, the point
v
defined by
\mathcal{F}_{v}=\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(q)
as in (
3.13
) is such that
v\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
, which implies therefore that the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{v}
is surjective. This gives therefore
1=h^{1}(K_{C}-(D-q))=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(q))
, as desired.
∎

Proposition 3.11
.

A general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
as above cannot have either
an effective quotient line bundle
L
with
h^{1}(L)=2
or an effective quotient line bundle
L
with
h^{1}(L)=1
and of degree
\delta<2g-3
.

Proof.

We start by proving that speciality-
2
line bundle quotients of
\mathcal{F}
are avoided. Assume by contradiction that a general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
has an effective quotient line bundle
L
with
h^{1}(L)=2
; set
L:=K_{C}-E
, where
h^{0}(E)=2
. Notice further that, from Lemma
3.6
,
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
general satisfies all the assumptions as in Lemma
3.10
, with
D_{s}=p\in C
general. Then, one has the following commutative diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&&&0&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&N-p&=&N-p&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow\tilde{\alpha}&&\downarrow{\beta}&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N_{L}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\rightarrow}}&L=K_{C}-E&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&K_{C}&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&0&&&&\end{array}

where the vertical exact sequence comes from Lemma
3.10
, whereas
N_{L}
denotes the
kernel of the surjection
\mathcal{F}\to\!\!\to K_{C}-E
and where
\beta
is the composition map
\beta:N-p\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-E
. For determinantal reason, one has
N_{L}\simeq N+E-p
. We claim that
\beta
is not the zero-map: otherwise, there would exist an injective line-bundle map
N-p\hookrightarrow N_{L}\simeq N+E-p
, so the composition
N-p\to N_{L}\to\mathcal{F}
would not be a bundle homomorphism, which gives a contradiction. Hence
\beta\neq 0
as an element in
{\rm Hom}(N-p,K_{C}-E)
and, considering the map
\psi
as:

{\small{\rm Hom}(N-p,\mathcal{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi^{\prime}{}}}{{\to}}{\rm Hom}(N-p,K_{C}-E)\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}-N-E+p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\to}}{\rm Ext}^{1}(N-p,N_{L})\simeq H^{1}(N_{L}-N+p),}

one gets
\psi(\beta)=0
, since
\beta=\psi^{\prime}{}(\tilde{\alpha})
. On the other hand, since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(L)=2
, the coboundary map
H^{0}(K_{C}-E)\to H^{1}(N_{L})
is surjective. Hence the dual homomorphism

H^{1}(N_{L})^{\vee}\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}-N_{L})\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}-N-E+p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\to}}H^{0}(K_{C}-E)^{\vee}\simeq H^{1}(E)\simeq H^{1}(N_{L}-N+p)

is
injective. Hence
\psi(\beta)\neq 0
, which gives a contradiction.

Now assume by contradiction that a general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
has an effective quotient line bundle
L

with
h^{1}(L)=1
and
\mbox{deg}(L)=\delta<2g-3
. Thus,
K_{C}-L=D_{2g-2-\delta}
is an effective divisor of degree
2g-2-\delta\geq 2
and
L=K_{C}-D_{2g-2-\delta}
. Let
N^{\prime}
denote the kernel line bundle of the surjection
\mathcal{F}\to\!\!\to K_{C}-D_{2g-2-\delta}
. Since
\mathcal{F}
fits also in (
3.8
), then
N^{\prime}\simeq N+D-p
which is general of
\mbox{deg}(N^{\prime})=d-\delta
, as
N
is general of
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+3
. If
d-\delta\geq g-1
, then
h^{1}(N^{\prime})=0
and this is a contradiction since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=2
and
h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{2g-2-\delta})=1
.

If otherwise
d-\delta\leq g-2
, we take into account parameters for this construction. We first take into account
g
parameters for
N^{\prime}
and
2g-2-\delta
parameters for
K_{C}-D_{2g-2-\delta}
. Taking notation as in Theorem
3.1
, we now consider
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{2g-2-\delta},N^{\prime}))=h^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}-D_{2g-2-\delta})
; notice that
\mbox{deg}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}-D_{2g-2-\delta})=2g-2+2\delta-d
, where
2\delta-d\geq s(\mathcal{F})\geq 1
, by stability; thus it is non-special and
m=g-1+2\delta-2
. Now
l:=h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{2g-2-\delta})=\delta-g+2
, as it is of speciality
1
, and
r:=h^{1}(N^{\prime})=g-1+\delta-d
, by generality of
N^{\prime}
of degree
d-\delta\leq g-2
. From the bounds on
d
and the fact that
d-\delta\leq g-2
, it follows that
l\geq r
and
m\geq l+1
. Therefore, by Theorem
3.1
-(ii),

\mathcal{W}_{1}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{2g-2-\delta},N^{\prime})
is irreducible, of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=m-(l-r+1)=3g-5+2\delta-d
. Regardless stability, in this way we get as usual an irreducible scheme

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=g+(2g-2-\delta)+(3g-6+2\delta-2d)=6g-8+\delta-2d

and the latter is strictly less than
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
, since

\delta<2g-3
, and we are done also in this case.
∎

As a direct consequence of Proposition
3.11
, one has:

Corollary 3.12
.

For
p\in C
general and
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
general as above, the line bundle
\omega_{C}(-p)
is of minimal degree among special and effective quotient line bundles of
\mathcal{F}
, even if it is not of minimal degree among all possible quotients, and
\mathcal{F}
is rigidly specially presented (rsp) via the extension (
3.8
).

On the contrary,
\mathcal{F}
is not rigidly specially presented (not rsp) via canonical quotient line bundle, i.e. via extensions as in Lemma
3.10
.

Proof.

The fact that
\omega_{C}(-p)
is of minimal degree among special and effective quotient line bundles of
\mathcal{F}
follows from (
3.8
) and from Theorem
3.11
. The fact that it is not of minimal degree among all possible quotients comes from Claim
3.8
. The fact that
\mathcal{F}
general is rigidly specially presented via (
3.8
) is a consequence
of Lemma
3.7
and of the density of
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
in
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
. The fact that
\mathcal{F}
general admits also canonical quotients comes from Lemma
3.10
, as the bundle
\mathcal{F}
satisfies all the assumptions therein. Therefore, one can construct the relevant
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}

as in Lemma
3.6
but using the canonical quotient; the associated dominant, rational map
\pi
onto
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
turns out to have general fiber
isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
being identified, as e.g. in Claim
3.4
,
to the linear system
|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|
on the scroll
F_{u}
.
∎

3.2.
Superabundant components in speciality 2

It remains to study possible components
\mathcal{B}
of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
for which
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general is of
first type
in the sense of Definition
1.15
, namely it fits into an exact sequence as in (
1.22
), with
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
s.t.
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(L)=2
.

As occurred for the
regular
component
B_{\rm reg,2}
studied in §
3.1
, some values of
d
have been already partially considered in
[
10
,
11
]
. For reader’s convenience and in order to present a complete picture of the whole range of interest, i.e.
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4
as in (
1.17
), we will go brief when reminding the main results in
[
10
,
11
]
.

\boxed{4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-4}
From §
3.1
, in this range there is no component
\mathcal{B}\subset B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
for which
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general can be s.t.
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(L)=2
with
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
effective, as it follows either from Proposition
3.2
(from which
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\emptyset
when
4g-6\leq d\leq 4g-4
) or from
Proposition
3.3
(from which
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
is irreducible, for any
3\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, consisting only of the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
already studied in §
3.1
).

\boxed{3g-3\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu}
These cases have already been studied in
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(ii)]
, and they occur when the gonality
\nu
is such that
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-2}{2}
, in particular
g\geq 8
(indeed, for
\frac{g-2}{2}<\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, one has
3g-3>4g-5-2\nu
so we are in the previous case, where
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
is either empty or it is irreducible, consisting only of the regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
). In
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(ii)]
we precisely proved the following:

Proposition 3.13
.

(cf.
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(ii)]
) Let
g\geq 8
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-2}{2}
and
3g-3\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
contains an extra irreducible component, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
, which is generically smooth, of dimension
6g-6-d-2\nu>\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
, i.e.
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
is
superabundant. Moreover, it is ruled and a general element
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
corresponds to a rank two vector bundle of degree
d
and speciality
2
, fitting in the exact sequence

(3.14)

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}\to 0,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and where
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
is general of its degree. In particular,
s(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-d-2\nu
and
\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}
is of minimal degree among quotient line bundles of
\mathcal{F}
.

Moreover,
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
is the only component of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
for which the general bundle
\mathcal{F}
is such that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(L)=2
, for effective and special quotient line bundles
L
as in (
1.22
).

Remark 3.14
.

To be more precise, in
[
11
]
we studied the cases
3g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
; nevertheless, as mentioned in the Introduction, in
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(ii)]
it was wrongly stated that for
d=3g-4,3g-5
the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
therein was even
non-reduced
. This is due to tricky computations of kernels of suitable Petri/multiplication maps in

[
11
, Thm. 3.12-(ii)]
. We will fix here this mistake (cf. Proposition
3.15
and Remark
3.19
-(a) below) by reproving the cases
d=3g-4,3g-5
in the brand new part of the paper, whereas we briefly remind here the correct part of
[
11
, Theorem 1.5-(ii)]
, i.e. what was proved for
3g-3\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
.

Sketch of the proof of Proposition
3.13
.

The strategy used in
[
11
, § 3.3]
to study the cases
3g-3\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
was as follows: first, in
[
11
, Lemma 3.10]
we proved that if
\mathcal{F}
is a rank-two vector bundle arising as a general extension in
{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
, where
N
any line bundle in
{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
, then
\mathcal{F}
is stable with
s(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-2\nu-d
, i.e.
K_{C}-A
is a minimal quotient line bundle of
\mathcal{F}
. Moreover, if
N
is non-special, one obviously has
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(K_{C}-A)=2
.

Next, to construct the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
as in
[
11
, Thm. 1.5-(ii)]
we proved the existence of a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}
on a suitable open, dense subset
S\subseteq\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
, whose rank is

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N))=5g-5-2\nu-d
as in (
1.24
) (cf.
[
2
, pp. 166-167]
).
Taking the associated projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\to S
(consisting of the family of projective spaces
\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)\right)
’s varying as
N
varies in
S
) we got

\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}))=g+(5g-5-2\nu-d)-1=6g-6-2\nu-d.

We proved stability for the general bundle arising from the general pair

(N,u)\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
and, using the fact that such a bundle has speciality
2
, we deduce the existence of a natural (rational) modular map

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{d,\nu}}}{{\dashrightarrow}}

\displaystyle U_{C}(d)

\displaystyle(N,[u])\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{u};

for which
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})\subseteq B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
. We then proved that

\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image (cf.
[
11
, Claim 3.11]
); this in particular showed that the closure
of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
in
U_{C}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
, is
ruled
, being birational to
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
, and such that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2})=6g-6-2\nu-d
.

The content of
[
11
, Thm. 3.12-(i)]
was that, for
3g-3\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, one has
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2})>\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
and also that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
is generically smooth; the latter has been proved via a careful study of suitable Petri/multiplication maps. At last, we also proved that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
is the only component of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
for which the general bundle
\mathcal{F}
is such that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(L)=2
, for quotient line bundles
L
as in (
1.22
) (cf.
[
11
, Lemma 3.14]
).
∎

\boxed{2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4}
This range is brand new; in these cases
d
can be also written as
d=3g-4-j
, with
0\leq j\leq g-2
. From the cases previously studied, notice that
3g-4-j<4g-5-2\nu
iff
\nu<\frac{g-1+j}{2}
. Thus, according to the choice of
d
, equivalently of the integer
j
, if
\frac{g-1+j}{2}\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, these cases have been already discussed in the previous case. We will prove here the following result.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
g\geq 8
and
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-2}{2}
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then:

(i)

for any
2g-5+2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4
,
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
contains an extra-component, different from
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
as in Proposition
3.9
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
, which is ruled, generically smooth and superabundant, being of dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2})=6g-6-d-2\nu>\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
. Furthermore,
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
general is rigidly presented (rp) as an extension (
3.14
), where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
is general;

(ii)

for
d=2g-5+2\nu
,
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
contains an extra-component, different from
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
as in Proposition
3.9
, denoted as above by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
, which is ruled and regular. Furthermore, as above,
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
general is rigidly presented (rp) as an extension (
3.14
), where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle such on
C
that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
is general;

(iii)

for
2g-2\leq d\leq 2g-6+2\nu
,
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
is irreducible, consisting only of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
as in Proposition
3.9
.

To prove Proposition
3.15
, we start with the following more general result.

Lemma 3.16
.

Let
g\geq 8
,
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
and
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-2}{2}
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve. Let
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
be a degree-
d
, rank-two vector bundle, arising as a general extension
u\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
, where
A\in\hbox{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
is general. Then
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(K_{C}-A)=2
, i.e.
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is of first type in the sense of Definition
1.15
.

Proof.

Notice that, from the bounds on
d
and
\nu
, one has
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2+\nu<2g-2-\nu=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A)
; thus

N\ncong K_{C}-A
. Setting
d=3g-4-j
, for
0\leq j\leq g-2
, and
\delta:=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A)=2g-2-\nu
,
by (
1.24
) one gets
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N))=2g-2\nu+1+j
, which certainly is positive by the bounds on
\nu
.
Let
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
be general and let
\mathcal{F}_{u}
be the corresponding rank-two vector bundle. Then
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits into the exact sequence (
3.14
).

As in Theorem
3.1
, set
l:=h^{0}(K_{C}-A)=g+1-\nu
,
m=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N))=2g-2\nu+1+j
and
r:=h^{1}(N)
. Since
N
is general of degree
\mbox{deg}(N)=g-2+\nu-j
, if
\nu-j\leq 0
one has

h^{1}(N)=-\chi(N)=1+j-\nu
whereas, for
\nu-j\geq 1
,
h^{1}(N)=0
; therefore
r={\rm max}\{0,\;1+j-\nu\}
. Since
0\leq j\leq g-2
, then
l\geq r
and
m\geq l+1
hold true. Hence we can apply Theorem
3.1
to deduce that, for general
u\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
, the corresponding coboundary map is surjective, therefore one has
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2
.

To prove stability of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, as
\delta=2g-2-\nu
and
\nu\leq\frac{g-2}{2}
, one has that

2\delta-d\geq 2(2g-2-\nu)-(3g-4)=g-2\nu\geq 2
so we can apply Theorem
1.16
. Thus, set
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N))
and consider the natural morphism

C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}X\subset\mathbb{P}
given by the complete linear system
|2K_{C}-A-N|
, where
X:=\varphi\left(C\right)
; let
\sigma
be any integer such that
\sigma\equiv 2\delta-d\ {\rm{(mod\;2)\ \ and}}

2\leq\sigma\leq\min\{2\delta-d,g\}
one has

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(2\delta-d+\sigma-2)}(X)\right)=2\delta-d+\sigma-3<2\delta-d+g-2=\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N))\right),

as

\sigma\leq g
. Hence
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable.
∎

The parametric construction of the locus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
, when
N
varies in a suitable open dense subset
S\subseteq\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
, is similar to that in
[
10
, § 3.1]
; indeed, one can construct a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}
over
S
, of rank
m=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N))=5g-5-2\nu-d
as in (
1.24
) and consider the associated projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\to S
, which parametrizes the family of

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)\right)
’s as
N
varies in
S
. One has

\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}))=g+(5g-5-2\nu-d)-1=6g-6-2\nu-d.

From stability and speciality
2
as from Lemma
3.16
, we have a natural (rational) modular map

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{d,\nu}}}{{\dashrightarrow}}

\displaystyle U_{C}(d)

\displaystyle(N,[u])\to

\displaystyle[\mathcal{F}_{u}],

such that
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})\subseteq B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
. Once we show that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image,
we will get that the closure of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
in
U_{C}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
, is
ruled
(being birational to
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
) of dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2})=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}))=6g-6-2\nu-d
. In the same spirit of the proof of Claim
3.8
, we first prove some
results concerning families of sections on associated surface scrolls.

Lemma 3.17
.

With assumptions as in Lemma
3.15
, let
N\in\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
be general and
\mathcal{F}_{u}
correspond to
u\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
general. Let
F_{u}
denote the surface scroll associated to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and let
\Gamma\subset F_{u}
be the section of
F_{u}
corresponding to the
quotient line bundle
K_{C}-A
of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
.

(i) If
3g-3-2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4
, then
\Gamma
is algebraically isolated (ai). In particular,
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is rigidly presented (rp) via the quotient line bundle
K_{C}-A
.

(ii) If
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4-2\nu
, then one has
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})=1
. In particular, the section
\Gamma\subset F_{u}
is linearly isolated on the scroll
F_{u}
.

Proof.

(i) Note that, if
3g-3-2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4
, from (
1.12
) and from the generality of
N
it follows that
h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma/F})\simeq h^{0}(K_{C}-A-N)=0
, hence it also follows that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is rigidly presented via the quotient line bundle
K_{C}-A
(recall Definition
1.6
).

(ii) The second assertion follows directly from the first and from (
1.14
). Therefore, we focus on proving the first assertion. Consider the exact sequence obtained from (
3.14
) tensored by
A
, namely:

\begin{CD}&&0\to&N+A&\to&\ \ \mathcal{F}\otimes A\ \ &\to\ &\ \ K_{C}\ \ &\to 0\\
&\mbox{deg}&&d+2\nu-2g+2&&d+2\nu&&2g-2&\\
\end{CD}

Since the bundle
\mathcal{F}
corresponds to a general element in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
, for
N
general, and since

\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+A)\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
, being both isomorphic to
H^{1}(N+A-K_{C})
, then also

\mathcal{F}\otimes A
corresponds to a general element of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+A)
, with
N+A
also general of its degree since
N
is. As
\mbox{deg}(N+A)=d+2\nu+2-2g
and from the bounds on
d
, one has that
2\nu\leq\mbox{deg}(N+A)\leq g-2
. Therefore, since
N+A
is general, it is not effective and of speciality
r:=h^{1}(N+A)=3g-3-d-2\nu\geq 1
.

We want to apply Theorem
3.1
to show that the coboundary map
\partial:H^{0}(K_{C})\to H^{1}(N+A)
associated
to such a general extension is surjective. To do this, set
l:=h^{0}(K_{C})=g
, hence we get
l\geq r
since, from the bounds on
d
, we have
h^{1}(N+A)\leq g-1-2\nu<g
. Now set
m:=\operatorname{dim}({\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+A))=h^{0}(2K_{C}-N-A)
; since

\mbox{deg}(2K_{C}-A-N)\geq 3g-2>2g-2
, then
2K_{C}-A-N
is non-special so we have
m=5g-5-d-2\nu
; thus
m=5g-5-d-2\nu\geq l+1=g+1
certainly holds from the bounds on
d
. From Theorem
3.1
we get that, for general
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+A)
the coboundary map

\partial:H^{0}(K_{C})\to H^{1}(N+A)
is surjective, which yields

h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A)=h^{1}(C,K_{C})=1
. By Serre duality, this is
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(K_{C}-A))=1
; since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is of rank two, with determinant
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=N-A+K_{C}
one has
\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(K_{C}-A)\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}(-N)
and we are done by (
1.14
).
∎

Corollary 3.18
.

With assumptions as in Proposition
3.15
, the map
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image, whose closure
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
in
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
is ruled, of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2})=6g-6-2\nu-d
. The general point
[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
corresponds to a rank-two, degree
d
stable vector bundle, of speciality
2
which is rigidly presented (rp) as an extension (
3.14
).

Proof.

If one shows that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational, it then follows that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
is ruled and of the stated dimension, since it is birational to
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
, and moreover that
[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
general is rigidly presented (rp) via extension (
3.14
), since
the general fiber of
\pi_{d,\nu}
is a singleton.

To prove that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image, one observes that the quotient line bundle
K_{C}-A
is fixed in the triples
(N,K_{C}-A,\mathcal{F}_{u})
parametrized by
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
so, similarly as in the proof of Claim
3.8
, from Proposition

1.7
-
(1)
it is enough to show that for general
(N,K_{C}-A,\mathcal{F}_{u})\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
the section
\Gamma\subset F_{u}
corresponding to the
quotient line bundle
K_{C}-A
is linearly isolated (li) on
F_{u}
. This follows from
Lemma
3.17
.
∎

We finally have all the necessary ingredients to prove Proposition
3.15
.

Proof of Proposition
3.15
.

Point
(iii)
is a consequence of the following easy observation: from Lemma
3.16
,
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
contains stable bundles thus, from Remark
1.10
, any component of
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
containing
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
must have at least the expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
. Then one concludes by observing that, for
2g-2\leq d\leq 2g-6+2\nu
, one has
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2})=6g-6-d-2\nu<\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}
, thus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
cannot fill-up an irreducible component of
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
. Nonetheless, from the previous section we know the existence of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
; furthermore, from the results contained in
§
3.3
below, we will also deduce that no further component can exist. Therefore, in this case
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
. The reader is referred to §
3.3
for
the precise conclusion of the proof.

Concerning
(i)
and
(ii)
, they will be proved together. The fact that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
is ruled and of the stated dimension has been showed in Corollary
3.18
