. Take now

[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
general, i.e.
\mathcal{F}_{u}
arising as a
general extension in (
3.14
) with
N\in\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
general, and, from Lemma
3.16
, we have
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(K_{C}-A)=2
. Consider the following exact diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N+A-K_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(A-K_{C})&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{(}K_{C}-A)\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee}&\rightarrow&(K_{C}-A)\otimes N^{\vee}&0&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&\end{array}

which arises from (
3.14
) and from its dual sequence. If we tensor the column in the middle by
\omega_{C}
and pass to cohomology, we get the injection
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A)\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})
.

Since
H^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\simeq H^{1}(K_{C}-A)
, from (
3.14
) and from Lemma
3.16
, by Serre duality one has
H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(A)
, hence the kernel of the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}
as in (
1.21
) coincides
with the kernel of the multiplication map

H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\to}}H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A)\subseteq H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}).

By the
base-point-free pencil trick, we have
\operatorname{ker}(\alpha)\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\vee})
. Now consider the exact sequence

0\to N-A\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\vee}\to K_{C}-2A\to 0,

obtained from (
3.14
) tensored by
A^{\vee}
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{u}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)
is
general, then also
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\vee}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,N-A)
is general, as the two extension spaces are isomorphic.

In notation of Theorem
3.1
, set
l:=h^{0}(K_{C}-2A)
. Since
g\geq 2\nu-2
, from Proposition
1.1
, one has
3=h^{0}(2A)=h^{1}(K_{C}-2A)
so, by Riemann-Roch theorem, one has
l=g-2\nu+2
. Now set
r:=h^{1}(N-A)
; note that
\mbox{deg}(N-A)=d-2g+2
which, from the bounds

2g-5+2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4
, gives
2\nu-3\leq\mbox{deg}(N-A)\leq g-2
. Thus, by the generality of
N
,

N-A
is non-effective and of speciality
H^{1}(N-A)=3g-d-3>0
.
By the assumption of
d\geq 2g-5+2\nu
, we have in particular
l=g-2\nu+2\geq r=3g-d-3
. Finally, set
m:=\operatorname{dim}({\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,N-A))=h^{0}(2K_{C}-A_{N})
; we want to prove that
m\geq l+1
.
Since
\mbox{deg}(2K_{C}-A_{N})>2g-2
, we have
m=5g-5-d-2\nu
and it gives

m=5g-5-d-2\nu>l+1=g-2\nu+3
since
d\leq 3g-4
. Thus, from Theorem
3.1
, the associated coboundary map
\partial_{u}:H^{0}(K_{C}-2A)\to H^{1}(N-A)
is surjective, so

H^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\vee})\simeq H^{1}(K_{C}\otimes 2A^{\vee})
which gives
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\vee})=d-2\nu-2g+5
. Thus

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}T_{[\mathcal{F}_{u}]}(B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(2,d))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4g-3-2(d-2g+4)+(g+2-2\nu)+(d-3g+3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6g-6-2\nu-d=\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}).

To complete the proof, it suffices to observe that since
d\geq 2g-5+2\nu
, then
\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d\leq 6g-6-2\nu-d
, where equality holds if and only if
d=2g-5+2\nu
.
∎

Remark 3.19
.

(a) Notice that the proof of Proposition
3.15
-(i) above for
d=3g-4,\;3g-5
fixes
[
11
, Theorem 3.12-(ii)]
, where it was wrongly stated that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
was non-reduced.

(b) When
d=2g-5+2\nu
, the components
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, even if of the same dimension (because both are
regular
), are distinct since their general points have different
presentations
, respectively (
3.8
) and (
3.14
).

3.3.
No other component in speciality 2

In this section, we will show the following:

Proposition 3.20
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
and let
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4
be an integer. Then
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
is either empty or it contains no other components than
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
constructed in Sections
3.1
and
3.2

Proof.

For
3g-3\leq d\leq 4g-4
, Proposition
3.20
follows from what discussed in the previous sections. Namely, for
4g-6\leq d\leq 4g-4
, we have
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)=\emptyset
, as proved in Proposition
3.2
. For
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7
,
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
, as in Proposition
3.3
, which in turn is a consequence of Theorem
0.1
. For
3g-3\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, the statement of Proposition
3.20
has been proved in
[
11
, Prop. 3.8, Lemma 3.14]
.

For
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
, what stated will be a direct consequence of the next Propositions
3.21
and
3.23
.
∎

Let us assume therefore
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
and that
\mathcal{B}\subset B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
is any irreducible component; from Remark
1.10
, then
\operatorname{dim}(B)\geq\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
. Moreover, Lemma
2.1
gives that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=2
for
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general. Finally, from Lemma
1.12
and from stability, such a bundle
\mathcal{F}
must fit in an exact sequence of the form (
1.22
), where
L
is a line bundle, of a suitable degree
\delta>\frac{d}{2}
, which is effective and special, in particular
1\leq h^{1}(L)\leq 2
. We first have the following:

Proposition 3.21
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve and let
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
be an integer. Assume that
\mathcal{B}
is any irreducible component of
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
such that
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general fits in an exact sequence (
1.22
), with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=2
and
h^{1}(L)=1
. Then,
\mathcal{F}
fits in (
3.8
), for
p\in C
general, and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}
correspond to a general element in
\mathcal{B}
, fitting in an exact sequence as (
1.22
), i.e.
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
, for some
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N)
. Set
\delta:=\mbox{deg}(L)
,
l:=h^{0}(L)
and
r:=h^{1}(N)
. Then, from the fact that
L
has to be special and effective, cf. Lemma
1.12
, we have
g\leq\delta\leq 2g-2
. Hence, using that
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
and (
1.23
), it follows that

(3.15)

0\leq\mbox{deg}(N)=d-\delta\leq\frac{d}{2}\leq\frac{3g-4}{2}.

By (
1.22
), together with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=2
and
h^{1}(L)=1
,
N
is special with speciality

r\geq 1
, and the coboundary map
\partial_{u}
corresponding to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
has to be of corank one. The conditions
h^{1}(L)=1

and
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=1
respectively yield
l=\delta-g+2
and
l\geq r-1
.
Now set
m:=\operatorname{dim}({\rm Ext}^{1}(L,N))
; from (
1.24
), one has
m\geq g+2\delta-d-1
, whence
m\geq l+1
by (
3.15
).

With this set-up, an open dense subset of the component
\mathcal{B}
has to be associated to a suitable open dense subset

S\subseteq W^{r-1}_{2g-2+\delta-d}(C)\times C^{(2g-2-\delta)}
and, consequently, to an irreducible subvariety
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{Tot}_{1}\subsetneq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})
, endowed with a modular (rational) map:

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{Tot}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dasharrow}}\mathcal{B}\subset B_{d}^{k_{2}}

whose general fiber over
S
is
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
, which is the projectivization of the (locally determinant) variety
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subsetneq{\rm Ext}^{1}(L,N)
. In other words, the component
\mathcal{B}
has to be the image of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{Tot}_{1}
via a dominant rational modular map
\pi
as above (cf.
[
12
, § 6]
for details). From above, one must have

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\leq\operatorname{dim}(W^{r-1}_{2g-2-d+\delta}(C))+\operatorname{dim}(C^{(2g-2-\delta)})+\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1})=\operatorname{dim}(W^{r-1}_{2g-2-d+\delta})+2g-2-\delta+\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}).

Observe that, from (
3.15
), one has
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-N)\leq 2g-2
. To conclude the proof for
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-N)\leq g-1
one can refer to
[
10
, proof of Prop. 3.13]
. Assume therefore
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-N)=g+a
where
0\leq a\leq g-2
; thus
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-\delta=g-2-a
. Since
\mbox{deg}(N)=g-2-a
and
h^{1}(N)=r\geq 1
, by Riemann-Roch one has
h^{0}(N)=g-2-a-g+1+r=r-a-1
.
If
r\geq a+2
, then we have
h^{0}(N)\geq 1
, hence
N\in W^{r-a-2}_{g-2-a}(C)\subsetneq\text{Pic}^{g-2-a}(C)
; otherwise, if

r=a+1
, by
\mbox{deg}(N)=g-2-a
one deduces that
N\in\text{Pic}^{g-2-a}(C)
is general.
Hence we get

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\leq\begin{cases}\ \operatorname{dim}(\text{Pic}^{g-2-a}(C))+(2g-2-\delta)+m-\delta+g+r-4\ &\text{ if }r=a+1\\
\ \operatorname{dim}(W^{r-a-2}_{g-2-a}(C))+(2g-2-\delta)+m-\delta+g+r-4\ &\text{ if }r\geq a+2.\end{cases}

Using that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\geq\rho^{k_{2}}_{d}
we obtain the conclusion of the proof by using the same arguments as
[
11
, page 305]
, which are independent on the range of
a
.
∎

As for the unicity of the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
, we first need some preliminary general results. Recalling notation and definitions as in Theorem
1.3
, one has the following:

Lemma 3.22
.

Let
g\geq 4
and
3\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
be integers. Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
and let
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
be the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
.
Let
t\geq\nu
be an integer and, for a given integer
0\leq\ell\leq 1
, let

\vec{w}_{1,\ell}
be the corresponding maximal splitting type vector and

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{1,\ell})
be the associated balanced-plus-balanced bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
as in Theorem
1.3
. The one has:

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{1,0})=B(\nu-2,\,t+1-g-\nu)\oplus B(2,\,0)\;\;{\rm and}\,\;\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{1,1})=B(\nu-1,\,t+1-g-\nu)\oplus B(1,\,1),

where

B(-,-)
denotes the most balanced bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
of given
({\rm rank},\;{\rm degree})
, so
B(2,0)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus 2}
,
B(1,1)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
whereas the other summands are bundles of negative degrees. Moreover,

W^{1}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}&\mbox{ if $\nu\leq t<\frac{g+2}{2}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}&\mbox{ if $\frac{g+2}{2}\leq t\leq g+2-\nu$ }\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}&\mbox{ if $g-\nu+2<t<g+1$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+1$}\end{cases}

and, when
W^{1}_{t}(C)\subsetneq{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)
, any of its irreducible components is generically smooth of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}})=t-\nu\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}})=\rho(g,1,t)=2t-g-2.

Furthermore:

(i)

a general element in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
is given by
\mathcal{L}_{1,1}:=A\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(B_{t-\nu})
, where
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and
B_{t-\nu}=p_{1}+\cdots+p_{t-\nu}
the effective divisor of base points of
|\mathcal{L}_{1,1}|
;

(ii)

a general element in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
is given by
\mathcal{L}_{1,0}
, where
|\mathcal{L}_{1,0}|
is a base-point-free, complete
g^{1}_{t}
.

Proof.

By Riemann-Roch theorem, if
t\geq g+1
, i.e.
1\leq t-g
, then
W^{1}_{t}(C)={\rm Pic}^{t}(C)
(cf. also Theorem
1.3
-(1)).

When otherwise
t<g+1
, to compute possible maximal splitting type vectors
\vec{w}_{1,\ell}
as in Theorem
1.3
-(2-ii), we need to consider integers
\ell
such that
0={\rm max}\{0,3-\nu\}\leq\ell\leq r=1
with either
\ell=0
or
\ell\leq g+2+1-t-\nu=g+3-t-\nu
. Notice that
g+3-t-\nu\geq 1=r
iff
t\leq g+2-\nu
. Therefore, for
t\leq g+2-\nu
, one has the two cases
\ell\in\{0,\,1\}
whereas, for

g+2-\nu<t<g+1
, only
\ell=0
occurs. Moreover, using Riemann-Roch theorem, one gets
\rho(g,1,t)=g-2(g-t+1)=2t-g-2
, which is non-negative as soon as
t\geq\frac{g+2}{2}
.

As in Theorem
1.3
, setting
t^{\prime}:=t+1-g-\nu
, one has the following:

\bullet
for
(r,\ell)=(1,0)
, the maximal splitting type vector
\vec{w}_{1,0}
gives
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{1,0})=B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime})\oplus B(2,\,0)
. Notice that
t^{\prime}=t+1-g-\nu<0
, as
t<g+1
, and that
B(2,\,0)=\mathcal{O}^{\oplus\,2}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
. Moreover, in this case,
\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{1,0})=\rho(g,1,t)=2t-g-2\geq 0
iff
t\geq\frac{g+2}{2}
;

\bullet
for
(r,\ell)=(1,1)
, the maximal splitting type vector
\vec{w}_{1,1}
gives
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{1,1})=B(\nu-1,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(1,\,1)
, where
t^{\prime}-1=t-g-\nu<0
and where
B(1,\,1)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
. Moreover,
\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{1,1})=g-u(\vec{w}_{1,1})=\rho(g,0,t)-\nu=t-\nu\geq 0
iff
\nu\leq t
.

Therefore, the first part of the statement is a consequence of the previous computations and of Theorem
1.3
.

For what concerns the description of the general element of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
as in (i), this is a consequence of a simple dimension count; namely

\operatorname{dim}\{g^{1}_{\nu}+p_{1}+\cdots+p_{t-\nu}\}=\operatorname{dim}(C^{(t-\nu)})=\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}})
. To prove (ii) instead, let
\mathcal{L}_{1,0}
denote a general element of

\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
; thus one has
h^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{1,0})=2
as

\mathcal{L}_{1,0}
is, by definition, associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime})\oplus B(2,\,0)

on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We set
g^{1}_{t}:=|\mathcal{L}_{1,0}|
. Suppose by contradiction that such a pencil
g^{1}_{t}
has a base point
p
; so
g^{1}_{t}(-p)\in W^{1}_{t-1}(C)
has the same global section space as
g^{1}_{t}
. Since
g^{1}_{t}
is associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime})\oplus B(2,\,0)
, the linear series

g^{1}_{t}(-p)
must be therefore associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(2,\,0)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, that is
g^{1}_{t}(-p)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}_{*}\subset W^{1}_{t-1}(C)
, where
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}_{*}
denotes the component of
W^{1}_{t-1}(C)
whose general element is associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(2,\,0)
. This implies that

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}})\leq\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}_{*})+1
, which is contrary to

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}})=\rho(g,1,t)=2t-g-2>\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}_{*})+1=\rho(g,1,t-1)+1=2t-g-3
. Therefore the general element
\mathcal{L}_{1,0}
of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
gives a base-point-free linear series.
∎

We can now prove the following:

Proposition 3.23
.

Let
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
be an integer and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Assume that
\mathcal{B}
is any irreducible component of
B^{k_{2}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
such that
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general fits in an exact sequence like (
1.22
), with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(L)=2
. Then
L=K_{C}-A
and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
.

Proof.

Let
L\in{\rm Pic}^{\delta}(C)
be effective, with
h^{1}(L)=2
. Thus,

(K_{C}-L)\in W^{1}_{t}(C)
, where
t=2g-2-\delta
.

Since
\delta>\frac{d}{2}
, by Lemma
1.12
, one has
t<2g-2-\frac{d}{2}\leq g-1
, as
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
. Thus, from Lemma
3.22
,
W^{1}_{t}(C)\subsetneq{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)
and either
|K_{C}-L|=|A|+D_{2g-2-\delta}
, where
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
uniquely determined on
C
and
D_{2g-2-\delta}
is the base-divisor of
|K_{C}-L|
(which is formed by distinct points when
K_{C}-L
is general in the component of type
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
) or

|K_{C}-L|=g^{1}_{n}+D_{b^{\prime}}
, where
g^{1}_{n}
is base-point-free, for some
\frac{g+2}{2}\leq n\leq 2g-2-\delta
, and
D_{b^{\prime}}
is the base-divisor of degree
b^{\prime}\geq 0
of

|K_{C}-L|
(when
K_{C}-L
is general in the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
, then

n=2g-2-\delta
and
b^{\prime}=0
).

Consider first the case
L=K_{C}-(A-D_{2g-2-\delta-\nu})
. If
2g-2-\delta-\nu=0
, i.e. when
L=K_{C}-A
, we get
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
; when otherwise
2g-2-\delta-\nu\geq 1
, same arguments as in the proof of
[
11
, Lemma 3.14]
show that the extensions (
1.22
) with quotient line bundles like these can fill-up at most a proper, closed subscheme of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
.

If otherwise
L=K_{C}-(g^{1}_{n}+D_{b^{\prime}})
, with
b^{\prime}\geq 1
, reasoning as in the proof of
[
11
, Lemma 3.14]
one shows that extensions (
1.22
) with quotient line bundles like these are in the boundary of the family of extensions arising with quotient line bundles where
b^{\prime}=0
, namely of the form
L=K_{C}-g^{1}_{2g-2-\delta}
. Take therefore such an extension

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-g^{1}_{2g-2-\delta}\to 0

and assume that it gives rise to a
general point
[\mathcal{F}]
of a component
\mathcal{B}
. By a parameter count,

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\leq\operatorname{dim}(\{N\})+\rho(g,1,2g-2-\delta)+\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-g^{1}_{2g-2-\delta},N))-1.

Since
\operatorname{dim}(\{N\})\leq g
,
\rho(g,1,2g-2-\delta)=2(2g-2-\delta)-g-2=3g-6-2\delta
and
\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-g^{1}_{2g-2-\delta},N))=h^{0}(2K_{C}-g^{1}_{2g-2-\delta}-N)=g-1+2\delta-d
, as it follows from the fact that

\mbox{deg}(2K_{C}-g^{1}_{2g-2-\delta}-N)=2g-2+2\delta-d>2g-2
, because
2\delta-d\geq s(\mathcal{F})\geq 1
from the stability of
\mathcal{F}
, so the line bundle
2K_{C}-g^{1}_{2g-2-\delta}-N
is non-special. To sum-up, we have therefore

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\leq g+(3g-6-2\delta)+(g-2+2\delta-d)=5g-8-d.

On the other hand,
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\geq\rho_{d}^{k_{2}}=8g-11-2d
and this is a contradiction, as

5g-8-d<8g-11-2d
because
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
. Hence the proof is complete.
∎

Corollary 3.24
.

Let
g\geq 8
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-2}{2}
and

2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-4
be integers. Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
.
Then one has:

(i)

for
2g-5+2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4
,
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}\cup\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
;

(ii)

for
2g-2\leq d\leq 2g-6+2\nu
,
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
and the locus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,2}
is contained therein as a proper, closed subscheme.

Proof.

Part (i) follows from Propositions
3.9
,
3.15
-(i) and (ii),
3.21
and
3.23
. Part (ii) follows from Propositions
3.9
,
3.15
-(iii),
3.21
and
3.23
.
∎

Remark 3.25
.

Notice that the irreducibility of
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
for
2g-2\leq d\leq 2g-5+2\nu
is in accordance with Teixidor I Bigas’ result, i.e. Theorem
0.1
-(i) above. Indeed, the claim therein is that
B_{d}^{k_{2}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
can have extra components than
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,2}
if and only if there exists a positive integer
n
,
2n<4g-4-d
, for which
W^{1}_{n}(C)\neq\emptyset
and s.t.
\operatorname{dim}(W^{1}_{n}(C))\geq 2g+2n-d-5
. The bounds on
d
in particular give
2n<2g-2
, i.e.
n<g-1
; moreover, since we are on a general
\nu
-gonal curve, one must have also
n\geq\nu
. Thus
n
must be such that
\nu\leq n<g-1
. From Lemma
3.22
, we know that in such cases one has either
\operatorname{dim}(W^{1}_{n}(C))=n-\nu
or
\operatorname{dim}(W^{1}_{n}(C))=2n-g-2
. Using Teixidor I Bigas’ reducibility condition, one should have therefore either

2n-g-2=\operatorname{dim}(W^{1}_{n}(C))\geq 2g+2n-d-5
, implying
d\geq 3g-3
,
or
n-\nu=\operatorname{dim}(W^{1}_{n}(C))\geq 2g+2n-d-5
, giving
d\geq 2g-5+n+\nu\geq 2g-5+2\nu
. Both of them contradict that
2g-2\leq d\leq 2g-6+2\nu
.

4.
Brill Noether loci in speciality 3: proof of Theorem
0.5

Here we focus on bundles of speciality
3
, with the aim of studying irreducible components of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
. In this case, recall that the expected dimension of any such a component is

\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=10g-18-3d.

From Lemma
2.1
, if
\mathcal{B}
is any irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
, its general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
corresponds to a bundle
\mathcal{F}
of degree
d
such that
i:=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
. Therefore, in the light of Lemma
1.12
and of Theorem
1.14
, in order to find possible obstructions for a component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
to exist we are going to analyze extensions as in (
1.22
) (equivalently as in (
1.25
)) via quotient line bundles
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
being effective, of degree
\delta:=\mbox{deg}(L)>\frac{d}{2}
(by stability) and of speciality either
h^{1}(L)=1
, in which case the bundle
\mathcal{F}
will be of
second type
, or

h^{1}(L)=3
, in which case
\mathcal{F}
will be instead of
first type
, in the sense of Definition
1.15
(cf. e.g. Propositions
4.8
,

4.9
,
4.13
,
4.32
).

Nevertheless, when it turns out that obstructions do not exist, to explicitly construct irreducible components
\mathcal{B}
of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
with a
parametric approach
via line-bundle extensions as well as to control the infinitesimal behavior at its general point
[\mathcal{F}]
(namely, the dimension of the kernel of the associated
Petri map

\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
as in Remark
1.10
) we will sometimes allow also quotients
L=\omega_{C}(-D)
s.t.
h^{1}(L)=2
, when such a variation to the main subdivision
among
first type
or
second type bundles
as in Definition
1.15
makes it easier to deal with the
parametric construction
of such a component
\mathcal{B}
as well as with the study of the local behavior at its general point (cf. e.g. Proposition
4.22
below).

\boxed{4g-6\leq d\leq 4g-4:\;\mbox{no component}}
In this range, using same arguments as in the proof of Proposition
3.2
, one gets:

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
g\geq 4
,
3\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
and
4g-6\leq d\leq 4g-4
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\emptyset
.

Therefore, from now on, we will limit our analysis to
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-7
.
To approach the study in this range especially for bundles of
first type
(as in Definition
1.15
) on
C
a general
\nu
-gonal curve, we will make use of the following preliminary result.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
g\geq 4
and
3\leq\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
be integers. Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
and let
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
be the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
.
Let
t\geq\nu
be an integer and, for a given integer
0\leq\ell\leq 2
, let

\vec{w}_{2,\ell}
denote the corresponding maximal splitting type vector and

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,\ell})
be the associated balanced-plus-balanced bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
as in Theorem
1.3
. Then one has:

\begin{cases}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,0})=&B(\nu-3,\,t+1-g-\nu)\oplus B(3,\,0),\\
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,1})=&B(\nu-2,\,t-g-\nu)\oplus B(2,\,1),\\
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,2})=&B(\nu-1,\,t-1-g-\nu)\oplus B(1,\,2)\end{cases}

where
B(-,-)
denotes the most balanced bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
of given
({\rm rank},\;{\rm degree})
, so
B(3,0)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{\oplus 3}
,
B(2,1)=\mathcal{\mathcal{}}O_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
and
B(1,2)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(2)
, whereas the other summands are bundles of negative degrees. Moreover:

(i)
for
\nu=3
, one has respectively:

for
4\leq g\leq 5

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $3\leq t<\frac{g+5}{2}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{g+5}{2}\leq t<\frac{2g+6}{3}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq t<6=2\nu$ }\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $6=2\nu\leq t<g+2$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

for
g=6

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $3\leq t<\frac{g+5}{2}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{g+5}{2}\leq t<6=2\nu=\frac{2g+6}{3}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $6=2\nu=\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq t<g+2$ }\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

whereas, for
g\geq 7
,

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $3\leq t<6=2\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}&\mbox{if $6=2\nu\leq t<\frac{g+5}{2}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{g+5}{2}\leq t<\frac{2g+6}{3}$ }\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq t<g+2$ }\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

(ii)
for
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, one has:

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $\nu\leq t<2\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}&\mbox{if $2\nu\leq t<\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}\leq t<\frac{2g+6}{3}$ }\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq t\leq g+3-\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $t=g+4-\nu$ }\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

(iii)
for
4\leq\nu=\frac{g+3}{3}
, one has:

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $\nu\leq t<\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}\leq t<2\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\,\cup\,\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}&\mbox{if $2\nu\leq t<\frac{2g+6}{3}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq t\leq g+3-\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\ \cup\ \overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $t=g+4-\nu$ }\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

(iv)
for
4\leq\nu=\frac{g+4}{3}
, one has:

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $\nu\leq t<2\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}&\mbox{if $2\nu\leq t<g+3-\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\,\cup\,\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}&\mbox{if $t=g+3-\nu=\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $t=g+4-\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

(v)
for
4\leq\frac{g+4}{3}<\nu\leq\frac{g+6}{3}
, one has:

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $\nu\leq t\leq g+3-\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $t=g+4-\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

(vi)
for
4\leq\frac{g+6}{3}<\nu\leq\frac{g+9}{3}
, one has:

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $\nu\leq t\leq g+4-\nu$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

(vii)
for
4\leq\frac{g+9}{3}<\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, one has:

W^{2}_{t}(C)=\begin{cases}\emptyset&\mbox{if $\nu\leq t<\frac{2g+6}{3}$}\\
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}&\mbox{if $\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq t<g+2$}\\
{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)&\mbox{if $t\geq g+2$}\end{cases}

Furthermore, when
\emptyset\neq W^{2}_{t}(C)\subsetneq{\rm Pic}^{t}(C)
, any of its irreducible components as above is generically smooth of the following dimensions:

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}})=t-2\nu,\;\;\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}})=2t-g-\nu-2\;{\rm and}\;\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})=3t-2g-6,

and

(a)

a general element in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
is given by
\mathcal{L}_{2,2}:=A^{\otimes 2}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(B_{t-2\nu})
, where
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and
B_{t-2\nu}=p_{1}+\cdots+p_{t-2\nu}
an effective divisor of base points of
|\mathcal{L}_{2,2}|
;

(b)

a general element in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}
is given by
\mathcal{L}_{2,1}:=A\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(M_{t-\nu})
, where
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and
M_{t-\nu}=p_{1}+\cdots+p_{t-\nu}\in C^{(t-\nu)}
an effective divisor of movable points such that
|\mathcal{L}_{2,1}|
is a base-point-free
and birationally very ample linear system
g^{2}_{t}
, that is, the morphism associated to the
g^{2}_{t}
is a birational map onto its image.

(c)

a general element in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
is given by
\mathcal{L}_{2,0}
, where
|\mathcal{L}_{2,0}|
is a base-point-free and birationally very ample linear system
g^{2}_{t}
, that is, its associated morphism is a birational map onto its image.

Proof.

We prove the most general case
\nu\geq 4
, as the case
\nu=3
being similar so left to the reader. We use notation as in Theorem
1.3
; if
2=r\leq t-g
, namely
t\geq g+2
, by Riemann-Roch (cf. also Theorem
1.3
-(1)) one has

W^{2}_{t}(C)={\rm Pic}^{t}(C)
.

Let us consider therefore
4\leq\nu\leq t<g+2
. To compute maximal splitting type vectors
\vec{w}_{2,\ell}
as in Theorem
1.3
-(2-ii), we need to consider integers
\ell
such that
0={\rm max}\{0,4-\nu\}\leq\ell\leq r=2
. Therefore as in Theorem
1.3
, setting
t^{\prime}:=t+1-g-\nu
one has:

\bullet
when
(r,\ell)=(2,0)
,

\vec{w}_{2,0}
gives
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,0})=B(\nu-3,\,t^{\prime})\oplus B(3,\,0)
, where
t^{\prime}=t+1-g-\nu<0
, as
t<g+2
and
\nu\geq 4
, and where
B(3,\,0)=\mathcal{O}^{\oplus\,3}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
;

\bullet
when
(r,\ell)=(2,1)
, one has
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,1})=B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(2,\,1)
, where
t^{\prime}-1=t-g-\nu<0
and where
B(2,\,1)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
;

\bullet
when
(r,\ell)=(2,2)
,
\vec{w}_{2,2}
is s.t.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,2})=B(\nu-1,\,t^{\prime}-2)\oplus B(1,\,2)
, where
t^{\prime}-2=t-1-g-\nu<0
and
B(1,\,2)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(2)
.

Since, from Theorem
1.3
-(2-iv), the integer
0\leq\ell\leq r=2
has to be furthermore chosen in such a way that either
\ell=0
or
\ell\leq g+2+1-t-\nu=g+5-t-\nu
, one sees that the quantity
g+5-t-\nu
is greater than or equal to
2
iff
t\leq g+3-\nu
, it equals
1
iff
t=g+4-\nu
whereas it is less than or equal to
0
iff
g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2
. Therefore our first main sub-division is given by the following:

\bullet

\nu\leq t\leq g+3-\nu
, in which case all integers
\ell=0,1,2
have to be considered,

\bullet

t=g+4-\nu
, in which case only integers
\ell=0,1
have to be considered,

\bullet

g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2
, in which case only
\ell=0
can occur.

On the other hand, from Theorem
1.3
-(2-iv), we also need to consider when the integers
\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{2,\ell})=\rho(g,2-\ell,t)-\ell\,\nu
are non-negative, for
0\leq\ell\leq 2
. Therefore, one first computes:

\rho(g,2,t)=3t-2g-6,\;\,\rho(g,1,t)=2t-g-2\,\;{\rm and}\;\;\rho(g,0,t)=t,

to get

\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{2,0})=3t-2g-6,\;\;\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{2,1})=2t-g-2-\nu\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{2,2})=t-2\nu.

Notice that

\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{2,0})\geq 0
iff
t\geq\frac{2g+6}{3}
,

\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{2,1})\geq 0
iff
t\geq\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}
whereas
\rho^{\prime}(g,\vec{w}_{2,2})\geq 0
iff
t\geq 2\nu
.

For

(4.1)

g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2,

where from above only
\ell=0
can occur, one has that

g+5-\nu\geq\frac{2g+6}{3}
iff
\nu\leq\frac{g+9}{3}
. In other words, for
g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2
and
\frac{g+9}{3}<\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, one has
W^{2}_{t}(C)=\emptyset
, whereas, for
g+5-\nu\leq t<g+2
and
\nu\leq\frac{g+9}{3}
, one has that
W^{2}_{t}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
is irreducible, generically smooth and of the expected dimension
\rho(g,2,t)=3t-2g-6
, as it follows from Theorem
1.3
-(2-iv).

For
t=g+4-\nu
, where from above
\ell=0,1
can occur, one has
that
g+4-\nu\geq\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}\geq\frac{2g+6}{3}
iff
\nu\leq\frac{g+6}{3}
. In other words, for
\frac{g+6}{3}<\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, one has
W^{2}_{g+4-\nu}(C)=\emptyset
, whereas, for
\nu\leq\frac{g+6}{3}
,
W^{2}_{t}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
, where each irreducible component is generically smooth and of stated dimension, as it follows from Theorem
1.3
-(2-iv).

For
\nu\leq t\leq g+3-\nu
, all cases
\ell=0,1,2
have to be considered. Thus, one is reduced to compare the four relevant integers involved in these cases, namely

g+3-\nu,\;\,\frac{2g+6}{3},\,\;\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}\,\;{\rm and}\,\;2\nu.

Notice that,
for
\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, one has
\nu\leq 2\nu\leq\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}\leq\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq g+3-\nu
; for
\nu=\frac{g+3}{3}
, one has

\nu\leq\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}\leq 2\nu\leq\frac{2g+6}{3}\leq g+3-\nu
; for
\nu=\frac{g+4}{3}
, one has
\nu\leq 2\nu\leq\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}=g+3-\nu<\frac{2g+6}{3}
; finally, for
\frac{g+6}{3}<\nu<\lfloor\frac{g+3}{2}\rfloor
, one has

g+3-\nu<\frac{g+2+\nu}{2}<\frac{2g+6}{3}<2\nu
and one concludes using Theorem
1.3
-(2-iv).

Therefore, the first part of the statement is a direct consequence of the previous computations and of Theorem
1.3
.

For what concerns the description of the general element of any component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,\ell}}}
, for
0\leq\ell\leq 2
, the reasoning is as follows.

(a) Considering
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
, since
g\geq 2(\nu-1)
, then by Proposition
1.1
one has
\operatorname{dim}(|A^{\otimes 2}|)=2
, i.e.
h^{0}(A^{\otimes 2})=3
, so
A^{\otimes 2}\in W^{2}_{2\,\nu}(C)
. Thus, for
p_{1}+\cdots+p_{t-2\nu}\in C^{(t-2\nu)}
general, one has
A^{\otimes 2}(p_{1}+\cdots+p_{t-2\nu})\in W^{2}_{2\,t}
. If
f:C\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
denotes the map associated to
|A|
then one concludes from the fact that
f_{*}(A^{\otimes 2}(p_{1}+\cdots+p_{t-2\nu}))
gives rise to the maximal splitting type vector
\vec{w}_{2,2}
and from
\operatorname{dim}(C^{(t-2\nu)})=t-2\nu=\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}})
.

(b) Let
\mathcal{L}_{2,1}
be a general element of

\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}
. Then one has that
h^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{2,1})=3
since
\mathcal{L}_{2,1}
is associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(2,\,1)
. We set

g^{2}_{t}:=|\mathcal{L}_{2,1}|
.
Assume that
g^{2}_{t}
has a base point
p
. Then the linear series

g^{2}_{t}(-p)\in W^{2}_{t-1}(C)
has the same global section space as
g^{2}_{t}
. Thus
g^{2}_{t}(-p)
must be associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime}-2)\oplus B(2,\,1)
, since
\mathcal{L}_{2,1}
is associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(2,\,1)
. This means that
g^{2}_{t}(-p)
is an element of the component

\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}_{*}
of
W^{2}_{t-1}(C)
whose general element is associated to
B(\nu-2,\,t^{\prime}-2)\oplus B(2,\,1)
. Thus it follows
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}})\leq\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}_{*})+1
. It cannot happen due to
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}})=\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}_{*})+2
. Therefore a general element
|\mathcal{L}_{2,1}|=g^{2}_{t}
of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}
gives rise to a base-point-free linear series.

Now, to prove birational very-ampleness, consider the morphism
\varphi:C\to\varphi(C)\subset\mathbb{P}^{2}
defined by the complete linear series
g^{2}_{t}
. Set

m:=\mbox{deg}(\varphi)\ \mbox{ and }\gamma:=\mbox{ geometric genus of }\varphi(C).

If we assume that
m\geq 2
and if we let
\Sigma^{m}_{g,\gamma}\subset\mathcal{M}_{g}
be the sublocus of curves admitting a
m
-sheeted covering map to a smooth curve of genus
\gamma
, it is well-known that
\operatorname{dim}(\Sigma^{m}_{g,\gamma})=2g-2+(2m-3)(1-\gamma)
, see for example
[
26
]
. Thus, since the
\nu
-gonal stratum
\mathcal{M}^{1}_{g,\nu}\subset\mathcal{M}_{g}
has dimension
2g-5+2\nu>2g-2
, previous computations yield that

\mbox{ either }m=1\ \mbox{ or }\gamma=0.

Suppose by contradiction that
\gamma=0
; since
|\mathcal{L}_{2,1}|=g^{2}_{t}
is complete from above, then the line sections of
\varphi(C)
form a complete linear series on
\varphi(C)
, i.e.
h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{\varphi(C)}(1))=3
.
Thus the rational plane curve
\varphi(C)
is a smooth conic and

g^{2}_{t}=|\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\varphi(C)}(1))|
. This forces
t=2m
and hence the curve
C
carries a

g^{1}_{m}=|\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\varphi(C)}(q))|
with
m=\frac{t}{2}
for
q\in\varphi(C)
. On the other hand,

[
1
, Theorem (2.6)]
states that any
g^{1}_{h}
with
h<\frac{g+2}{2}
on a general
\nu
-gonal curve is composed with the
g^{1}_{\nu}
; this implies that
g^{1}_{m}=g^{1}_{\nu}
, since
t<g+2
and
g^{1}_{m}
is a base-point-free complete pencil. This, combined with
g^{2}_{t}=|\varphi^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\varphi(C)}(1))|
, yields that
g^{2}_{t}=2g^{1}_{\nu}
which is a contradiction, since
g^{2}_{t}=|\mathcal{L}_{2,1}|
is a general element of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}
. As a consequence, one has
\gamma\neq 0
which yields
m=1
, i.e., the net
g^{2}_{t}=|\mathcal{L}_{2,1}|
is birationally very ample on
C
.

(c) Let now
\mathcal{L}_{2,0}
be a general element of

\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
. Then it turns out that
h^{0}(\mathcal{L}_{2,0})=3
since
\mathcal{L}_{2,0}
is associated to the bundle

B(\nu-3,\,t^{\prime})\oplus B(3,\,0)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We set

g^{2}_{t}:=|\mathcal{L}_{2,0}|
; if by contradiction the net
g^{2}_{t}
had a base-point
p
, then the linear series

g^{2}_{t}(-p)\in W^{2}_{t-1}(C)
would be associated to
B(\nu-3,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(3,\,0)
, since
\mathcal{L}_{2,0}
is associated to the bundle
B(\nu-3,\,t^{\prime})\oplus B(3,\,0)
. Let
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}_{*}
denote the component of
W^{2}_{t-1}(C)
whose general element is associated to
B(\nu-3,\,t^{\prime}-1)\oplus B(3,\,0)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
; therefore one would have
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})\leq\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}_{*})+1
which cannot occur since

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})=3t-2g-6
whereas

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}_{*})=3(t-1)-2g-6
. Therefore a general element
\mathcal{L}_{2,0}
of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
gives rise to a base-point-free linear series. By the same reasoning as in the case (b) above, this linear system
g^{2}_{t}
is also birationally very ample.
∎

To proceed with our analysis in the range
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-7
we recall, from Remark
1.10
, that any possible irreducible component
\mathcal{B}\subseteq B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
must have
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\geq\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=10g-3d-18
and, from Lemma
2.1
, a general
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
must have
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
. Furthermore, by Theorem
1.14
, we get that
\mathcal{F}
can be either of
first type
or of
second type
in the sense of Definition
1.15
. In this set-up, we find some necessary conditions for a component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
to exist.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
and let
\mathcal{\mathcal{F}}
be a rank-
2
stable bundle, of degree

2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-7
of speciality
i=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
and fitting in an exact sequence (
1.25
) where, using notation as in (
1.22
), we set

\delta:=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-D))
(namely
D\in C^{(2g-2-\delta)}
), with
\delta\leq 2g-2
, so that
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-\delta
.

(1) Assume
\mathcal{F}
to be a bundle of first type (in the sense of Definition
1.15
). Then one of the following occurs:

(1-a)

\delta\leq 2g-2-2\nu
, or

(1-b)

\delta\leq\frac{3g-6-\nu}{2}
, or

(1-c)

\delta\leq\frac{4g-12}{3}
.

(2) If otherwise
\mathcal{F}
is assumed to be of second type, then one of the following occurs:

(2-a)

2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-3
, or

(2-b)

2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-4-\nu
, or

(2-c)

2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{7g-6}{2}
.

Proof.

To simplify our computations, we take into account the natural isomorphism given by
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)\cong B^{3}_{4g-4-d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(4g-4-d)
which is induced by
\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{E}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
; in other words, by Serre duality, we can deal with stable bundles
\mathcal{E}
, of degree
d^{\prime}:=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{E})=4g-4-d
such that
3\leq d^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
and
h^{0}(\mathcal{E})=3
. From
[
5
, Lemma 1]
,
\mathcal{E}
fits in:

(4.2)

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\to\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{L}\to 0,

where
D
is an effective divisor, of degree
\mbox{deg}(D)=2g-2-\delta
. Notice that (
4.2
) is nothing but the exact sequence (
1.25
) dualized and then tensored by
\omega_{C}
so, in that notation,
\mathcal{L}:=\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}
. Therefore,
\mathcal{E}
will be of
first type
iff
h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D))=3
otherwise of
second type
iff
h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D))=1
in the sense of Definition
1.15
.

We set
\delta^{\prime}:=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{L})=2g-2+\delta-d
, as by assumption
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-\delta
, and so
\mbox{deg}(D)
reads also
\mbox{deg}(D)=d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}
. Since
D
is effective and
\mathcal{E}
is stable, then we have
0\leq d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}=\mbox{deg}(D)<\frac{d^{\prime}}{2}\leq g-1
, i.e.
0\leq d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}=\mbox{deg}(D)\leq g-2
. Once again from stability we have
\delta^{\prime}>\frac{d^{\prime}}{2}\geq\frac{3}{2}
, i.e.
\delta^{\prime}\geq 2
; moreover, since
d^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}+(d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime})\leq 2g-2
, using that
0\leq d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}\leq g-2
, then one deduces that
2\leq\delta^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
.

To sum-up, we have conditions
0\leq d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}\leq g-2
and
2\leq\delta^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
which, in notation
d^{\prime}=4g-4-d
,
\delta^{\prime}=2g-2+\delta-d
and
d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}=2g-2-\delta
, are respectively equivalent to

(4.3)

g\leq\delta=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-D))\leq 2g-2\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;0\leq d-\delta=\mbox{deg}(N)\leq 2g-4.

(1) We start by assuming that
\mathcal{E}
is of
first type
, i.e.
h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D))=3
. Since
\mbox{deg}(D)=d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}\leq g-2
, therefore
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)
turns out to be not general of its degree but it must belong to (at least) one of the irreducible components of
W^{2}_{d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}(C)
as described in Lemma
4.2

(1-a) If
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}(C)
, then one must have
d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}\geq 2\nu
, which is the necessary and sufficient condition for the component

\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
of
\subseteq W^{2}_{d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}(C)
to be not empty. Taking into account that
d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}=2g-2-\delta
, the previous condition reads
2g-2-\delta\geq 2\nu
, equivalent to condition in (1-a).

(1-b) If
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}(C)
, then one must have

2(d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime})-g-\nu-2\geq 0
ensuring
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\neq\emptyset
. This reads
3g-6-2\delta-\nu\geq 0
, which is the stated condition in (1-b).

(1-c) If
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}(C)
, then one must have

3(d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime})-2g-6\geq 0
. This gives
4g-12-3\delta\geq 0
, which is exactly condition (1-c).

(2) Assume now that
\mathcal{E}
is of
second type
. Thus
h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D))=1
, so
\mbox{deg}(D)=d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}\geq 0
and
h^{0}(\mathcal{L})\geq 2
.

(2-a) If
\mathcal{L}
is general of its degree, then necessarily
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{L})=\delta^{\prime}\geq g+1
so
d^{\prime}=(d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime})+\delta^{\prime}\geq 0+g+1=g+1
; in other words
g+1\leq d^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
. Since
d^{\prime}=4g-4-d
, we get the condition in (2-a).

(2-b) If otherwise
\mathcal{L}
is not general in
{\rm Pic}^{\delta^{\prime}}(C)
with
\delta^{\prime}\leq g
, the fact that
h^{0}(\mathcal{L})\geq 2
forces
\mathcal{L}\in W^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}(C)
. From Lemma
3.22
, when
\mathcal{L}
is an element of the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\subset W^{1}_{\delta^{\prime}}(C)
, then one must have
\delta^{\prime}\geq\nu
which is the necessary and sufficient condition for the non-emptyness of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
, by Lemma
3.22
. Thus, in such a case, one must have
\nu\leq\delta^{\prime}\leq d^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
, i.e.
\nu\leq d^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
. Plugging
d^{\prime}=4g-4-d
, we get condition in (2-b).

(2-c) Finally, if
\mathcal{L}
belongs to the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
which by Lemma
3.22
is not empty iff
\delta^{\prime}\geq\frac{g+2}{2}
, then
\frac{g+2}{2}\leq\delta^{\prime}\leq d^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
, in particular
\frac{g+2}{2}\leq d^{\prime}\leq 2g-2
, and we are therefore in the last case (2-c).
∎

According to the cases considered in Lemma
4.3
, we set the following:

Definition 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{B}\subseteq B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
be an irreducible component.

(1)

Assume that
\mathcal{B}
is such that its general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
is a bundle of first type in the sense of Definition
1.15
. We will set
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,3,(1-x)}
(respectively,
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3,(1-x)}
) according to the fact that the component is superabundant (respectively, regular) and its general point
[\mathcal{F}]
corresponds to a stable, rank-
2
, degree
d
vector bundle of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
arising as in case (1-x), for
x=a,b,c
as in the proof of Lemma
4.3
, whose cases arise from Lemma
4.2
;

(2)

Similarly, assume that
\mathcal{B}
is such that its general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
is a bundle of second type. We will set
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,3,(2-x)}
(respectively,
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3,(2-x)}
) according to the fact that the component is superabundant (respectively, regular) and its general point
[\mathcal{F}]
corresponds to a stable, rank-
2
, degree
d
vector bundle of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
arising as in case (2-x), for
x=a,b,c
as in the proof of Lemma
4.3
, whose cases arise from Lemma
3.22
;

(3)

If otherwise the component
\mathcal{B}
has been constructed via parametric approach using line-bundle extensions obtained by suitable natural modifications of case (1-x) (respectively, (2-x)), for
x=a,b,c
as above, we will set

\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,3,(j-x)-mod}
(respectively,
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3,(j-x)-mod}
) according to the fact that the component is superabundant (respectively, regular) and its general point
[\mathcal{F}]
corresponds to a stable, rank-
2
, degree
d
vector bundle of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
arising via
modification of case (j-x), for
j=1,2
and
x=a,b,c
as in the proof of Lemma
4.3
.

The following auxiliary result deals with rank-
2
vector bundles of
second type
with speciality
i=3
and it is similar to Lemma
3.10
, proved for vector bundles of
second type
but of speciality
i=2
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
and let
\mathcal{F}
be a rank-
2
vector bundle of degree
d
, speciality
i=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which is assumed to be of second type in the sense of Definition
1.15
. Let
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
be an effective divisor of degree
s>0

and
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+s}(C)
such that
\mathcal{F}
fits in an exact sequence of the form (
1.25
).

(a) when
\mathcal{F}
is of type
(2-a)
as in Lemma
4.3
, assume that
N
is general and that
\mathcal{F}
arises from a general point of an irreducible component of the degeneracy locus

\mathcal{W}_{2}:=\left\{u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N)\;|\;{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})\geq 2\right\}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N),

as in (
3.1
), and that, for any
q\in D_{s}
, each irreducible component of the degeneracy locus

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{2}}:=\left\{w\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q),N(q))\;|\;{\rm corank}(\partial_{w})\geq 2\right\}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q),N(q))

is of expected codimension
\widetilde{c(2)}:=2(h^{0}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q))-h^{1}(N(q))+2)
, as in (
3.2
);

(b) when
\mathcal{F}
is of type
(2-b)
as in Lemma
4.3
, assume that
N=K_{C}-(A+B_{b})
, where
A
is the unique line bundle on
C
s.t.
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
,
B_{b}
is an effective divisor of degree
b\geq 0
such that
|A+B_{b}|=g^{1}_{\nu+b}
, i.e.
B_{b}
is the base locus for such a
g^{1}_{\nu+b}
, which is assumed to be such that
B_{b}\cap D_{s}=\emptyset
;

(c) when
\mathcal{F}
is of type
(2-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
, assume that
N=K_{C}-g^{1}_{t}-B_{b}
, where
g^{1}_{t}
is a base point free linear series of degree
t
and dimension
1
,
B_{b}
is an effective divisor of degree
b\geq 0
such that
|g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}|=g^{1}_{t+b}
, i.e.
B_{b}
is the base locus for such a
g^{1}_{t+b}
, which is assumed to be such that
B_{b}\cap D_{s}=\emptyset
.

Then, in all these cases,
\mathcal{F}
fits also in the exact sequence:
0\to N(-D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0
.

Proof.

Notice that, from the assumptions, being
\mathcal{F}
of speciality
3
, of
second type
and fitting in an exact sequence of the form (
1.25
), one has that
h^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))=1
(so
h^{0}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))=g-s
),
h^{1}(N)\geq 2
and

\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for some
u\in\mathcal{W}_{2}\subseteq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N)
.

As in the proof of Lemma
3.10
, from the assumptions one deduces that
N(-D_{s})
is also a sub-line bundle of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
so, to prove the statement, it is enough to show that the quotient sheaf
\frac{\mathcal{F}_{u}}{N(-D_{s})}
is torsion-free on
C
, namely that the sub-line bundle
N(-D_{s})
is
saturated
in
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. This in turn is equivalent to prove that the general global section of
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})\simeq\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
is nowhere vanishing.

Therefore, as in the proof of Lemma
3.10
, we need to show that the intersections of the zero-loci of global sections of
\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
is empty, i.e.
h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(-q))=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(q))\leq 2,\;\;\mbox{for every point}\;q\in C
. To do so, tensoring the exact sequence (
1.25
) by
N^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})
gives

0\ \to\ \mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\to}}\ \omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}=N^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\ \to\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}\ \ \to\ \ 0,

where the injective bundle-map
\sigma
gives rise to a global section
\tilde{\sigma}\in H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})
which is not zero at any point
q\notin D_{s}
. We claim that, under the given assumptions, for any
q\in D_{s}
there exists also a global section in
H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})
which is not zero at
q
. Assume therefore
q\in D_{s}
and tensor (
1.25
) by
\mathcal{O}_{C}(q)
to get

(4.4)

0\to N(q)\to\mathcal{F}_{v}:=\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(q)\to K_{C}-(D_{s}-q)\to 0.

In case (b), since
N=K_{C}-(A+B_{b})
, then
h^{1}(N(q))=h^{0}(A+B_{b}-q)=1
, as
q\in D_{s}
and, by assumption,
D_{s}\cap B_{b}=\emptyset
. Since
h^{1}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q))=1
, as
q\in D_{s}
, from (
4.4
) we deduce that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(q))\leq 2
, as desired. Similar conclusion holds true in case (c), as
N=K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})
and
B_{b}\cap D_{s}=\emptyset
. Finally, in case (a), by assumption each irreducible component of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{2}}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q),N(q))
is of the expected codimension
\widetilde{c(2)}:=2(h^{0}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q))-h^{1}(N(q))+2)
as in (
3.2
). Notice that
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q),N(q))\simeq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N)
, since they are both isomorphic to
H^{1}(N+D_{s}-K_{C})
; moreover, since
N
is general of its degree and of speciality at least
2
, as
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
, whereas
h^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))=1
, then
h^{1}(N)=h^{1}(N(q))+1
and

h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})=h^{0}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q))-1
. By (
3.2
), each irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
has dimension at least
m-c(2)
, where
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N))
and
c(2)=2(h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})-h^{1}(N)+2)
. Since
\widetilde{c(2)}=c(2)+4
, then for
u
general in an irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
, we have that
v\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{2}}
, where
v
is the point defined as in (
4.4
). Hence, from
h^{1}(K_{C}-(D_{s}-q))=1
as
q\in D_{s}
, we get
h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}(-q))=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})\leq 2
, as desired.
∎

\boxed{4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7:\;\mbox{no component}}
In this range, Lemma
4.3
gives complete obstructions for an irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
to exist. Indeed, we have the following:

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
g\geq 15
,
3\leq\nu<\frac{g-2}{4}
and
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Then
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)=\emptyset
.

Proof.

To prove this, we will make use of Lemma
4.3
. Notice that, in the chosen ranges for
d
and for
\nu
, a priori any possible irreducible component
\mathcal{B}\subseteq B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)

has to be necessarily
superabundant
. Indeed, from the bounds on
d
, one has

\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=10g-18-3d\leq 10g-18-3(4g-4-2\nu)=-2g-6+6\nu

and the latter is negative as soon as
\nu<\frac{g+3}{3}
, which certainly holds true by the chosen bounds on
\nu
.

Assume therefore by contradiction that
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)\neq\emptyset
and let
\mathcal{B}\subseteq B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
be any of its irreducible components.
Let
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
be a general point of such a component.

For
4g-3-\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7
, one immediately deduces that cases
(2-a),\;(2-b),\;(2-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
cannot occur; indeed, the three quantities appearing therein, namely
3g-3
,
\frac{7g-6}{2}
and
4g-4-\nu
respectively, are strictly less than
4g-3-\nu
, under the bounds on
\nu
. In other words, in such a range for
d
and
\nu
, if such a bundle
\mathcal{F}
actually exits then it must be necessarily a vector bundle of
first type
. In such a case, from the proof of Lemma
4.3
, for such a bundle
\mathcal{F}
(equivalently for the bundle
\mathcal{E}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
therein) it must hold that
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in W^{2}_{d^{\prime}-\delta^{\prime}}(C)=W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
. Case
(1-a)
deals with
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
, where the necessary and sufficient condition for such a component to be not empty is
\delta\leq 2g-2-2\nu
. This, together with
d-\delta\leq 2g-4
as in (
4.3
), gives
d\leq 4g-6-2\nu
, which is out of the range for
d
, so case
(1-a)
cannot occur. Similarly, case
(1-b)
deals with
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
, where the necessary and sufficient condition for such a component to be not empty is
\delta\leq\frac{3g-6-\nu}{2}
. This gives another contradiction; indeed
d-\delta\leq 2g-4
by (
4.3
) therefore

d=\delta+(d-\delta)\leq\frac{3g-6-\nu}{2}+(2g-4)=\frac{7g-14-\nu}{2}
which contradicts that
d\geq 4g-3-\nu
, because of the bounds on
\nu
. Thus also case (1-b) cannot occur in this range. Finally, case (1-c) is related to the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subset W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
, which is not empty iff
\delta\leq\frac{4g-12}{3}
. Since

d-\delta\leq 2g-4
from (
4.3
), one in particular has
d=\delta+(d-\delta)\leq\frac{4g-12}{3}+2g-4=\frac{10g-24}{3}
. From the fact that
d\geq 4g-3-\nu
, it follows that

4g-3-\nu\leq\frac{10g-24}{3}
, i.e.
12g-9-3\nu\leq 10g-24
, thus
\nu\geq\frac{2}{3}g+5
, contradicting the assumptions on
\nu
. So also case (1-c) cannot occur in such a range for
d
. To sum-up,
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\emptyset
for
4g-3-\nu\leq d\leq 4g-7
.

Consider now
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-4-\nu
, where we can argue as follows. In case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
, one must have
\delta\leq 2g-2-2\nu
and, by (
4.3
),
d-\delta\leq 2g-4
, giving
d=\delta+(d-\delta)\leq 4g-6-2\nu
which is out of the range for
d
. In case
(1-b)
one must have
\delta\leq\frac{3g-6-\nu}{2}
, on the other hand by the necessary condition on stability we must have also
d<2\delta\leq 3g-6-\nu
which contradicts
d\geqslant 4g-4-2\nu
from the bounds on
\nu
. As for case
(1-c)
, one must have
\delta\leq\frac{4g-12}{3}
; this, together with
d-\delta\leq 2g-4
from (
4.3
), gives
d=\delta+(d-\delta)\leq\frac{4g-12}{3}+(2g-4)=\frac{10g-24}{3}
. Notice that
\frac{10g-24}{3}<4g-4-2\nu
as soon as
\nu<\frac{g+6}{3}
, which certainly holds true in our bounds for
\nu
. Therefore, in the given range for
d
no bundles
\mathcal{F}
of
first type
are allowed. For case
(2-a)
, from Lemma
4.3
, we must have
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-3
on the other hand, form the bounds on
\nu
, we have that
3g-3<4g-4-2\nu
, therefore we are out of the chosen range for
d
. Similar strategy can be used for case
(2-c)
which, from Lemma
4.3
, forces
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{7g-6}{2}
; since
\nu<\frac{g-2}{4}
by assumption, we notice that
\frac{7g-6}{2}<4g-4-2\nu
, therefore once again we are out of the chosen range for
d
.

We are left with excluding case
(2-b)
, when
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-4-\nu
. For a possible general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
, since
\mathcal{F}
has to be of
second type
(in the sense of Definition
1.15
), it must fit in an exact sequence of the form (
1.25
), with
h^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D))=1
. On the other hand, since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
with
\mathcal{F}
as in case
(2-b)
, then

K_{C}-N\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\subset W^{1}_{2g-2+\delta-d}(C)
, where elements of such a component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
are described in Lemma
3.22
, namely
N=K_{C}-(A+B_{b})
, where
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
uniquely determined whereas

B_{b}
is an effective divisor of degree
\mbox{deg}(B_{b})=b\geq 0
(which is the base locus of
|A+B_{b}|=g^{1}_{\nu+b}
). Thus, setting
D:=D_{s}
, so that
\mbox{deg}(D)=\mbox{deg}(D_{s})=s\geq 0
, we have that
\mathcal{F}
fits in an exact sequence of the form

(4.5)

0\to K_{C}-(A+B_{b})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-D_{s}\to 0\,.

Since we must have
h^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))=1
, because
\mathcal{F}
is of
second type
, we may take
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
general of its degree. Moreover, since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
the coboundary map

H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}))
arising from the cohomology sequence associated to (
4.5
) cannot be surjective. Thus, since assumptions as in Lemma
4.5
-(b) hold true, we deduce that
\mathcal{F}
fits also in the exact sequence

0\to K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}\to 0,

which implies the existence of canonical sections
\Gamma
on the ruled surface
F:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})
(corresponding to the surjection
\mathcal{F}\to\!\!\!\!\!\to K_{C}
) such that
\operatorname{dim}(|\Gamma|)=2
, as it follows from

3=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{0}(\mathcal{F}(-K_{C}+A+B_{b}+D_{s}))
(by Serre duality and determinantal reason) and by (
1.14
). Take
q\in F
a general point which projects, via the scroll map
F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}C
, to
p:=\rho(q)\in C
a general point. Denoting by
\mathcal{I}_{q/F}
the ideal sheaf of
q
in
F
, we may consider the sub-linear system

|\mathcal{I}_{q/F}(\Gamma)|\subset|\Gamma|
on
F
. Since the linear system
|\Gamma|
is base point free on
F
by Bertini’s Theorem, the cohomology sequence associated to
0\to\mathcal{I}_{q/F}(\Gamma)\to\mathcal{O}_{F}(\Gamma)\to\mathcal{O}_{q}\to 0
is surjectve at the
H^{0}
-level. This implies
h^{0}(\mathcal{I}_{q/F}(\Gamma))=h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{F}(\Gamma))-1=3-1=2
, namely
|I_{q/F}(\Gamma)|
is a linear pencil. From Proposition
1.7
-(i), this linear pencil certainly contains reducible unisecants;
for any such reducible unisecant, the section contained in it gives rise to an exact
sequence of the form

0\to K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-E_{f}\to 0\,,

for some effective divisor
E_{f}
of degree
\mbox{deg}(E_{f})=f
such that
p\in{\rm Supp}(E_{f})
. Notice that, since
D_{s}
is general and
3g-5\leq 4g-4-2\nu\leq d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-\nu-b-s
, we get
h^{0}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}))=g-\nu+1-b-s\geq 0
, which yields
h^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s})=2
. Since
p\in C
is general and
p\in{\rm Supp}(E_{f})
, we get therefore that
1\geq h^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})=h^{1}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f}))
. Thus, since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, from the previous exact sequence we deduce that necessarily
h^{1}(K_{C}-E_{f})\geq 2
. Hence
f=\mbox{deg}(E_{f})\geq\nu
. On the other hand, from the fact that
\mathcal{F}
has to be stable, of degree
d\geq 4g-4-2\nu
, we must have
4g-4-2\nu\leq d<4g-4-2f
, which implie
f<\nu
, contradicting the condition
f\geq\nu
deduced above. Therefore also case
(2-b)
cannot occur. To sum-up,
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)=\emptyset
also for
4g-4-2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-4-\nu
and the result is completely proved.
∎

\boxed{2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu:\;\mbox{obstructions and case exclusions}}
In view of what proved in Propositions
4.1
and
4.6
, from now on we can limit ourselves to study cases within the range

(4.6)

2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu.

Remark 4.7
.

Concerning values of
d
as in (
4.6
), one can split this integer interval in two integer sub-intervals. Indeed, under the condition
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{3}
one can consider
\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, where any possible irreducible component

\mathcal{B}\subseteq B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
has to be necessarily
superabundant
since for such values of
d
one always has
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=10g-18-3d<0
. When otherwise
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, one has
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}\geq 0
and, in principle, one can have both
superabundant
and
regular
components.
Therefore a main subdivision which will be used in our analysis is:

(4.7)

\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu,\;{\rm with}\;3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{3},\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6.

The next results present further obstructions for an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
to exist, hence they reduce the number of cases in Lemma
4.3
to be studied.

\boxed{\mbox{Obstructions: Case $(1-c)$ never a component}}
Concerning bundles of type
(1-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
, one has the following result.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Let
\mathcal{B}\subset B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
be an irreducible locus, whose general element
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
is assumed to be a stable, rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
of degree
d
, of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
and fitting in an exact sequence of the form

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-E\to 0,

where
E\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
for some integer
t
, whereas
N\in\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+t}(C)
. Then
\mathcal{B}
can not be an irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
.

In particular, case
(1-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
can never give rise to an irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
.

Proof.

Since
h^{1}(K_{C}-E)=h^{0}(E)=3=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})
, from the exact sequence in the statement, we deduce that either
N
has to be non-special or the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{u}:H^{0}(K_{C}-E)\to H^{1}(N)
has to surjective. From Lemma
4.2
,

\operatorname{dim}(\{K_{C}-E\})=\operatorname{dim}(\{E\})\leq\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})=\rho(g,2,t)=3t-2g-6,

in particular one must have
t\geq\frac{2g}{3}+2
for non-emptyness of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
. Stability of
\mathcal{F}
then implies that

d<4g-4-2t
. Thus
\mbox{deg}(2K_{C}-N-E)=6g-6-d-2t>6g-6-(4g-4-2t)-2t=2g-2
, which yields, by Riemann-Roch Theorem one gets
\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-E,N))=h^{0}(2K_{C}-N-E)=5g-5-d-2t
. No matter what the dimension of the family of possible line bundles
\{N\}
is, one certainly has
\operatorname{dim}(\{N\})\leq\operatorname{dim}({\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+t}(C))=g
, where the equality holds iff
N
is general of its degree.
Hence

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\leq g+(3t-2g-6)+(5g-6-d-2t)=4g-12-d+t.

In order for
\mathcal{B}
to be an irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
one must have also
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\geq\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=10g-3d-18
. Thus,

10g-3d-18\leq\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B})\leq 4g-12-d-t
should imply

(4g-12-d+t)-\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=2d+6-6g+t\geq 0.

which would give
d\geq 3g-3-\frac{t}{2}
. Thus, we would get

3g-3-\frac{t}{2}\leq d<4g-4-2t
. Comparing the number
4g-4-2t
with
3g-3-\frac{t}{2}
and recalling that
t\geq\frac{2g}{3}+2
, we have

(4g-4-2t)-(3g-3-\frac{t}{2})=g-1-\frac{3t}{2}\leq g-1-\frac{3}{2}(\frac{2}{3}g+2)=g-1-g-3=-4<0.

Hence we get a contradiction and
\mathcal{B}
can not give rise to a component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
.

The last part of the statement follows from the fact that case
(1-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
is related to the case
K_{C}-D
, where
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
.
∎

\boxed{\mbox{Obstructions: case exclusions for $d$ near the upper-bound in \eqref{eq:29dicnewbis}}}
In these cases one has some extra case exclusions, as proved in the following result.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. One has the following:

(i) For any
g\geq 9
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{3}
and

\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, there is no component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
arising from extensions as in cases
(1-b)
and
(2-a)
in Lemma
4.3
.

(ii) For any
g\geq 17
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g-5}{4}
and

\frac{7g-5}{2}\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, there is no component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
arising from extensions as in case
(2-c)
in Lemma
4.3
.

(iii) For any integers
0\leq s\leq\frac{g}{2}
,

3g-5\leq d\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5-s\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5
, there is no stable bundle
\mathcal{F}
arising from an extension as in case
(2-c)
in Lemma
4.3
of the form

(4.8)

0\to N:=K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-D_{s}\to 0,

where
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
is general whereas
t+b=4g-4-d-s
,
g^{1}_{t}
is a base point free linear system and
B_{b}\geq 0
(possibly
B_{b}=0
) is an effective divisor of base points, i.e.
g^{1}_{t+b}=|g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}|=g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}
(in other words

K_{C}-N=g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{4g-4-d-s}(C)
, as in Lemma
4.3
). In particular, there is no component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
is a bundle as in the previous description.

Proof.

(i) If by contradiction there existed an irreducible component
\mathcal{B}\subseteq B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
corresponded to a bundle
\mathcal{F}
as in case
(1-b)
of Lemma
4.3
,
then one would have
\delta\leq\frac{3g-6-\nu}{2}
; from the necessary condition of stability for
\mathcal{F}
one must have therefore
d<2\delta\leq 3g-6-\nu
, where the upper-bound is strictly less than
\frac{10}{3}g-5
, so we are out of the chosen range for
d
.

Concerning case
(2-a)
, from Lemma
4.3
one must have
d\leq 3g-3
which is once again strictly less than
\frac{10}{3}g-5
.

(ii) Recall that case
(2-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
can occur only for
d\leq\frac{7g-6}{2}
therefore one easily concludes.

(iii) Before starting the proof, we remark that the bound
d\geq 3g-5
we will see is required from the proof below, whereas
s\leq\frac{g}{2}
and
d\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5-s\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5
basically follow from the fact that we impose
g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
; indeed, from Lemma
4.3
, since

\mbox{deg}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})=t+b=4g-4-d-s
, a necessary condition for
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
to be not empty is
2(4g-4-d-s)-g-2\geq 0
, which gives
d\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5-s
. Since
s\geq 0
, this explains the upper-bound on
d
, moreover, since

d\geq 3g-5
, the
3g-5\leq d\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5-s
gives
s\leq\frac{g}{2}
. To accomplish the proof, we start with the following more general auxiliary result.

Claim 4.10
.

Let
C
be a smooth, irreducible, projective curve of genus
g\geq 4
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a rank-
2
vector bundle on
C
of degree
d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})\geq 3g-5
and of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
. Assume that
\mathcal{F}
fits in an exact sequence of the form

(u)\;\;\;0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-D_{s}\to 0

where:

(i)

h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=2
,

(iii)

D_{s}
is an effective divisor such that
h^{0}(D_{s})=1
,

(ii)

\mbox{deg}(N)\leq\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-D_{s})
.

Then either
{\rm Bs}(|K_{C}-N|)\neq\emptyset
or
h^{0}(N-D_{s})\geq h^{0}(N)-s+1
.

Proof of Claim
4.10
.

Notice that assumption (i), namely

h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=2
, and the fact that
d\geq 3g-5
imply that
h^{0}(N)\geq s
; indeed

2=h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=h^{1}(N)
gives by Riemann-Roch
h^{0}(N)=\mbox{deg}(N)-g+3
so, from the exact sequence
(u)
in which
\mathcal{F}
fits, we observe that
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2+s
, therefore
h^{0}(N)=d-3g+5+s
so
h^{0}(N)-s=d-3g+5\geq 0
, as it follows from the bounds on
d
.

Notice further that assumption (ii), namely
1=h^{0}(D_{s})=h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s})
, implies
s\leq g
and, by Riemann-Roch,
h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})=g-s\geq 0
. Finally, assumption (iii) is obviously equivalent to
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-N-D_{s})\geq 0
.

Since
\mathcal{F}
fits in the exact sequence
(u)
, with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
,
h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s})=h^{0}(D_{s})=1
and
h^{1}(N)=h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=2
, one deduces that the corresponding coboundary map
H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(N)
is the zero-map. From
[
12
, Remark 5.4]
, if we consider
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N)\simeq H^{1}(N+D_{s}-K_{C})
, then the map
\partial=\partial_{u}
is defined via the cup-product map

\cup:H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\otimes H^{1}(N+D_{s}-K_{C})\to H^{1}(N)

by
\partial_{u}(-)=-\;\cup\;u
. By Serre duality, the map
\cup
is related to the natural multiplication map

\mu:=\mu_{K_{C}-N,K_{C}-D_{s}}:H^{0}(K_{C}-N)\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\to H^{0}(2K_{C}-N-D_{s}).

The fact that
\partial_{u}=0
implies that
\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\mu))\leq\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N))-1
.

Assume therefore by contradiction that both
|K_{C}-N|
is base point free, i.e.
{\rm Bs}(|K_{C}-N|)=\emptyset
, and that
h^{0}(N-D_{s})=h^{0}(N)-s
. Then, from the base point free pencil trick, we have that

\operatorname{dim}({\rm ker}(\mu))=h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s}-(K_{C}-N))=h^{0}(N-D_{s})=h^{0}(N)-s
, so

\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\mu))=2(g-s)-(h^{0}(N)-s)=2(g-s)-(\mbox{deg}(N)-g+3-s)=3g-3-\mbox{deg}(N)-s.

On the other hand,

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N))=h^{1}(N-K_{C}+D_{s})=h^{0}(2K_{C}-N-D_{s})
which, from assumption (iii), is such that

\mbox{deg}(2K_{C}-N-D_{s})\geq\mbox{deg}(K_{C})=2g-2
; moreover
K_{C}-N-D_{s}
cannot be linearly equivalent to
0
since
h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=2
whereas
h^{0}(D_{s})=1
, so
K_{C}-N
and
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{s})
cannot be isomorphic. These arguments imply that
2K_{C}-N-D_{s}
is non-special, so by Riemann-Roch
\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s},N))=h^{0}(2K_{C}-N-D_{s})=3g-3-\mbox{deg}(N)-s
, which equals
\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\mu))
contradicting the previous inequality.

Therefore, either
{\rm Bs}(|K_{C}-N|)\neq\emptyset
or
h^{0}(N-D_{s})\geq h^{0}(N)-s+1
.
∎

Notice that, when
\mathcal{F}
is as in (
4.8
), one sees that assumptions as in Claim
4.10
hold true; indeed, since
N=K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})
,
it is clear that
h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=2
and, similarly, as
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
is general with
0\leq s=\mbox{deg}(D_{s})\leq\frac{g}{2}
, it is clear that
h^{0}(D_{s})=1
. Furthermore, since
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2+s
whereas

\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-N)=2g-2-s
, we notice that
\mbox{deg}(N)\leq\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-N)

reads also
d\leq 4g-4-2s
which holds true because, from the fact that
s\leq\frac{g}{2}
it certainly follows that
\frac{7}{2}g-5-s<4g-4-2s
. Notice further that, in such a case,
h^{0}(N)=\mbox{deg}(N)-g+3=d-3g+5+s
and, from the generality of
D_{s}
, we have
h^{0}(N-D_{s})=h^{0}(N)-s=d-3g+5\geq 0
. Thus, as a consequence of Claim
4.10
, we see that for
\mathcal{F}
as in (
4.8
) one must have
{\rm Bs}(|K_{C}-N|)\neq\emptyset
, namely
B_{b}>0
of degree
b=\mbox{deg}(B_{b})>0
. In particular, statement (iii) of Proposition
4.9
is then proved when
B_{b}=0
, namely in the case for which
K_{C}-N\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{4g-4-d-s}(C)
is general in the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
as in Lemma
3.22
.

Therefore, from now on we will focus on the case
B_{b}\neq 0
. Since
N=K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})
, with
B_{b}>0
, and
D_{s}
is general of its degree, in particular
B_{b}\cap D_{s}=\emptyset
, from Lemma
4.5
-(c) we know that
\mathcal{F}
as in (
4.8
) fits also in the exact sequence

(4.9)

0\to N(-D_{s})=K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}+D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}\to 0.

This suggests the following more general result, which will also be used in the proof of Claim
4.12
below.

Claim 4.11
.

Let
C
be any smooth, irreducible, projective curve of genus
g\geq 4
and let
g^{1}_{t}
be a base point free complete pencil on
C
.
Assume that a rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}
on
C
, with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, fits in an exact sequence of the form:

(4.10)

0\to K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\to 0,

where
B_{b}
is an effective divisor of degree
b
with
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})=2.
Then one has
s(\mathcal{F})\leq-t+b
.

Proof of Claim
4.11
.

Let
F
be the surface scroll
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})
and let
\widetilde{\Gamma}
be a canonical section of
F
, corresponding to the quotient line bundle
\omega_{C}
of
\mathcal{F}
as in (
4.10
). The exact sequence, combined with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
and determinantal reasons, gives therefore
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}(-K_{C}+g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}))=h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})=3
, hence
\operatorname{dim}|\mathcal{O}_{F}(\widetilde{\Gamma})|=2
by (
1.14
).

Take a general point
q\in F
, which therefore projects via the natural scroll map
F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}C
onto a general point
p\in C
. Then the linear pencil
|\mathcal{I}_{q/F}(\widetilde{\Gamma})|
of canonical sections passing through
q
contains, from Proposition
1.7
, a reducible unisecant
\Gamma
which contains the fiber
f_{p}
. Thus one sets
\Gamma=\Gamma_{D_{p}}+f_{D_{p}}
, where
D_{p}
is an effective divisor on
C
with
D_{p}\geq p
and
\Gamma_{D_{p}}
is a section on
F
. For determinantal reasons, such a section
\Gamma_{D_{p}}
is associated to the exact sequence:

(4.11)

0\to K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})+D_{p}\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-D_{p}\to 0.

Then we have
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}(-K_{C}+g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p}))\geq 1
which, by the fact that
\mathcal{F}\simeq\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\det(\mathcal{F})
, gives
h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}(-D_{p}))\geq 1
. From (
4.10
) dualized and then tensorized by
\omega_{C}(-D_{p})
, one gets

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(-D_{p})\to\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}(-D_{p})\to g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p}\to 0;

since
h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(-D_{p}))=0
and
h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}(-D_{p}))\geq 1
from above, the previous exact sequence gives
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p})\geq 1
. On the other hand, since
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})=2
and
D_{p}\geq p
with
p\in C
general, one more precisely has
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p})=1
. Dualizing (
4.11
) and tensoring it by
\omega_{C}
, one gets also

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{p})\to\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\to g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p}\to 0,

from which we deduce
h^{0}(D_{p})=2
, as it follows from the fact that the previous exact sequence together with
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p})=1
and
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})=3
imply
2\leq h^{0}(D_{p})\leq 3
, and the fact that
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})=2
and
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p})=1
imply
h^{0}(D_{p})\leq 2
. Conditions
h^{0}(D_{p})=2
and
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}-D_{p})=1
give that
|D_{p}|=g^{1}_{t}+B^{\prime}
, for some possible base locus
0\leq B^{\prime}\leq B_{b}
. Therefore the exact sequence (
4.11
) can be also written as:

0\to K_{C}-(B_{b}-B^{\prime})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-g^{1}_{t}-B^{\prime}\to 0,

from which we deduce
s(\mathcal{F})\leq-t+b
as in (
1.23
).
∎

Claim 4.12
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve, ad let
\mathcal{F}
be a rank-
2
vector bundle of degree
d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})\geq 3g-5
and of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
which is assumed to fit in an exact sequence of the form

(4.12)

0\to K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-D_{s}\to 0,

such that

(1)

g^{1}_{t}
is a base point free complete pencil of degree
t
;

(2)

B_{b}\in C^{(b)}
and
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
such that
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})=h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}+D_{s})=2
;

(3)

2t\geq t+b+s=4g-4-d
.

Then
\mathcal{F}
is not stable.

Proof of Claim
4.12
.

Set
B^{\prime}:=B_{b}\cap D_{s}
which can be empty, i.e.
0\leq B^{\prime}\leq B_{b}
. From the exact sequence (
4.12
), we get

(4.13)

0\to K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})+B^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime})\to K_{C}-D_{s}+B^{\prime}\to 0

Notice that assumption (2) implies
h^{0}(D_{s})=1
hence,
from
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
and from the exact sequence obtained by dualizing (
4.12
) and then tensoring by
\omega_{C}
, we get
H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})\oplus H^{0}(D_{s})
. This implies that any global section of
\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
vanishes along the locus
B^{\prime}=B_{b}\cap D_{s}
, i.e.
h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})=h^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}(-B^{\prime}))
which, by Serre duality, gives

3=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime}))
.

Since
\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime})
fits in (
4.13
),
g^{1}_{t}
is a base point free complete pencil on
C
and
0\leq B^{\prime}\leq B_{b}
, so
|g^{1}_{t}+(B_{b}-B^{\prime})|=g^{1}_{t}+(B_{b}-B^{\prime})
,
and finally
(D_{s}-B^{\prime})\cap(B_{b}-B^{\prime})=\emptyset
by the definition of
B^{\prime}
, we can apply Lemma
4.5
-(c) and deduce that
\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime})
as in (
4.13
) fits also in

(4.14)

0\to K_{C}-g^{1}_{t}-(B_{b}-B^{\prime})-(D_{s}-B^{\prime})\to\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime})\to K_{C}\to 0.

Finally, from assumption (2), we have
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+(B_{b}-B^{\prime})+(D_{s}-B^{\prime}))=h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}+D_{s})=2
. Thus, since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime}))=3
,
\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime})
fits in (
4.14
) and
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+(B_{b}-B^{\prime})+(D_{s}-B^{\prime}))=2
we see that
\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime})
satisfies assumptions as in Claim
4.11
therefore we deduce that

s(\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime}))\leq-t+(b-b^{\prime})+(s-b^{\prime})=-t+b+s-2b^{\prime}\leq-t+b+s.

Assumption (3) gives
0\geq-2t+t+b+s=-t+b+s
, from which we deduce that
\mathcal{F}(B^{\prime})
is not stable, so it is
\mathcal{F}
, as claimed.
∎

We can now complete the proof of (iii). Indeed, by assumptions,
\mathcal{F}
fits in (
4.8
) with
B_{b}>0
, as the case
B_{b}=0
has been already treated after the proof of Claim
4.10
. Notice that the assumptions
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
general,
K_{C}-N=g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{4g-4-d-s}(C)
as in Lemma
4.3
and
0\leq s\leq\frac{g}{2}
,

3g-5\leq d\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5-s\leq\frac{7}{2}g-5
,
t+b+s=4g-4-d
imply that assumptions as in Claim
4.12
are satisfied. Indeed, (1) is obviously satisfied by
g^{1}_{t}
; as for assumption (3), i.e.
2t\geq t+b+s=4g-4-d
, from
d\geq 3g-5
we have
4g-4-d\leq g+1
whereas from
g^{1}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{t}(C)
and
Lemma
4.3
we have that
2t-g-2\geq 0
, i.e.
2t\geq g+2
, therefore also assumption (3) holds true. Finally we have to check if assumption (2) is satisfied, i.e. we have to prove that
h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}+D_{s})=2
. By Riemann-Roch

h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}+D_{s})=(t+b+s)-g+1+h^{0}(K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})-D_{s})
. Since

h^{0}(K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}))=h^{1}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})=g+1-t-b
, the generality of
D_{s}
gives

h^{0}(K_{C}-(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b})-D_{s})={\rm max}\{0,g+1-t-b-s\}
and, from the fact that
(
4.12
) gives
d=4g-4-t-b-s\geq 3g-5
, we get
g+1-t-b-s\geq 0
. Therefore, from above

h^{0}(g^{1}_{t}+B_{b}+D_{s})=(t+b+s)-g+1+(g+1-t-b-s)=2
, i.e. also assumption (2) of Claim
4.12
holds true. Thus from Claim
4.12
we deduce that
\mathcal{F}
is not stable and the proof of (iii) is complete.
∎

\boxed{\mbox{Obstructions: case exclusions for $d$ near the lower-bound in \eqref{eq:29dicnewbis}}}
The following restrictions, holding for
d
near the lower-bound in (
4.7
), in particular imply further case exclusions (recall that, from Proposition
4.8
, case
(1-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
can never give rise to an irreducible component; this is the reason why it will not appear in the statement of the next result).

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
g\geq 7
,
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Assume that
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(3,d)\neq\emptyset
and let
\mathcal{B}
be any of its irreducible component, whose general point is denoted by
[\mathcal{F}]
