. Then one of the following cases must occur.

(1) If
\mathcal{F}
is of first type, with
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)
as in (
1.25
), then:

(1-a)

either
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
, where necessarily either
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
and
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
, or
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
and
\frac{g}{6}<\nu<\frac{g}{2}
. In particular, case
(1-a)
cannot occur for
\frac{g}{6}<\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and
4g-4-4\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
;

(1-b)

or
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
, where necessarily
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-7-\nu<\frac{10}{3}g-6
for any
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
. In particular, for
3g-6-\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
case
(1-b)
can never occur.

(2) If otherwise
\mathcal{F}
is of second type, then:

(2-a)

either
N
is general of its degree and either
g=7,8
, for any
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
and
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
or, when
g\geq 9
, necessarily
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-3<\frac{10}{3}g-6
, for any
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
. In particular, for
g\geq 9
and
3g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, case
(2-a)
cannot occur;

(2-b)

or
N
is such that
K_{C}-N\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{2g-2+\delta-d}(C)
and either
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, for any
g\geq 12
and
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
, or
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, for
7\leq g\leq 11
and
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{2}{3}g-2
. In particular, for
7\leq g\leq 11
,
\frac{2}{3}g-1\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, case
(2-b)
cannot occur;

(2-c)

or finally
N
is such that
K_{C}-N\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{2g-2+\delta-d}(C)
, for any
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
and
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
.

Proof.

From Theorem
1.14
, we know that
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general has to correspond to a bundle
\mathcal{F}
which is either of
first type
or of
second type
, arising as an extension (
1.25
) with
\mbox{deg}(D)=2g-2-\delta
.
The necessary numerical condition for the stability of such a
\mathcal{F}
gives in particular

d<2\delta
. We may therefore consider this condition together with what proved in Lemma
4.3
.

First we focus on the case of
\mathcal{F}
of
first type
. If
\mathcal{F}
is assumed to be as in case (1-a), where
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
and
\delta\leq 2g-2-2\nu
, one must have therefore
d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
. Notice that
4g-5-4\nu\geq 2g-2
for any
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
but
4g-5-4\nu\geq\frac{10}{3}g-6
iff
\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
. In other words for
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
, this case has to be studied for any
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
in the range on interest whereas, for
\frac{g}{6}<\nu<\frac{g}{2}
, one has that
4g-5-4\nu<\frac{10}{3}g-6
, therefore this case cannot occur for
4g-4-4\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
and it must be studied only for

2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
when
\frac{g}{6}<\nu<\frac{g}{2}
. Therefore, possibilities split as in the statement. If otherwise
\mathcal{F}
is assumed to be as in case (1-b), where
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,1}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2g-2-\delta}(C)
and
\delta\leqslant\frac{3g-6-\nu}{2}
, condition
d<2\delta
gives
d\leq 3g-7-\nu
. Notice that, for any
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
, one has

2g-2<3g-7-\nu<\frac{10}{3}g-6
. Therefore, this case has to be studied only for

2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-7-\nu
. Finally, from Proposition
4.8
, we already know that case
(1-c)
can never give rise to an irreducible component.

Assume now that
\mathcal{F}
is of
second type
. Case (2-a) in Lemma
4.3
can occur only for
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-3
, the latter being strictly less than
\frac{10}{3}g-6
when
g\geq 9
and for any
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
. If we assume that
\mathcal{F}
is as in case (2-b), we must have
d\leq 4g-4-\nu
. When
g\geq 12
, one has that
4g-4-\nu\geq\frac{10}{3}g-6
for any
3\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
; otherwise for
7\leq g\leq 11
one has that
4g-4-\nu\geq\frac{10}{3}g-6
iff
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{2}{3}g-2
, which is strictly less than
\frac{g}{2}
. Finally, when
\mathcal{F}
is assumed to be as in case (2-c), then
K_{C}-N\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{2g-2+\delta-d}(C)
, and this can occur for
any
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{7g-6}{2}
; one concludes by observing that
\frac{7g-6}{2}
is always strictly less that
\frac{10}{3}g-6
.
∎

\boxed{2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu:\;\mbox{Components from Case (1-a)}}
In the light of Propositions
4.9
-
(i)
and
4.13
-
(1)
, we know that
there is no irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point arises from a bundle of
first type
as in case
(1-b)
of Lemma
4.3

for
\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
and, respectively, for
3g-6-\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
. Similarly, for case
(1-c)
the same conclusion holds from Proposition
4.8
. Therefore, in the chosen range for
d
and among bundles of
first type
in the sense of Definition
1.15
, case
(1-a)
as in Lemma
4.3
are the only candidates to be bundles giving rise to possible irreducible components of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
filled-up by bundles of
first type
. For this reason, we concentrate here on case
(1-a)
and prove the following result.

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve
C
of genus
g
.

(i) If either
g\geq 9
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{3}
and
4g-4-4\nu\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
or
\frac{g}{6}<\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and
4g-4-4\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, there is no irreducible component
\mathcal{B}
of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
arises as a bundle as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
.

(ii) If
g\geq 18
, then for any integers
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
and
\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
, there exists an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(1-a)}
, which is uniruled, generically smooth and superabundant, being of dimension

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(1-a)}\right)=6g-6-4\nu-d>\rho_{d}^{k_{3}},

whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(1-a)}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degre
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which is of first type as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
, fitting in an exact sequence of the form

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-2A\to 0,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)

is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
, whereas
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
is general.

Furthermore, such component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(1-a)}
is the only
irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point arises from a bundle
\mathcal{F}
of first type on
C
.

(iii) If
g\geq 9
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{3}
and
2g-6+2\nu\leq d\leq{\rm min}\left\{4g-5-4\nu,\frac{10}{3}g-6\right\}
(more precisely, when
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
one has
\frac{10}{3}g-6\leq 4g-5-4\nu
so we consider
2g-6+2\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, when otherwise
\frac{g}{6}<\nu\leq\frac{g}{3}
we have
4g-5-4\nu<\frac{10}{3}g-6
, so we consider
2g-6+2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
), there exists an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(1-a)}
, which is uniruled, generically smooth and of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(B_{\rm 3,\,(1-a)})=6g-6-4\nu-d.

In particular when
d\geq 2g-5+2\nu
it is superabundant, i.e.
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(1-a)}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(1-a)}
, whereas it is regular, i.e.
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(1-a)}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,\,3,\,(1-a)}
, when otherwise
d=2g-6+2\nu
.

Furthermore, its general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in B_{\rm 3,\,(1-a)}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
, speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which is of first type as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
and fitting in an exact sequence of the form

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-2A\to 0,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
, wheras
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
is general and
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(1-a)}
is the only irreducible component whose general point arises from a bundle
\mathcal{F}
of first type.

(iv) If
g\geq 9
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{3}
and
2g-2\leq d\leq 2g-7+2\nu
, there is no component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which is of first type as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
.

Proof.

(i)
Recall that, from Remark
4.7
, we have
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{3}
; thus, when
4g-4-4\nu\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, one can easily concludes that
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
cannot contain any irreducible component whose general point corresponds to a bundle of
first type
as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
. Indeed, a necessary condition for such a case to give rise to stable bundles filling-up an irreducible component is
\delta=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-D))\leq 2g-2-2\nu
which, together with the necessary condition for stability, forces
d<2\delta\leq 4g-4-4\nu
which proves the statement. Instead, for what concerns
\frac{g}{6}<\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and
4g-4-4\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, this is the content of Proposition
4.13
-
(1)
.

(ii) Notice that
\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
forces
\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
as in the assumptions, which also explains the condition
g\geq 18
. Once again the necessary condition for case
(1-a)
to give rise to a component is
\delta=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-D))\leq 2g-2-2\nu
. To construct the irreducible component as described in the statement we will take into account
\delta=2g-2-2\nu
, i.e.
\delta
reaching its upper-bound.

Indeed, recall that case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
deals with
\mathcal{O}_{C}(D)\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{2\nu}(C)
, where the latter consists only of the bundle
A^{\otimes 2}
, namely
D=2A
, where
A
is the unique line bundle on
C
corresponding to the
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
on
C
. With notation as in (
1.22
), we have
L:=\omega_{C}(-2A)
, i.e.
\mbox{deg}(L)=\delta=2g-2-2\nu
. Since the bounds on
d
in
(ii)
ensure
d<4g-4-4\nu
, then the necessary condition for the stability of the bundles we want to construct, i.e.
2\delta-d>0
, is certainly satisfied.

Concerning the kernel line bundle
N
of the extensions we want to consider, from the bounds on
d
and from the choice of
\delta=2g-2-2\nu
, one must have
\mbox{deg}(N)\geq g
. We take
N
general of its degree, so it is effective and non-special. From (
1.22
), one has
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(\omega_{C}(-2A))=h^{0}(2A)=3
, as from Proposition
1.1
, and
h^{1}(N)=0
.

In order to construct stable bundles, from (
1.23
), a necessary condition is
2\delta-d=4g-4-4\nu-d>0
. If
2\delta-d=4g-4-4\nu-d\geq 2
, i.e. for any
\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-6-4\nu
, one has therefore
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-\delta\leq\delta-2=2g-4-2\nu
, i.e.
g\leq\mbox{deg}(N)\leq 2g-4-2\nu
, which is compatible with the given bounds on
\nu
as in the assumptions. Moreover, since
2\delta-d\geq 2
, we can apply Proposition
1.16
; from (
1.24
) one has
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N))=5g-5-4\nu-d
so, for any integer
\sigma\leq g
, one has

\operatorname{dim}\left({\rm Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(2g-4-4\nu-d+\sigma-2)}(X)\right)<m-1=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C}(-2A),N));

in other words, a bundle
\mathcal{F}
arising from a general such extension is certainly stable and of speciality
3
. If otherwise
2\delta-d=1
, i.e.
d=4g-5-4\nu
is odd, then

\mbox{deg}(N)=d-\delta=\delta-1=2g-3-2\nu
; in particular, from the necessary stability condition and the bounds on
d
, one has

g<\frac{d}{2}<\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-2A))=2g-2-2\nu\;\;{\rm and}\;\;g<\mbox{deg}(N)=\delta-1=2g-3-2\nu,

which are once again compatible with the given bounds on
\nu
, as in the assumptions. In such a case, one gets
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C}(-2A),N))=g
. Assume by contradiction that the general bundle
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C}(-2A),N)
were not stable, therefore there would exist a maximal sub-line bundle
\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{M}_{\rm max}\subset\mathcal{F}
such that
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})\geq\frac{d+1}{2}
, since
d
is odd. Because

\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})>\mbox{deg}(N)=d-\delta=\frac{d-1}{2}
, there is no line-bundle map from
\mathcal{M}
to
N
therefore, composing the inclusion
M\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
with the surjection
\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}(-2A)\to 0
gives a line-bundle map
\mathcal{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}L
which must be an isomorphism, since
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})\geq\frac{d+1}{2}=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-2A))
. Thus, we would have that
\omega_{C}(-2A)
is also a sub-line bundle of
\mathcal{F}
, i.e. the exact sequence (
1.22
) would split and
\mathcal{F}=\omega_{C}(-2A)\oplus N
, so it would correspond to the zero-vector of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C}(-2A),N)
. This contradicts the fact that
\mathcal{F}
was chosen to be general in this
g
-dimensional extension space. Thus
\mathcal{F}
is in any case stable.

We then argue as in Remark
3.14
: for any integers
\nu
and
d
as in
(ii)
, one has a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}
over a suitable open, dense subset
S\subseteq\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
, whose rank is
m=5g-5-4\nu-d
. We take the associated projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\to S
, consisting of the family of
\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C}(-2A),N)\right)
’s as
N
varies in
S
, to get

\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}))=g+m-1=6g-6-4\nu-d
. By the stability and the speciality of the bundle arising from the general pair
(N,[u])\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
, one has a natural (rational) modular map

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{d,\nu}}}{{\dashrightarrow}}

\displaystyle U_{C}(d)

\displaystyle(N,[u])\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{u}

such that
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})\subseteq B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
.

Claim 4.15
.

The map
\pi_{d,\nu}
is generically finite. In particular,
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is uniruled and

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu}))=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d\nu}))=6g-6-4\nu-d
.

Proof of Claim
4.15
.

The uniruledness of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
directly follows from the fact that it is dominated by
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
, which is ruled over
S
. Once we show that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is generically finite onto its image, we will get also the statement about the dimension of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
.

To prove that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is generically finite, we argue as in the proof of Claim
3.4
. Let
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
be general, so
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
(N,u)\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
general and

\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])=\left\{(N^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\,|\;\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}\right\}
. Let
(N^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])\setminus\{(N,[u])\}
.

Since
K_{C}-2A+N^{\prime}=\det(\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}})\simeq\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=K_{C}-2A+N
, then
N=N^{\prime}
. Thus
[u]\neq[u^{\prime}]
belong to the same
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,N))
; since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable (so simple), this implies the existence of an isomorphism
\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{F}_{u}
(which is not an endomorphism) giving rise to the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}&\to&L=K_{C}-2A&\to 0\\
&&&\downarrow^{\varphi}&&&\\
0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\to&L=K_{C}-2A&\to 0.\end{array}

The maps
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
determine, respectively, two non-zero global sections
s_{1}\neq s_{2}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})
.
These sections must be linearly dependent, otherwise one would have
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})>1
which is a contradiction; indeed, from (
1.14
), this would imply that the section
\Gamma\subset F_{u}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
corresponding to the quotient
L=K_{C}-2A
of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
would be movable in a linear system but, from the bound
d\geq\frac{10}{3}g-5
, one gets

\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-2A-N)\leq 4g-4-4\nu-\left(\frac{10g-15}{3}\right)=\frac{2}{3}g+1-4\nu<g-1,

thus the generality of
N
implies that also
K_{C}-2A-N
is general of its degree, therefore
h^{0}(K_{C}-2A-N)=0
and, by (
1.12
),
\Gamma
is therefore algebraically isolated (ai) (recall Definition
1.4
). On the other hand, if the two global sections
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
were linearly dependent,
[
12
, Lemma 4.5]
would give
[u]=[u^{\prime}]
, a contradiction. Previous arguments show that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is generically finite onto its image, as desired.
∎

Notice further that

(4.15)

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu}))-\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=(6g-6-d-4\nu)-(10g-18-3d)=2d+12-4g-4\nu,

where the latter quantity is certainly positive, from the bounds
d\geq\frac{10}{3}g-5
and
\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
. We complete the proof with the following:

Claim 4.16
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
general, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is such that

(4.16)

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}))=2d+12-4g-4\nu.

In particular, the closure in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\delta,\nu})
gives rise to an irreducible component
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
, which is generically smooth, uniruled, superabundant and such that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)})=6g-6-4\nu-d
.

Proof of Claim
4.16
.

The fact that
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
is uniruled and of the stated dimension follows from the fact that
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is a dense subset
in it and from Claim
4.15
. The superabundance follows from (
4.15
).

The fact that, the locus
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
is actually a generically smooth irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
will
follow once we show that
\operatorname{dim}(T_{[\mathcal{F}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)))=6g-6-d-4\nu,
where
T_{[\mathcal{F}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d))
is the Zariski tangent space
to
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
at the general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
, which is stable. From Remark
1.10
(in particular (
1.21
) and lines above this formula),
T_{[\mathcal{F}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d))
is isomorphic to the cokernel of the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
thus, from (
1.21
) and the line below this formula, one has

\operatorname{dim}(T_{[\mathcal{F}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)))=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}})).

From (
4.15
), we are therefore left to proving (
4.16
). To do this, recall that

\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
arises as an extension of the line bundles

N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
general and
L=K_{C}-2A
. In particular, since
h^{1}(N)=0
, then

H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\simeq H^{0}(N)\oplus H^{0}(K_{C}-2A)\;{\rm and}\;H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(2A).

Thus
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
reads also as

\begin{CD}\left[H^{0}(N)\oplus H^{0}(K_{C}-2A)\right]&\;\otimes\;&\;H^{0}(2A)&\;\;\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\;\;&H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}).\\
\end{CD}

Consider the exact diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N+2A&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes A^{\otimes 2}&\rightarrow&\omega_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\omega_{C}\otimes N&\rightarrow&\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{C}(2K_{C}-2A)&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\omega_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}&\rightarrow&2K_{C}-2A-N&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&\end{array}

obtained by tensoring the exact sequence

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-2A\to 0
, from which
\mathcal{F}
arises as extension, with the dual exact sequence tensored by
\omega_{C}
.
Since
N
is general with
h^{1}(N)=0
, so it is
N+2A
and thus
h^{1}(N+2A)=0
. In particular, from the above diagram one has

H^{0}(N+2A)\oplus H^{0}(\omega_{C})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes A^{\otimes 2})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}),

where the isomorphism follows from the first row whereas the inclusion comes from the middle column of the previous diagram. Considering therefore the natural multiplication map

\mu_{N,2A}:H^{0}(N)\otimes H^{0}(2A)\to H^{0}(N+2A)

and the Petri map of
A^{\otimes 2}
, namely

\mu_{2A}:H^{0}(K_{C}-2A)\otimes H^{0}(2A)\to H^{0}(K_{C}),

from above we have that
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}=\mu_{N,2A}\oplus\mu_{2A}
and we can consider the following exact diagram:

{\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{cclcccl}&0&&0&&0&\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0\to&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{N,2A})&\to&H^{0}(N)\otimes H^{0}(2A)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{N,2A}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(N+2A)&\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0\to&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}})&\to&[H^{0}(N)\oplus H^{0}(K_{C}-2A)]\otimes H^{0}(2A)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes A^{\otimes 2})&\subset H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
0\to&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{2A})&\to&H^{0}(K_{C}-2A)\otimes H^{0}(2A)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{2A}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&\\[3.44444pt]
&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[3.44444pt]
&0&&0&&0&\end{array}}

We consider first the multiplication map
\mu_{N,2A}
. To study
\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{N,2A})
we use the “
H^{0}
-Lemma”
[
18
, Theorem (4.e.1)]
where, comparing with notation therein, we consider
\mathcal{L}:=A^{\otimes 2}
,
W:=H^{0}(A^{\otimes 2})
and
\mathcal{M}:=N
. Since
|A^{\otimes 2}|
is base-point-free and, from Proposition
1.1
,
h^{0}(A^{\otimes 2})=3
then
\operatorname{dim}(W)-2=1
and
\mathcal{M}-\mathcal{L}=N-2A
. Notice that

\mbox{deg}(N-2A)\geq\frac{4}{3}g-3
, since

d\geq\frac{10}{3}g-5
; from the fact that
N
is general, it follows that
N-2A
is also general, whose degree is certainly greater than or equal to
g-2
. In particular
h^{1}(N-2A)\leq 1=\operatorname{dim}(W)-2
. Thus, by “
H^{0}
-Lemma”, the multiplication map
\mu_{N,2A}
is surjective, which implies

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{N,2A}))=h^{0}(2A)\,h^{0}(N)-h^{0}(N+2A)
. From
Proposition
1.1
one has
h^{0}(2A)=3
whereas, from the non-speciality of both
N
and
N+2A
, one gets

h^{0}(N)=d-3g+3+2\nu
and
h^{0}(N+2A)=d-3g+3+4\nu
. Thus,

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{N,2A}))=2(d-3g+3+\nu)
, which is positive since
d\geq\frac{10}{3}g-5
.

Concerning the Petri map
\mu_{2A}
, it is surjective since
W^{2}_{2\nu}(C)
is a reduced pont, so
\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{2A}))=3\,h^{1}(2A)-g=3(g+2-2\nu)-g=2g+6-6\nu
. Hence, plugging all the computations from above, we get

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}))=2(d-3g+3+\nu)+2(g+3-3\nu)=2d+12-4g-4\nu,

as desired.
∎

To complete the proof of
(ii)
we need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
described above is the only irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
arises from a bundle of
first type
, in the sense of Definition
1.15
. For bundles of type
(1-b)
and
(1-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
, this has already been proved in Proposition
4.9
-
(i)
. We need to show that, among bundles of type
(1-a)
, the previous construction is the only possibility to get a component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
. This will be proved in the following:

Claim 4.17
.

Let
g\geq 18
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
and
\frac{10}{3}g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve. Assume that
\mathcal{B}
is any irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
arising as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
. Then
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
as in Claim
4.16
.

Proof of Claim
4.17
.

Since, by assumption,
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general arises as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
, we have

K_{C}-L\cong A+B_{b}
, where
B_{b}\in C^{(b)}
is the base locus of
|A+B_{b}|
of degree
b\geq 0
.

By assumption,
\mathcal{F}
corresponds to a suitable
v\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A-B_{b},N_{b})
, for some
N_{b}\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu+b}(C)
. Moreover, always by assumption,
L=K_{C}-A-B_{b}
is such that
h^{1}(L)=h^{1}(K_{C}-A-B_{b})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
; therefore, by taking cohomology in

0\to N_{b}\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-A-B_{b}\to 0,

regardless the speciality of
N_{b}
, the corresponding
coboundary map
H^{0}(K_{C}-A-B_{b})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{v}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(N_{b})
has to be surjective. From semicontinuity on the (affine) space
{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A-B_{b},N_{b})
and the fact that semistability is an open condition inirreducible families, it follows that for a general
u\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A-B_{b},N_{b})
the coboundary map
\partial_{u}
is surjective too and that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is semistable, of speciality
3
.

Since
\mathcal{B}
is by assumption an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
and since

u
general specializes to the vector
v\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A-B_{b},N_{b})
, one has that

[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}
general has to come from a general
u\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A-B_{b},N_{b})
, for some
B_{b}\in C^{(b)}
and some
N_{b}\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+\nu+b}(C)
. On the other hand one observes that a general extension as

(*):\;\;\;0\to N_{b}\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-A-B_{b}\to 0
is a
flat specialization
of a general extension of the form

(**):\;\;\;0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-A\to 0
, where
N\cong N_{b}-B_{b}
; to observe this fact, it suffices to notice that extensions
(**)
are parametrized by the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,N)\cong H^{1}(N+A-K_{C})
whereas extensions
(*)
are parametrized by the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A-B_{b},N+B_{b})\cong H^{1}(N+2B_{b}+A-K_{C})
; the aforementioned existence of such a flat specialization is granted by the surjectivity

H^{1}(N+A-K_{C})\twoheadrightarrow H^{1}(N+2B_{b}+A-K_{C}),

which follows from the exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(2B_{b})\to\mathcal{O}_{2B_{b}}\to 0
tensored by
N+A-K_{C}
(cf.
[
31
, pp. 101-102]
for the use of
elementary transformations
of vector bundles to interpret the above surjectivity). Thus, from Claim
4.16
and the construction of
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
and from semicontinuity, general extensions
(**)
give rise to points in

\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
. By flat specialization of a general extension
(**)
to a general extension
(*)
, one can conclude that
\mathcal{B}\subseteq\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
, i.e. that
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup},3,(1-a)}
.
∎

The above arguments complete the proof of
(ii)
.

(iii) Here we consider case
(1-a)
for which, from Proposition
4.13
-
(1)
, we must have

2g-2\leq d\leq{\rm min}\left\{\frac{10}{3}g-6,\,4g-5-4\nu\right\}.

More precisely from the proof therein we recall that,
when
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g}{6}
, one must have
2g-2\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
, otherwise, when
\frac{g}{6}<\nu<\frac{g}{3}
, one must have
2g-2\leq d\leq 4g-5-4\nu
. As in the proof of
(ii)
, we are going to construct an irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
whose general point arises as an extension of the form

(u):\;\;0\to N\to\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-2A\to 0,

with
\delta:=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-2A)=2g-2-2\nu
, where
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
. Notice that, since
3=h^{0}(2A)=h^{1}(K_{C}-2A)
by Proposition
1.1
, one has in particular
h^{0}(K_{C}-2A)=g+2-2\nu>0
.

Claim 4.18
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve with
d
and
\nu
as in the above assumptions. Let
\mathcal{F}_{u}
be a rank-
2
vector bundle arising as a general extension
u\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,N)
, where
N
is a general line bundle in
{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
. Then
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable and such that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(K_{C}-2A)=3
.

Proof of Claim
4.18
.

Set
L:=K_{C}-2A
, so that
\mbox{deg}(L)=\delta=2g-2-2\nu
. Take
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
general; numerical assumptions on
d
and
\nu
give

2\delta-d>0
. Thus, for degree reason, one has
N\ncong L
so, from (
1.24
), one has
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A),N))=2\delta-d+g-1=(4g-4-4\nu-d)+g-1=5g-5-4\nu-d>0
. By construction,
\ell:=h^{0}(L)=h^{0}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=g+2-2\nu;
thus, as in Theorem
3.1
, one has
m\geq\ell+1
.

Now let us consider
r:=h^{1}(N)
, where
N
is general of its degree. If
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2+2\nu<g-1
, i.e.
d<3g-3-2\nu
, by the generality of
N
, one has
h^{0}(N)=0
and
r=h^{1}(N)=3g-3-2\nu-d>0
. Notice further that, from the fact that
d\geq 2g-2
, one always has
\ell=g+2-2\nu\geq r=3g-3-2\nu-d
. If otherwise
\mbox{deg}(N)=d-2g+2+2\nu\geq g-1
, i.e.
d\geq 3g-3-2\nu
, by generality of
N
one has
r=h^{1}(N)=0
so
\ell\geq r
certainly holds true in this case. In both cases, let

\mathcal{F}_{u}
be the bundle arising from a general
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(L,N)
, i.e. it fits into the exact sequence

\begin{CD}&&\;\;\;0\to&N&\to&\ \ \mathcal{F}_{u}\ \ &\to\ &\ \ L=K_{C}-2A\ \ &\to 0\\
&\mbox{deg}&&d-2g+2+2\nu&&d&&2g-2-2\nu&\\
&h^{0}&&&&&&g-2\nu+2&\\
&h^{1}&&\operatorname{max}\{0,3g-3-2\nu-d\}&&&&3&.\end{CD}

Previous computations and Theorem
3.1
show that the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{u}
is always surjective. Thus
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
.

To prove stability of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0},N)
general as above, we argue as e.g. in the proof of Proposition
4.14
. Indeed, since
2\delta-d:=4g-4-4\nu-d>0
, we have to consider two cases: if
2\delta-d\geq 2
, i.e.

d\leqslant 4g-6-4\nu
, we can apply Proposition
1.16
, with
X=\varphi(C)
the image in
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0},N))
via the usual morphism
\varphi
. From above one has
m=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0},N))=5g-5-4\nu-d
so, for any integer
\sigma\leq g
, one has

\operatorname{dim}\left({\rm Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}((4g-4-4\nu-d)+\sigma-2)}(X)\right)<m-1=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0},N)),

so
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable. If otherwise
2\delta-d=1
, i.e.
d=4g-5-4\nu
is odd, then
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0})=\frac{d+1}{2}
,
\mbox{deg}(N)=\frac{d-1}{2}
and, in such a case, one gets
m=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0},N))=g
. If we assume by contradiction that the general bundle
\mathcal{F}\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0},N)
were not stable, there would exist therefore a maximal sub-line bundle
\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{M}_{\rm max}\subset\mathcal{F}
such that
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})\geq\frac{d+1}{2}
, since
d
is odd. Because

\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})>\mbox{deg}(N)=\frac{d-1}{2}
, there is no line-bundle map from
\mathcal{M}
to
N
therefore, composing the inclusion morphism
M\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
with the surjection
\mathcal{F}\twoheadrightarrow K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
gives rise to a line-bundle map
\mathcal{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
which must be an isomorphism, since
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})\geq\frac{d+1}{2}=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0})
. Thus, we would have that
K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is also a sub-line bundle of
\mathcal{F}
, i.e. the exact sequence (
1.22
) would split and
\mathcal{F}=(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0})\oplus N
, so it would correspond to the zero-vector of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\;N)
. This contradicts the fact that
\mathcal{F}
was chosen to be general in this
g
-dimensional vector space. Hence in any case, the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable and the claim is proved.
∎

From the above arguments, one can therefore consider a vector bundle

\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}
on a suitable open, dense subset
S\subseteq\hbox{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu+b}(C)
, described by
N
varying in
S
, whose rank is

m=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,\,N))=5g-5-d-4\nu
. Taking the associated projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu,b})\to S
(consisting of the family of
\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,\;N)\right)
’s as
N
varies in
S
) one has
\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}))=g+(5g-6-d-4\nu)=6g-6-d-4\nu
. Notice that the latter quantity equals
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=10g-18-3d
only when
d=2g-6+2\nu
, whereas it is bigger than
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
for
d>2g-6+2\nu
. When otherwise
d<2g-6+2\nu
, the dimension of
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
is instead less than
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
(which in particular will also prove
(iv)
). Moreover, from Claim
4.18
, one has a natural (rational) modular map

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{d,\nu}}}{{\dashrightarrow}}

\displaystyle U_{C}(d)

\displaystyle(N,[u])\to

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{u};

s.t.
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})\subseteq B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
.

Claim 4.19
.

In the above assumptions, the map
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image. In particular,
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is uniruled and one has also that

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu}))=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu}))=6g-6-d-4\nu
.

Proof of Claim
4.19
.

Uniruledness of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
directly follows from the fact that it is dominated by
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
, which is ruled over
S
.
Once we show that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational, we will also get the statement about the dimension of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
.

To prove that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational we argue as in the proof of Claim
3.4
. Let
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
be general, so
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
(N,u)\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
general. Then

\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])=\left\{(N^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,b})\,|\;\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}\right\}
. Assume there is
(N^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])\setminus\{(N,[u])\}
; since the quotient
L=K_{C}-2A\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
is fixed, then also
N^{\prime}=N
, as
K_{C}-2A+N^{\prime}=\det(\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}})\simeq\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=K_{C}-2A+N
. Thus
[u]\neq[u^{\prime}]
belong to the same
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,\;N))
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable (so simple), this implies the existence of an isomorphism
\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{F}_{u}
(which is not an endomorphism) giving rise to the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}&\to&L=K_{C}-2A&\to 0\\
&&&\downarrow^{\varphi}&&&\\
0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\to&L=K_{C}-2A&\to 0.\end{array}

The maps
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
determine, respectively, two non-zero global sections
s_{1}\neq s_{2}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})
.

On the other hand, one has
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})=1
: indeed since the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is s.t.
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=N-2A+K_{C}
, one has
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}\otimes(K_{C}-2A)
. Serre duality gives

h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})=h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}\otimes(K_{C}-2A))=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes 2})
. Now, from the exact sequence
(u)
, one gets:

0\to N+2A\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes 2}\to K_{C}\to 0,

from which
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes 2}
corresponds to a general element in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+2A)
, as
\mathcal{F}_{u}
corresponds to a general element of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,N)
and the two extension vector spaces are clearly isomorphic. If
h^{1}(N+2A)=0
, the generality of
N
implies
\mbox{deg}(N+2A)\geq g-1
, then from above one obtains
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes 2})=1
. If otherwise
h^{1}(N+2A)\geq 1
then, combined with the generality of
N
, this should give
\mbox{deg}(N+2A)\leq g-2
, hence
d\leq 3g-4-4\nu.
We now check whether
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},\,N+2A))
,
\ell:=h^{0}(K_{C})
and
r:=H^{1}(N+2A)
satisfy the conditions in Theorem
3.1
. Notice that
r\geq 1
is already our assumption; from
d\leq 3g-4-4\nu
, one has that
m=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},\,N+2A))=5g-5-d-4\nu\geq h^{0}(K_{C})+1=\ell+1.
The hypothesis
d\geq 2g-6+2\nu
gives
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-2A-N)\leq 2g-6\nu+2
, whence
r=h^{1}(N+2A)=h^{0}(K_{C}-2A-N)<h^{0}(K_{C})=\ell
. Thus, Theorem
3.1
implies that the coboundary map
H^{0}(K_{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(N+2A)
is surjective, since
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes 2}
corresponds, as observed above, to a general element of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N+2A)
. This yields that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes 2})=h^{1}(K_{C})=1
, whence once again
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes N^{\vee})=1
as stated.

To sum-up, the above arguments force the two global sections
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
to be linearly dependent. According to
[
12
, Lemma 4.5]
one has that
[u]=[u^{\prime}]
, a contradiction. As a consequence,
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image, as desired.
∎

Let us denote by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
the closure of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
; from Claim
4.19
, one has

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)})=6g-6-d-4\nu,

since
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is dense in it. We want to find under which conditions
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
gives rise to an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
and, if so, determine when it is also generically smooth. To do so, by Claim
4.18
, we have
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=h^{1}(K_{C}-2A)=h^{0}(2A)=3
; consider the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N+2A-K_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes A^{\otimes 2}\otimes\omega^{\vee}_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&N\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{(}K_{C}-2A)\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}\otimes N^{\vee}&\rightarrow&(K_{C}-2A)\otimes N^{\vee}&0&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&\end{array}

which arises from the exact sequence
0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-2A\to 0
and from its dual sequence. If we tensor the column in the middle of the above diagram by
\omega_{C}
, we get
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes A^{\otimes 2})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})
. From the isomorphism
H^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(2A)
, the Petri map of
\mathcal{F}
reads

\mu_{\mathcal{F}}:H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(2A)\to H^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}).

Moreover, from the same column tensored by
\omega_{C}
, we get also the injection

(4.17)

H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes 2A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathfrak{h}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}H^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})

from which we deduce that the kernel of
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is the same as the kernel of the following multiplication map

H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(2A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F},\;2A}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes 2A).

We need therefore to investigate the connection between the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
and
the multiplication map
\mu_{\mathcal{F},\;2A}
. Let us therefore consider

[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
general, so that
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits in an exact sequence of the form

(4.18)

(u)\ \ \ 0\to N\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-2A\to 0,

in which
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g-2-2\nu}(C)
is general. Dualizing (
4.18
), one gets

(4.19)

0\to 2A\to K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}\to K_{C}-N\to 0,

whence one has the natural injection

H^{0}(2A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\boldsymbol{\iota}}}{{\hookrightarrow}}H^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})
which is indeed an isomorphism as

h^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{0}(2A)=3
. Taking into account the injection (
4.17
), in sum we obtain the following commutative diagram where, to ease notation, we have set
\mu^{\prime}:=\mu_{\mathcal{F},\;2A}
:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})&&\\[4.30554pt]
\uparrow{\rm Id}\otimes{\boldsymbol{\iota}}&&\uparrow\mathfrak{h}&\\[4.30554pt]
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(2A)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu^{\prime}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes 2A)&\end{array}

We notice that
{\rm Id}\otimes{\boldsymbol{\iota}}
is an isomorphism so, by the injectivity of
\mathfrak{h}
, it follows that

(4.20)

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}({\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}}))=\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu^{\prime})).

We focus on the computation of
\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu^{\prime})).
To do this, we establish the following more general result.

Claim 4.20
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve and let
\mathcal{F}_{u}
be a rank-
2
, degree
d
vector bundle arising as a general extension as in (
4.18
), where
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2+2\nu}(C)
is general. If
d\geq 2g-6+2\nu
, then for any integer
1\leq k\leq 2
the multiplication map

\mu_{k}:H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes(k-1)})\otimes H^{0}(A)\to H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes k})

is surjective.

Proof of Claim
4.20
.

To prove the claim, we consider
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes A^{\otimes s}
as an element of the extension space
{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(2-s)A,N+sA)
, for any integer
-1\leq s\leq 2
, and we use Theorem
3.1
. We first show the validity of the following inequality:

(4.21)

\ell:=h^{0}(K_{C}-(2-s)A)\geq r:=h^{1}(N+sA)\ \mbox{ for any}-1\leq s\leq 2.

We remark that, by assumption we have
3\nu\leq g
, whence
3A
is special and, due to
Proposition
1.1
, one has also
\operatorname{dim}(|tA|)=t
, for arbitrary integer
0\leq t\leq 3
. Thus, by Riemann-Roch theorem, one has

(4.22)

\ell=h^{0}(K_{C}-(2-s)A)=g-(2-s)\nu+2-s\geq 0,\ \mbox{ for any }-1\leq s\leq 2.

We now estimate
h^{1}(N+sA)
; since
d\geq 2g+2\nu-6
one has

\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-N-sA)\leq 2g-(4+s)\nu+2
, whence
h^{1}(N+sA)\leq{\rm max}\{g-(4+s)\nu+3,\,0\}
, as
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2\nu+2}(C)
is general.
This, combined with (
4.22
), establishes the validity of (
4.21
).

Observe further that, for any
-1\leq s\leq 2
,

{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(2-s)A,\;N+sA)\simeq{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,\;N)
so, from the proof of Claim
4.18
, we have that
\operatorname{dim}({\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(2-s)A,\;N+sA))=m=5g-5-4\nu-d
. Since from
(
4.21
) and (
4.22
) one has
\ell+1=g-(2-s)\nu+3-s
, one easily observes that also condition
m\geqslant\ell+1
as in Theorem
3.1
holds true, because of our bounds on
d
. Therefore, since we have already observed that the two extension spaces coincide and since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
was general in
{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-2A,\;N)
, then
\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes sA
is also general in
{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(2-s)A,N+sA)
so Theorem
3.1
establishes that the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{u}:H^{0}(K_{C}-(2-s)A)\to H^{1}(N+sA)

is surjective, for any
-1\leq s\leq 2
. This implies
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes sA)=h^{1}(K_{C}-(2-s)A)=3-s
for
-1\leq s\leq 2
, where the last equality holds by Proposition
1.1
combined with
3\nu\leq g
, as observed above. Hence, by the Riemann-Roch theorem, one has

(4.23)

h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes sA)=d+2s\nu-2g+(2-s)+3,\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;-1\leq s\leq 2.

Now we consider:

0\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(k-2)A)\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(k-1)A)\otimes H^{0}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{k}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes kA),\;\;\mbox{for any}\;\;1\leq k\leq 2,

derived from the exact sequence
0\to A^{\vee}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\otimes H^{0}(A)\to A\to 0
.

From the above sequence one deduces
\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{k})=H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(k-2)A)
so

(4.24)

h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes kA)\geq\operatorname{dim}({\rm Im}\mu_{k})=2h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(k-1)A)-h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(k-2)A).

Applying (
4.23
) to (
4.24
) one gets
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes kA)=\operatorname{dim}({\rm Im}\mu_{k})
which proves that the map
\mu_{k}
is surjective, for any
1\leq k\leq 2
, as desired.
∎

By using Claim
4.20
, we want to show that the map
\mu^{\prime}
is surjective. To do so, from Claim
4.20
we have that
\mu_{1}
is surjective, so the same occurs for the map

\mu_{1}\otimes{\rm Id}_{H^{0}(A)}:H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(A)^{\otimes 2}\twoheadrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes A)\otimes H^{0}(A).

Composing this latter map with
\mu_{2}
, which is also surjective by Claim
4.20
, one deduces the surjectivity of the composition
\widetilde{\mu}:=\mu_{2}\circ\left(\mu_{1}\otimes{\rm Id}_{H^{0}(A)}\right)
which is the following natural map

\tilde{\mu}:H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(A)^{\otimes 2}\twoheadrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes 2A).

Since the multiplication map
\mu_{2A}:H^{0}(A)^{\otimes 2}\to H^{0}(2A)
is also surjective, due to
\operatorname{dim}(|A^{\otimes 2}|)=2
by Proposition
1.1
, one gets the following commutative diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(A)^{\otimes 2}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Id}\otimes\mu_{2A}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(2A)&&\\[4.30554pt]
\downarrow\tilde{\mu}&&\downarrow\mu^{\prime}&\\[4.30554pt]
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes 2A)&=&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes 2A)&\end{array}

where both
\tilde{\mu}
and
{\rm Id}\otimes\mu_{2A}
are surjective from above. This implies that
\mu^{\prime}
is surjective, as desired. In particular, these computations imply the following:

Claim 4.21
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve and let
2g-6+2\nu\leq d\leq{\rm min}\left\{4g-5-4\nu,\frac{10}{3}g-6\right\}
be an integer. The locus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
constructed above is a reduced, irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
which is uniruled, generically smooth and of dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)})=6g-6-d-4\nu
.

Furthermore, if
d=2g-6+2\nu
,
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3,(1-a)}
, i.e. it is a regular component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(2,d)
; if otherwise
2g-5+2\nu\leq d\leq{\rm min}\left\{4g-5-4\nu,\frac{10}{3}g-6\right\}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,3,(1-a)}
is a superabundant component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(2,d)
.

Proof of Claim
4.21
.

\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
is obviously
uniruled
, since
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is dense in it and
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is dominated by
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
which is ruled over
S
. Moreover we have also

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)})=6g-6-d-4\nu
, as it follows from the fact that
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is dense in it and from Claim
4.19
.

Since, from Claim
4.20
, the map
\mu^{\prime}
is surjective then from (
4.20
) one deduces that

(4.25)

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}))=\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu^{\prime}))=h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})h^{0}(2A)-h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes 2A).

Thus,

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}T_{[\mathcal{F}_{u}]}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4g-3-h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\,h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})+\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4g-3-h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes 2A)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{by }\eqref{eq:Ker mu}\mbox{ and }h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{0}(2A)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4g-3-(d+4\nu-2g+3)\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \mbox{by }\eqref{eq;dim mA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}).

This shows that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
forms a component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(2,d)
which is also generically smooth. To complete the proof of Claim
4.21
, it suffices to observe that

\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}=10g-18-3d\leq 6g-6-4\nu-d

as
d\geq 2g-6+2\nu
, where the equality (and so the
regularity
) holds if and only if
d=2g-6+2\nu
.
∎

To conclude the proof of
(iii)
, we need to show that
\mathcal{B}_{3,(1-a)}
described above is the only irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
arises from a bundle as in the statement. The fact that bundles of type
(1-c)
as in Lemma
4.3
cannot fill-up a component for any
d
follows from Proposition
4.8
. Concerning bundles of type
(1-b)
, recall that from Proposition
4.13
-
(1)
such bundles cannot fill-up an
irreducible component when
3g-6-\nu\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
. Later on, precisley in Lemma
4.28
and in Proposition
4.32
, we will show that loci arising from bundles of type
(1-b)
as in Lemma
4.3
can never fill-up an irreducible component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
also for
2g-2\leq d\leq 3g-7-\nu
. We are therefore left to showing that, among bundles of first type on
C
, the previous construction of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
is the only possibility to get a component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
. As in
(ii)
we constructed
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,(1-a)}
filled-up by bundles as in case
(1-a)
of Lemma
4.3
, with the use of the fixed quotient line bundle
L=K_{C}-A
, i.e. for any chosen
d
fixing once and for all the integer
\delta:=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A)=2g-2-2\nu
as for the degree of the quotient line bundle used in presentations of such bundles. On the other hand, Lemma
4.3
-
(1-a)
more generally requires
\delta\leqslant 2g-2-2\nu
. Taking into account Lemma
4.2
-
(a)
, quotient line bundles
L
which could be used in principle to construct possible components via quotient presentations are therefore more generally of the form
L:=K_{C}-2A-B_{b}
, for some
b:=\mbox{deg}(B_{b})\geq 0
, where
B_{b}
an effective divisor of general points on
C
which is the base locus of a linear series of the form
|\mathcal{L}_{2,2}|=|2A|+B_{b}
, i.e.
\delta=\mbox{deg}(L)=2g-2-2\nu-b
as allowed by condition in Lemma
4.3
-
(1-a)
. Nevertheless, using the same strategy as in the proof of Claim
4.17
, one easily shows that the case
b>0
turns out to give rise to bundles which are specializations of those obtained in case with
b=0
. The above arguments conclude the proof of
(iii)
.

(iv) From computations as in
(iii)
, recall that when
d<2g-6+2\nu
the dimension of the projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d,\nu})
therein is smaller than
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
, namely the condition
d\geq 2g-6+2\nu
is necessary for the existence of an irreducible component. Therefore also
(iv)
is proved.
∎

\boxed{2g-7+2\nu\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu:\;\mbox{Components from modifications of Case (2-b)}}
Concerning bundles of
second type
, as in Definition
1.15
, recall that Propositions
4.9
and
4.13
show that there is no component of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
whose general point arises from a bundle as in case
(2-a)
of Lemma
4.3
for
3g-2\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
. Similarly, from Proposition
4.9
, the same conclusion holds for bundles as in case
(2-c)
when
\frac{7g-5}{2}\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
.

Therefore, in the chosen range for
d
, it is natural to consider case
(2-b)
in Lemma
4.3
as a possible candidate to conduct parametric constructions of irreducible components of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
filled-up by bundles of
second type
. However, in Proposition
4.22
-
(i)
below we will show that, for
3g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
, in order for a bundle
\mathcal{F}
of type
(2-b)
to give rise to a general point of an irreducible component, then the kernel line bundle
N
of a presentation of
\mathcal{F}
, which is s.t.
\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{\nu+b}(C)
as requested for bundle of type
(2-b)
, necessarily is s.t.
|\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}|
has a non-empty base locus (recall description of the elements in the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
as in Lemma
3.22
-
(i)
), which makes possible parametric constructions of components of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
more difficult to handle.

Nevertheless, we will also show that any such a bundle has also another presentation with no base locus, namely via the quotient line bundle
\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}
, where
A
the unique line bundle s.t.
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
(cf. the proof of Claim
4.23
), whereas the line bundle
\omega_{C}(-D)
, where
D
general, effective s.t.
h^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D))=1
, which is expected to be the quotient for a presentation of a bundle
\mathcal{F}
of type
(2-b)
, is instead given as the kernel line bundle of the presentation of
\mathcal{F}
via the aforementioned quotient
\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}
. Even if such a presentation is neither of
first type
nor of
second type
, in the sense of Definition
1.15
, it makes constructions of the desired components and the control of their Zariski tangent spaces much easier than using presentation as a bundle of type
(2-b)
(cf. the proof of Proposition
4.22
). Furthermore, as a nice extra result, such constructions extend also to lower values of
d
, namely for
2g-7+2\nu\leq d\leq 3g-6
(cf. the proofs of
(ii)
and
(iii)
in Proposition
4.22
) where, in such a range for
d
, it is not easy to prove that such bundles always come from a presentation as in case
(2-b)
of Lemma
4.3
(observe that in the proof of Claim
4.23
it is strongly used the assumption
d\geq 3g-5
)

In other words, Proposition
4.22
below will contain
parametric constructions
of irreducible components of
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
via suitable
modifications
of bundles of type
(2-b)
as in Lemma
4.3
,i.e. components of the form either
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}
or
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3,(2-b)-mod}
, in the sense of Definition
4.4
.

Proposition 4.22
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. One has the following situation:

(i) For any integers
g\geq 5
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+4}{3}
,
3g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
and
\nu+1\leq s\leq g-\nu+1
, there exists an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
, which is uniruled, generically smooth and superabundant, being of dimension

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}\right)=8g-11-2\nu-2d>\rho_{d}^{k_{3}},

whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which is obtained by a natural modification of bundles of second type as in case
(2-b)
of Lemma
4.3
. Indeed, such a bundle
\mathcal{F}
fits in an exact sequence of the form

0\to\omega_{C}(-D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}\to 0,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and where
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
is general, with
\nu+1\leq s\leq g-\nu+1
as above.

(ii) For any integers
g\geq 8
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+4}{4}
,
3g-3-\nu\leq d\leq 3g-6
and
g-\nu+2\leq s\leq g-1
, there exists an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
, which is uniruled, generically smooth and superabundant, being of dimension

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}\right)=8g-11-2\nu-2d>\rho_{d}^{k_{3}},

whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which is obtained by a natural modification of bundles of second type as in case
(2-b)
of Lemma
4.3
. Indeed, such a bundle
\mathcal{F}
fits in an exact sequence of the form

0\to\omega_{C}(-D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}\to 0,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and where
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
is general, where as above
g-\nu+2\leq s\leq g-1
.

(iii) For any
g\geq 9
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+3}{4}
and
2g-7+3\nu\leq d\leq 3g-4-\nu
there exists an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
, uniruled, generically smooth and superabundant, being of dimension

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}\right)=8g-11-2\nu-2d>\rho_{d}^{k_{3}},

whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which fits in an exact sequence of the form

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}\to 0\,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
whereas
N\in Pic^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
is general of its degree, so non-effective and special.

(iv) For any
g\geq 10
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+5}{5}
and
2g-7+2\nu\leq d\leq 2g-8+3\nu
there exists an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(2-b)-mod}
, which is uniruled, generically smooth and of dimension

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(2-b)-mod}\right)=8g-11-2\nu-2d\geq\rho_{d}^{k_{3}},

whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(2-b)-mod}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which fits in an exact sequence of the form

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}\to 0\,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
whereas
N\in Pic^{d-2g+2+\nu}(C)
is general of its degree, so non-effective and special. Moreover, the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(2-b)-mod}
is superabundant, i.e.
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(2-b)-mod}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
, if
2g-6+2\nu\leq d\leq 2g-8+3\nu
, whereas it is regular, i.e.
\mathcal{B}_{\rm 3,\,(2-b)-mod}=\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
, if
d=2g-7+2\nu
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a rank-
2
vector bundle of degree
d
as in case
(2-b)
of Lemma
4.3
, i.e., fitting in the exact sequence:

(4.26)

0\to K_{C}-(A+B_{b})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-D_{s}\to 0\,,

where
A
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
,
D_{s}
is a general effective divisor of degree
s
, so
h^{0}(D_{s})=1
, whereas
B_{b}
is an effective divisor of degree
b=\mbox{deg}(B_{b})=4g-4-\nu-s-d\geq 0

which is the base locus of the linear pencil
|A+B_{b}|=g^{1}_{\nu+b}
. Indeed we recall that,
from the proof of Lemma
4.3
-
(2-b)
, the kernel line bundle
N
for the presentation of
\mathcal{F}
as
second type
bundle of type
(2-b)
has to be such that
\omega_{C}\otimes N^{\vee}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{\nu+b}(C)
, for some
b\geq 0
, where the elements of the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
are described in Lemma
3.22
-
(i)
.

(i) Observe that, for
\mathcal{F}
a bundle fitting in (
4.26
), the necessary condition for stability (
1.23
) gives in particular
\nu+b>s
, which reads also
b>s-\nu\geq 1
, the latter inequality following from the assumption on
s
. Hence, in such cases, we must have
B_{b}\neq 0
. A first fundamental property is the following:

Claim 4.23
.

If
\mathcal{F}
is a bundle of degree
d\geq 3g-5
and of type
(2-b)
as in Lemma
4.3
, i.e. fitting in (
4.26
), then
\mathcal{F}
fits also in:

(4.27)

0\ \to\ K_{C}-(B_{b}+D_{s})\ \to\ \mathcal{F}\ \to\ K_{C}-A\ \to\ 0.

proof of Claim
4.23
.

Since the bundle
\mathcal{F}
fits in (
4.26
) then we have
d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-\nu-b-s
and, by Lemma
4.5
-(b),
\mathcal{F}
also fits in

(4.28)

0\to K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}\to 0\,.

Let
\Gamma
be a canonical section of the surface scroll
F:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})
, corresponding to the quotient line bundle
\mathcal{F}\to\!\!\!\!\!\to K_{C}
. Then
\operatorname{dim}(|\Gamma|)=2
by (
1.14
) and
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}-(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s})))=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
. For a general point
q\in F
, which projects via the scroll map
F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}C
onto a general point
p:=\rho(q)\in C
, the dimension of the family of unisecant (canonical) curves passing through
q\in F
is
1
so, similarly as in the proof of Proposition
4.6
,
|\Gamma|
contains
reducible unisecants. The section contained in such a reducible unisecant curve corresponds to a quotient line bundle of
\mathcal{F}
which is of the form
K_{C}-E_{f}
, where
E_{f}
is an effective divisor containing the point
p=\rho(q)\in C
. For determinantal reasons, we therefore have that

\mathcal{F}
fits also in the exact sequence

0\to K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-E_{f}\to 0.

From the assumption
3g-5\leq d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-\nu-b-s
, it follows that
s=\mbox{deg}(D_{s})\leq g+1-\nu-b
. Thus, since
D_{s}
is general of its degree and since
h^{0}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}))=h^{1}(A+B_{b})=g+1-\nu-b
, by Serre duality one has
h^{1}(A+B_{b}+D_{s})=h^{0}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}))=g+1-\nu-b-s
so, by Riemann-Roch,

h^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s})=2
. Since
p\in C
is general such that
p\in E_{f}
, we have therefore
h^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})\leq 1
.

Assume by contradiction that
h^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})=0
; in such a case the corresponding coboundary map

H^{0}(K_{C}-E_{f})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f}))
would be surjective; this would imply that the dual map

H^{1}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f}))^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial^{\vee}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}(K_{C}-E_{f})^{\vee}
is injective. Since

H^{1}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f}))^{\vee}\simeq H^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})\simeq{\rm Hom}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}),K_{C}-E_{f})

and

H^{0}(K_{C}-E_{f})^{\vee}\simeq H^{1}(E_{f})\simeq{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}),\,K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})),

we would therefore deduce that
\mathcal{F}
does not admit
K_{C}-(A+B_{b}+D_{s})
as a sub-line bundle, namely that
\mathcal{F}
would not fit in (
4.28
) contradicting Lemma
4.5
. Therefore, we must have that
h^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s}-E_{f})=1
and that
A-E_{f}
effective. By the way, since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
,
h^{1}(K_{C}-E_{f})\geq 2
, hence
f:=\mbox{deg}(E_{f})\geq\nu
which forces that
\mathcal{O}_{C}(E_{f})=A
. Thus
\mathcal{F}
fits in (
4.27
), as stated.
∎

From Claim
4.23
and when
d\geq 3g-5
, we may therefore consider that any bundle
\mathcal{F}
of type
(2-b)
as in Lemma
4.3
can be also realized as
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
, with
u\in{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,\,K_{C}-(B_{b}+D_{s}))
. However,
as we saw at the beginnig of the proof, in order to hope to construct stable bundles from case
(2-b)
, necessarily we must have
B_{b}\neq 0
, where the presence of non-trivial base loci makes more complicated constructions.

Nevertheless, we can be inspired by what proved in Claim
4.23
; indeed in the next result we will show that presentations of the form (
4.26
), with instead
b=0
, actually give rise to irreducible components of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
as desired.

Claim 4.24
.

Let
g\geq 5
,
3\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+4}{3}
,
3g-5\leq d\leq 4g-5-2\nu
and
\nu+1\leq s\leq g-\nu+1
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve. Then there exists an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
, which is uniruled, generically smooth and superabundant, being of dimension

(4.29)

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}\right)=8g-11-2\nu-2d>\rho_{d}^{k_{3}},

whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm sup,\,3,\,(2-b)-mod}
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, fitting in an exact sequence of the form

0\to\omega_{C}(-D_{s})\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}\to 0\,

where
A\in{\rm Pic}^{\nu}(C)
is the unique line bundle on
C
such that
|A|=g^{1}_{\nu}
and where
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
is general, where as above
\nu+1\leq s\leq g-\nu+1
.

Proof of Claim
4.24
.

Set
L:=\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}
and
N:=\omega_{C}(-D_{s})
, with
s
any integer as in the statement. Therefore

d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-s-\nu
. Then
m:=\operatorname{dim}({\rm Ext}^{1}(L,N))=h^{1}(N-L)=h^{0}(K_{C}-A+D_{s})=2g-2-\nu+s-g+1=g-1+s-\nu
since
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A+D_{s})=2g-2+s-\nu>2g-2
. Moreover
\ell:=h^{0}(L)=2g-2-\nu-g+3=g+1-\nu>1=h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{s}):=r
. We see further that
m=g-1+s-\nu\geq\ell+1=h^{0}(L)+1=g+2-\nu
iff
s\geq 3
. Thus, from Theorem
3.1
,

\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset{\rm Ext}^{1}(L,N)
is irreducible, of
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=g-1+s-\nu-(g+1-\nu)=s-2
. Posing

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{1})
, we have therefore

\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1})=4g-7-d-\nu=s-3
by the definition of
d
.

Since
h^{1}(N)=h^{0}(D_{s})=1
, then
u\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
if and only if the corresponding coboundary map
H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{u}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(\omega_{C}(-D_{s}))
is the zero-map. Therefore, similarly as in (
3.3
),
\mathcal{W}_{1}
is a vector space (being the cokernel of a natural multiplication map) so
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
is a linear space inside
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}({\rm Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee},\;\omega_{C}(-D_{s})))
. Since
D_{s}
varies in a suitable open, dense subset
S\subseteq C^{(s)}
, whereas
L=\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee}
is uniquely determined, and since each corresponding linear space
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
is of (constant) dimension
4g-7-d-\nu
, we get an irreducible scheme, denoted by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, which is ruled over
S
by the linear spaces
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
’s as
D_{s}
varies; it turns out that
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=\operatorname{dim}(C^{(s)})+\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1})=2s-3=8g-11-2d-2\nu
, where the latter equality follows from
d=4g-4-s-\nu
. Similarly, we get
\rho^{k_{3}}_{d}=10g-3d-18=10g-3(4g-4-s-\nu)-18=-2g-6+3s+3\nu
so we notice that

(4.30)

\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})-\rho^{k_{3}}_{d}=(2s-3)-(-2g-6+3s+3\nu)=2g+3-s-3\nu=d-2g-2\nu+7>0,

as it follows from the assumptions on
d
.

We now show that the general bundle arising from a general couple
(s,[u])\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
is stable. Taking
\delta:=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}\otimes A^{\vee})=2g-2-\nu
, by assumptions on
d
we have that
2\delta-d>0
iff
\nu<s
, which is certainly satisfied by our assumptions on
s
.
Notice that
2\delta-d=2(2g-2-\nu)-(4g-4-s-\nu)=s-\nu
. Therefore if
s\geq\nu+2
,
i.e.
2\delta-d\geq 2
, we can apply Proposition
1.16
. We let
\sigma=1({\rm or}\;2)\equiv 2\delta-d=s-\nu{\mbox{ (mod 2) }}
; then
\sigma\leq 2\delta-d
because
2\delta-d\geq 2
; moreover
\sigma\geq 4+d-2\delta=4+\nu-s
since
\nu+2\leq s
. Therefore,

\sigma=1
means that
1\geq 4+\nu-s
, hence
s\geq\nu+3
(being
s
and
\nu
with different parity); if otherwise
\sigma=2
, this means that
2\geq 4+\nu-s
, hence
s\geq\nu+2
which is in our assumptions. If we set
X:=\varphi_{|K_{C}+L-N|}(C)
we can apply Proposition
1.16
: if
\sigma=1
, then
2\delta-d=s-\nu
is odd hence
Sec_{\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu+1-2)}(X)=Sec_{\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu-1)}(X)
so

\operatorname{dim}\left(Sec_{\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu-1)}(X)\right)=2\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu-1)-1=s-\nu-2<\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1})=s-3;

if otherwise
\sigma=2
, then
2\delta-d=s-\nu
is even hence
Sec_{\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu+2-2)}(X)=Sec_{\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu)}(X)
so

\operatorname{dim}\left(Sec_{\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu)}(X)\right)=2\frac{1}{2}(s-\nu)-1=s-\nu-1<\operatorname{dim}<\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1})=s-3.

Instead, if
2\delta-d=1
, i.e.
d=4g-5-2\nu
is odd, then
\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A)=\frac{d+1}{2}
and
\mbox{deg}(N)=\frac{d-1}{2}
. If we assume by contradiction that the general bundle
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
were not stable, there would exist a maximal sub-line bundle
\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{M}_{\rm max}\subset\mathcal{F}

such that
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})\geq\frac{d+1}{2}
, since
d
is odd. Because

\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})>\mbox{deg}(N)=\frac{d-1}{2}
, there is no line-bundle map from
\mathcal{M}
to
N
therefore,
composing the inclusion morphism
M\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}
with the surjection
\mathcal{F}\twoheadrightarrow K_{C}-A
gives rise to a line-bundle map
\mathcal{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}K_{C}-A
which must be an isomorphism,
since
\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{M})\geq\frac{d+1}{2}=\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-A)
. Thus, we would have that
K_{C}-A
is also a sub-line
bundle of
\mathcal{F}
, i.e. the exact sequence (
1.22
) would split and
\mathcal{F}=(K_{C}-A)\oplus N
, so it would correspond to the zero-vector of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,\;N)
. This contradicts the fact that
\mathcal{F}
was chosen to be
general in
\mathcal{W}_{1}
with
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})>0
. Therefore, in all cases the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
arising from a general couple
(s,[u])\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
is stable, as claimed.

Thus
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
is endowed with a natural (rational) modular map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi_{d,\nu}}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U^{s}_{C}(d)\\
(s,[u])&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{u}]\end{array}

for which
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})\subseteq B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
.

Our next task is to show that, for any integer
s
as in the statement, the map
\pi_{d,\nu}
is
birational
onto its image. If so, we will get in particular that
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is
uniruled
of

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu}))=\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=8g-11-2d-2\nu>\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
. Indeed, the uniruledness of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
directly follows from the fact that it is dominated by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, which is ruled over
S
. Once we show that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational, we will also get the statement about the dimension of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
. To prove that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image, we argue as in the proof of Claim
4.15
; let
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
be general i.e. it arises from a general couple
(s,[u])\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
. Then, as usual,
\pi_{d,\nu}^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])=

\left\{(s^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}\,|\;\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}\right\}
. Assume by contradiction
(s^{\prime},[u^{\prime}])\in\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])\setminus\{(s,[u])\}
; by determinantal reason, we have
D_{s^{\prime}}=D_{s}
, i.e.
s=s^{\prime}\in S
; then
[u]\neq[u^{\prime}]
belong to the same
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}\subset\mathbb{P}
. Since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable (so simple), this implies the existence of an isomorphism
\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}{\to}\mathcal{F}_{u}
(which is not an endomorphism) giving rise to the following commutative diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}0\to&K_{C}-D_{s}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}&\to&K_{C}-A&\to 0\\
&&&\downarrow^{\varphi}&&&\\
0\to&K_{C}-D_{s}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\to&K_{C}-A&\to 0.\end{array}

The maps
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
determine, respectively, two non-zero global sections
s_{1}\neq s_{2}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(K_{C}-D_{s})^{\vee})
.
These sections must be linearly dependent, otherwise one would have
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes(K_{C}-D_{s})^{\vee})>1
which is a contradiction; indeed, from (
1.14
), this would imply that the section
\Gamma\subset F_{v}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{v})
corresponding to the quotient
K_{C}-A
of
\mathcal{F}_{v}
would be movable in a linear system, but this is not the case because
K_{C}-A-(K_{C}-D_{s})=D_{s}-A
so
h^{0}(K_{C}-A-(K_{C}-D_{s}))=h^{0}(D_{s}-A)=0
. Thus, by (
1.12
),
\Gamma
is algebraically isolated (ai) (recall Definition
1.4
). On the other hand, if the two global sections
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
were linearly dependent,
[
12
, Lemma 4.5]
would give
[u]=[u^{\prime}]
, a contradiction. These arguments show that
\pi_{d,\nu}
is birational onto its image, as desired.

Denoting by
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}
the closure of
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
, we get that
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}
is irreducible (because
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
is), of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}})=\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot})=8g-11-2d-2\nu,

as it follows from birationality of
\pi_{d,\nu}
and from the dimension of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
; moreover
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}

is
superabundant
, from (
4.30
), and also
uniruled
, as
\mathrm{im}(\pi_{d,\nu})
is uniruled, being finitely dominated by
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
which is ruled over
S
, and dense in
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}
. To conclude that
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}
gives rise to an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
which is also
generically smooth
will follow once we show that
\operatorname{dim}(T_{[\mathcal{F}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)))=8g-11-2d-2\nu
.
From Remark
1.10
, this tangent space is isomorphic to the cokernel of the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
so

\operatorname{dim}(T_{[\mathcal{F}]}(B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)))=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}})).

From (
4.30
), we are reduced to proving that for
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi_{s,\nu})
general, the associated Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is such that

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}))=d-2g+7-2\nu.

To do this, recall that
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}
arises as a rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for some
u
is some fiber
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
, i.e.
[u]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
for
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
general, which in turn was equivalent to the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{u}
to be the zero-map. Thus, we have

H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}-A)\oplus H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\;{\rm and}\;H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(A)\oplus H^{0}(D_{s}).

In particular, for any such bundle
\mathcal{F}
, the domain of its Petri
map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
has constant dimension
3(d-2g+5)
, which is the same as the domain of the Petri map
\mu_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}
, with
\mathcal{F}_{0}:=(K_{C}-D_{s})\oplus(K_{C}-A)
the splitting bundle corresponding to the zero vector in
\mathcal{W}_{1}
. In other words

H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}^{\vee}),\;\;\forall\;\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{W}_{1}.

We will first study the multiplication map
\mu_{{\mathcal{F}}_{0}}
. Observe that

\begin{array}[]{ccl}H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{0})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}^{\vee})&\cong&\left(H^{0}(K_{C}-A)\otimes H^{0}(A)\right)\oplus\left(H^{0}(K_{C}-A)\otimes H^{0}(D_{s})\right)\oplus\\
&&\oplus\left(H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\otimes H^{0}(A)\right)\oplus\left(H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\otimes H^{0}(D_{s})\right).\end{array}

Moreover
\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{0}^{\vee}\simeq K_{C}\oplus(K_{C}+A-D_{s})\oplus(K_{C}+D_{s}-A))\oplus K_{C}
. Therefore, for Chern classes reason,

\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}=\mu_{D_{s}}\oplus\mu_{K_{C}-D_{s},\,A}\oplus\mu_{K_{C}-A,\;D_{s}}\oplus\mu_{A}
, where the maps

\displaystyle\mu_{K_{C}-D_{2},A}:H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\otimes H^{0}(A)\to H^{0}(K_{C}+A-D_{s})

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\mu_{D_{s}}:H^{0}(D_{s})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{s})\to H^{0}(K_{C})

\displaystyle\mu_{K_{C}-A,\;D_{s}}:H^{0}(K_{C}-A)\otimes H^{0}(D_{s})\to H^{0}(K_{C}+D_{s}-A)

\displaystyle{\rm and}

\displaystyle\mu_{A}:H^{0}(A)\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-A)\to H^{0}(K_{C})

are natural Petri/multiplication maps of line bundles. Since
h^{0}(D_{s})=1
, the maps
\mu_{D_{s}}
and
\mu_{K_{C}-A,D_{s}}
are obviously both injective. Therefore,

\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{0}})\simeq\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{K_{C}-D_{s},A})\oplus\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{A}).

Since
A
is base point free, by the base-point-free pencil trick we get

\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{K_{C}-D_{s},A})\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}-A-D_{s})\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{A})\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}-2A),

therefore
\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}))=h^{0}(K_{C}-A-D_{s})+h^{0}(K_{C}-2A)
. Notice that, by the assumptions,
\nu+s\leq g+1
and
h^{0}(K_{C}-A)=g+1-\nu
thus for a general
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
we have

h^{0}(K_{C}-A-D_{s})=(g+1-\nu)-\mbox{deg}(D_{s})=g+1-\nu-s\geq 0
,
whereas
h^{0}(K_{C}-2A)=h^{1}(2A)=-(2\nu-g+1)+3=g+2-2\nu
. Therefore,
\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{0}}))=2g+3-3\nu-s=d-2g+7-2\nu
which completes the proof of Claim
4.24
.
∎

The above arguments complete the proof of (i).

(ii) In this case we have that
s=\mbox{deg}(D_{s})
is s.t.
g-\nu+2\leq s\leq g-1
. Therefore, one cannot apply the same strategy as in the proof of Claim
4.23
to prove that any bundle of type
(2-b)
as in Lemma
4.3
always admits also a presentation as in (
4.27
) (recall that in the proof of Claim
4.23
, it was fundamental that
h^{0}(A+B_{b}+D_{s})=2=h^{0}(A+B_{b})
and this in turn was a consequence of the fact that
\nu+1\leq s\leq g-\nu+1
). Nevertheless, taking inspiration from construction and computations as in Claim
4.24
, we always consider here vector bundles
\mathcal{F}
arising from extensions of the form

(4.31)

0\to K_{C}-D_{s}\to\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-A\to 0,

where
u\in\mathcal{W}_{1}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-A,K_{C}-D_{s})
and where
D_{s}\in C^{(s)}
general, for
g-\nu+2\leq s\leq g-1
, even if they are neither of
first type
nor of
second type
. This is done because the construction of
\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}
, the estimate of its dimension, the proof that it is superabundant and uniruled can be done identically as in
(i)
. The only difference in the proof step
(ii)
resides in the computation of the kernel of the Petri map, namely how to prove that
\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}))=d-2g+7-2\nu
.

By the very construction as in
(i)
,
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{{\rm sup,3,(2-b)-mod}}
general arises as a rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for a general point
[u]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
in a general fiber over
S
of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{Tot}
. This, in turn was equivalent to the fact that the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{u}
is the zero-map. Thus, we have
H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(A)\oplus H^{0}(D_{s})
. Tensoring by
H^{0}(\mathcal{F})
, this fits in the following diagram of natural multiplication maps:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes A^{\vee})&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(A)&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F},\,A}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes A)&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}})&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}}}}{{\rightarrow}}&H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee})&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{D_{s}})&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F})\otimes H^{0}(D_{s})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{D_{s}}}}{{\rightarrow}}&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes D_{s})&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&0&&&&\end{array}

Since
h^{0}(D_{s})=1
, the map
\mu_{D_{S}}
is obviously injective. Hence we get

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}}))=\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F},\,A}))=h^{0}(\mathcal{F}\otimes A^{\vee})
