\mathbb{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pr_{1}}}{{\longleftarrow}}\mathcal{J}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pr_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P},

where
pr_{1}
is dominant. For
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}
general, one has
pr_{1}^{-1}([V_{2}])=\left\{\pi\in\mathbb{P}\,|\,{\rm im}(\mu_{V_{2}})\subseteq\pi\right\}
, i.e. this fiber is isomorphic to the linear system of hyperplanes in
\mathbb{P}
passing through the linear subspace
\mathbb{P}({\rm im}(\mu_{V_{2}}))
. Since

\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P})=m-1
and
\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{P}({\rm im}(\mu_{V_{2}})))=2\ell-1
then,
for
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}
general,
pr_{1}^{-1}([V_{2}])
is irreducible, of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(pr_{1}^{-1}([V_{2}]))=m-1-2\ell=3g-6-d>0
. In particular,
\mathcal{J}
is irreducible, of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{J})=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{G})+\operatorname{dim}(pr_{1}^{-1}(V_{2}))=(6g-10-2d)+(3g-6-d)=9g-16-3d.

Set
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}:=pr_{2}(\mathcal{J})
; by construction, one has
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{2})
, moreover
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
is irreducible and, for
[v]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
general, one has
{\rm coker}(\partial_{v})^{\vee}\simeq V_{2}
where
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}
general, so
{\rm corank}(\partial_{v})=2
.
Notice that

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{J})={\rm expdim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}_{2}})=m-1-c(2)=m-1-2(\ell-r+2),

where
c(2)=2(\ell-r+2)
is the
expected codimension
of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
as in (
3.2
). Thus
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})\leq\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{J})
, where the equality holds iff
pr_{2}
is generically finite.

On the other hand, since
\mathcal{J}
is irreducible and dominating
\mathbb{G}
, then
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
cannot be a proper, closed subscheme of an irreducible component, say
W
, of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}_{2}}
, otherwise by semicontinuity an element
w\in W\setminus\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
would give
[{\rm coker}(\partial_{w})^{\vee}]\in\mathbb{G}
, a contradiction. Thus,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
is an irreducible component of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}_{2}}
and, as such, it must have at least the
expected dimension
, i.e.
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})\geq{\rm expdim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}_{2}})=m-1-2(\ell-r+2)=\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{J})
. Therefore
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})=\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{J})=9g-16-3d
and so
pr_{2}
is generically finite.

Since
\mathcal{J}
is rational, being a projective bundle over an open dense subset of the Grassmannian
\mathbb{G}
,
then
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
is
unirational
, being finitely dominated by
\mathcal{J}
via
pr_{2}
. Considering
\mathcal{W}^{*}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
the affine cone over
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
, the uniqueness of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
among components of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
for which
{\rm Coker}(\partial_{v})^{\vee}
is general in
\mathbb{G}
follows from the fact that
\mathcal{J}
dominating
\mathbb{G}
is irreducible. This completes the proof of the claim.
∎

To construct the locus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
as usual we take into account a projective bundle
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\to S
, over a suitable open dense subset
S\subseteq{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
, which parametrizes the family of
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N))
’s as
N\in S
varies. Since from Claim
4.36
, for any such
N\in S
,
\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
is irreducible,
unirational
and of (constant) dimension
9g-16-3d
,
one has an irreducible projective variety

(4.59)

(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}:=\left\{(N,[v])\in\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{d})\;|\;[v]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}\right\},

which is therefore
uniruled
over
S
, of dimension

(4.60)

\operatorname{dim}((\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot})=\operatorname{dim}(S)+\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})=g+(9g-16-3d)=10g-3d-16=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+2.

Our next aim is to construct a (rational) modular map
(\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*})^{Tot}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dasharrow}}B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
; to define this map, we therefore need to show that the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
arising from a general pair
(N,[v])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
is stable. Using Theorem
1.16
, this will be done in the result below.

Claim 4.37
.

For
(N,[v])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
general, the corresponding rank-
2
, degree
d
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
is stable, of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
.

Proof of Claim
4.37
.

From above, since
{\rm corank}(\partial_{v})=2
and
h^{1}(\omega_{C})=1
, one easily gets
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
. To prove stability of
\mathcal{F}_{v}
we split arguments into two cases:

(Case 1):
2g-2\leq d\leq\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-6
and   (Case 2):
\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5\leq d\leq 3g-7
.

(Case 1) Assume
2g-2\leq d\leq\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-6
; since

\delta=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C})=2g-2
, from the bounds on
d
one has

2\delta-d=4g-4-d>2
thus we are in position to apply Theorem
1.16
; so we consider the natural morphism
C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)^{\vee})
, given by the complete linear system
|2K_{C}-N|
. If, as usual, we set
X:=\varphi\left(C\right)
and we let
\sigma
to be an integer such that

\sigma\equiv 2\delta-d=4g-4-d\ {\rm{(mod\;2)\ \ and}}

0\leq\sigma\leq 2\delta-d=4g-4-d
, then we have

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(2\delta-d+\sigma-2)}(X)\right)=2\delta-d+\sigma-3=4g-7-d+\sigma<\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})=9g-16-3d

if and only if

\sigma<5g-9-2d
. Since, from the bounds on
d
, one has
5g-9-2d\geq 3
,
we may take
\sigma=1
for
d
odd (resp.,
\sigma=2
for
d
even) which shows in particular the stability of
\mathcal{F}_{v}
.

(Case 2) Assume instead
\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5\leq d\leq 3g-7
. Here we will deduce stability of
\mathcal{F}_{v}
by using the fact that it is an open condition in irreducible families and by the stability of a particular bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
, fitting into (
4.61
) as in Claim
4.38
below, which will correspond to a
special point
of
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
. To do this recall, by Lemma
3.22
, that the necessary and sufficient condition for the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subset W^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}(C)
to be non-empty is

\rho(g,1,4g-4-d-t)=2(4g-4-d-t)-g-2=7g-10-2d-2t\geq 0.

Therefore
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
is non-empty when
d\leq 3g-7
and
t\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
. Based on this, we have the following:

Claim 4.38
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g\geqslant 7
, with

3\leqslant\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and let
d
be an integer with
\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5\leqslant d\leqslant 3g-7
, and either
t:=\frac{g+2}{2}
, when both
g
and
d
are even, or
t:=\lceil\frac{g}{2}\rceil
otherwise. Assume that
D_{t}\in C^{(t)}
is general and that
N^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Pic}^{d-2g+2+t}(C)
is such that
\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}
is general in the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}(C)
as in Lemma
3.22
.

Then there exists a stable, rank-
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=3
which fits into an exact sequence of the form

(4.61)

(w):\ \ \ 0\to N^{\prime}\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\to\omega_{C}(-D_{t})\to 0,

Proof of Claim
4.38
.

Since
K_{C}-N^{\prime}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
is general, by Lemma
3.22
one has
h^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime})=h^{1}(N^{\prime})=2
and hence
h^{0}(N^{\prime})=d-3g+5+t
. From the bounds on
d
and the definition of
t
one has that
h^{0}(N^{\prime})\geq\lfloor\frac{5g}{2}\rfloor-5-3g+5+t=-\lceil\frac{g}{2}\rceil+t\geq 0
.

For the existence of
\mathcal{F}_{w}
fitting in (
4.61
) with speciality
3
we first consider the degeneracy locus
{\mathcal{W}}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
as follows: using notations as in (
1.22
) and in Theorem
3.1
, we have
\ell:=h^{0}(K_{C}-D_{t})=g-t
, whereas
r:=h^{1}(N^{\prime})=h^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime})=2
and
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime}))=5g-5-2t-d
; it follows that
\ell\geq r
, because
g\geq 7
, and
m\geq\ell+1
, because this condition is equivalent to
t\leq 4g-d-6
which certainly holds for
t\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
and
d\leq 3g-7
. Thus, from Theorem
3.1
, for
u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
general, the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{u}
is surjective whereas, since
h^{1}(N^{\prime})=2
, the degeneracy locus
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
as in (
3.1
) turns out to be

{\small{\mathcal{W}}_{2}=\left(\mathrm{coker}\left(H^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}-D_{t})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi^{\vee}}}{{\leftarrow}}H^{0}(K_{C}-D_{t})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime})\right)\right)^{\vee},}

i.e.
\mathcal{W}_{2}=\left(\mathrm{im}\;\Phi^{\vee}\right)^{\perp}
is a sub-vector space of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
, so in particular it is irreducible. Since
|K_{C}-N^{\prime}|
is a base-point-free linear pencil on
C
, due to Lemma
3.22
, by the base-point-free pencil trick one has that

\operatorname{ker}(\Phi^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})
. From the fact that
h^{0}(N^{\prime})=d-3g+5+t
and that
D_{t}
is general of degree
t
, it follows that
\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\Phi^{\vee}))=h^{0}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=0
, because
d-3g+5<0
from the bounds on
d
, so
\Phi^{\vee}
is injective and thus

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{coker}(\Phi^{\vee}))=m-2\ell=(5g-5-2t-d)-2(g-t)=3g-5-d\geq 2,

the latter inequality following from the upper-bound on
d
. Therefore a bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
associated to a point
w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
has speciality 3, as consequence of
h^{1}(N^{\prime})=2
,
h^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t})=1
and the fact that
w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
iff
\partial_{w}=0
.

We want to show that, for
w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}
general, the corresponding bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
is also stable. To do so, setting
\delta:=\mbox{deg}(\omega_{C}(-D_{t}))=2g-2-t
, one notices that
2\delta-d\geq 2
; indeed, the latter inequality reads
d\leq 4g-6-2t
which, by the definition of
t
, certainly holds true because
d\leq 3g-7
which further implies
d\leq 3g-8
when both
g
and
d
are even. We are therefore in position to apply Theorem
1.16
. Set
\mathbb{P}:=\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}-D_{t})^{\vee})
and consider the morphism
C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{P}
, given by the complete linear system
|2K_{C}-N^{\prime}-D_{t}|
; set
X:=\varphi\left(C\right)
and let
\sigma
be an integer such that
\sigma\equiv 2\delta-d=4g-4-2t-d\ {\rm{(mod\;2)\ \ and}}

0\leq\sigma\leq 4g-4-2t-d
. Posing
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{W}_{2})
we have

\operatorname{dim}\left(\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(4g-4-2t-d+\sigma-2)}(X)\right)=4g-7-2t-d+\sigma\leq 3g-6-d=\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2})

if and only if

(4.62)

\sigma\leq-g+1+2t.

Assume that either
g
is odd or both
g
and
d
are even. Then (
4.62
) combined with the definition of
t
implies
0\leq\sigma\leq 2
. Let us take
\sigma=2
(for
d
even). If
g
is odd then the secant variety
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-5-d)}(X)
has the same dimension of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
due to
t=\frac{g+1}{2}
. Nonetheless, the latter is a proper linear subspace in
\mathbb{P}
so necessarily
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-5-d)}(X)\neq\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
because, being the curve
X
non-degenerate in
\mathbb{P}
, also its secant variety
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-5-d)}(X)
is non-degenerate. If
g
is even, then the definition of
t
gives
2t=g+2
and thus the dimension of
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-5-d)}(X)
is less than
\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2})
. Therefore, if either both
g
and
d
are even or
g
is odd and
d
is even, a general
[w]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
does not belong to
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-5-d)}(X)
so
s(\mathcal{F}_{w})\geq 2
which implies that
\mathcal{F}_{w}
is stable. If otherwise we take
\sigma=1
(for
d
odd), then
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-6-d)}(X)
which is such that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-6-d)}(X))=\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2})-1
so certainly
[w]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
general does not belong to this secant variety and so
s(\mathcal{F}_{w})\geq 1
which implies the stability of
\mathcal{F}_{w}
.

We now consider the remaining case, i.e. when
g
is even and
d
is odd. In this case we have
t=\frac{g}{2}
, whence (
4.62
) reads
0\leq\sigma\leq 1
. Since
d
is odd we take
\sigma=1
, which yields that
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-5-d)}(X)
has the same dimension of
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
. Thus we can conclude as above, recalling that
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
is a proper linear subspace in
\mathbb{P}
. In other words,
[w]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
general does not belong to
\mathrm{Sec}_{\frac{1}{2}(3g-5-d)}(X)
so
s(\mathcal{F}_{w})\geq 1
implying stability of
\mathcal{F}_{w}
, which concludes the proof of Claim
4.38
.
∎

We can therefore go on with the proof of (Case 2) of Claim
4.37
. A key step will be the following:

Claim 4.39
.

For
D_{t}\in C^{(t)}
,
\omega_{C}\otimes(N^{\prime})^{\vee}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subseteq W^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}(C)
and

w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
general elements as in (the statement and the proof of) Claim
4.38
, the pair
(N^{\prime}-D_{t},[w])
belongs to the closure of
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
as in (
4.59
).

Proof of Claim
4.39
.

In the above assumptions, we take
\mathcal{F}_{w}
fitting in (
4.61
) with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=3
. From the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
as in the proof of Claim
4.38
, the associated coboundary map
\partial_{w}
is the zero-map, in particular it is not surjective; moreover, since
N^{\prime}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}
, where
g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}
is a base-point-free linear series (as it follows from Lemma
3.22
-
(ii)
), we are in the assumptions of Lemma
4.5
-(c) which implies that any such bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
admits also a presentation of the form

(4.63)

0\to N^{\prime}-D_{t}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}-D_{t}\to\mathcal{F}_{w}\to\omega_{C}\to 0,

where the corresponding coboundary map
\widetilde{\partial_{w}}
is once again s.t.
{\rm corank}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})=2
, as it follows from
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=3
and

h^{1}(\omega_{C})=1
.

Notice that
\mbox{deg}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=d-2g+2=\mbox{deg}(N)
, where
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general as in Claim
4.36
. Since
h^{0}(N^{\prime})=h^{0}(K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})=d-3g+5+t
, the generality of
D_{t}
gives
h^{0}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})={\rm max}\{0,h^{0}(N^{\prime})-t\}
hence one has
h^{0}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=0
due to
d\leq 3g-7
. Thus, by Riemann-Roch, we have

h^{1}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})=h^{0}(K_{C}-(N^{\prime}-D_{t}))=3g-3-d=h^{0}(K_{C}-N)
,
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general as in Claim
4.36
. In other words, when
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general in
{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
specializes inside this Picard variety to
N^{\prime}-D_{t}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}-D_{t}\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
the two vector space
H^{0}(K_{C}-N)
and
H^{0}(K_{C}-(N^{\prime}-D_{t}))
are isomorphic (being of the same dimension) so they are their corresponding Grassmannians
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(K_{C}-N))
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}:=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(K_{C}-(N^{\prime}-D_{t}))
, the former as in Claim
4.36
.

Take
\mathcal{F}_{w}
presented as in (
4.63
); since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})=3
and
h^{1}(\omega_{C})=1
, the corresponding coboundary map
H^{0}(\omega_{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\partial_{w}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(N^{\prime}-D_{t})
gives
[{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}]\in\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
which, from above, is s.t.
\operatorname{dim}(\widetilde{\mathbb{G}})=\operatorname{dim}(\mathbb{G})=6g-10-2d
, the latter equality following from computations as in the proof of Claim
4.36
; let us denote by
\Sigma\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
the irreducible locus filled-up by points of the form
[{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}]
, for

w\in\mathcal{W}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t},N^{\prime})
general. On the one hand from the presentation of
\mathcal{F}_{w}
as in (
4.61
), where
\partial_{w}
has to be the zero-map (cf. the proof of Claim
4.38
), we have

0\to H^{1}(N^{\prime})={\rm coker}(\partial_{w})\to H^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})\to H^{1}(K_{C}-D_{t})\to 0
, namely
{\rm coker}(\partial_{w})^{\vee}\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime})=H^{0}(g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})
. On the other, from the presentation of
\mathcal{F}_{w}
as in (
4.63
), we get

0\to{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})\to H^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{w})\to H^{1}(K_{C})\to 0
. Dualizing the above sequences and using also Serre duality and the fact that
{\rm coker}(\partial_{w})^{\vee}\simeq H^{0}(g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})
, we get

{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}\oplus H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\simeq H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{w}^{\vee})\simeq H^{0}(g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})\oplus H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{t})).

If we denote by
\{1,f\}
a basis of
H^{0}(g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2}
and by
h\in H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(D_{t}))\simeq\mathbb{C}
any non-zero element, previous isomorphisms imply that
{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}
is generated by
\{f,h\}
. Thus, if we consider the linear pencil associated to

{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}
, its base locus of is a subdivisor of
G\cap D_{t}
, where
G
denotes the divisor on
C
defined by
f
. Since
D_{t}
is general, one has
\mbox{deg}(G\cap D_{t})\leq 1
. Therefore, denoting by
\mu_{{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}}
the restriction of the natural multiplication map

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\mu:=\mu_{K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t},\;K_{C}}:&H^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})&\to&H^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t})\\
||&||&&||\\
\mu_{g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}+D_{t},K_{C}}:&H^{0}(g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}+D_{t})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})&\to&H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}+D_{t})\end{array}

to the sub-vector space
{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})\subset H^{0}(K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})
, by the base-point-free pencil trick we have that

\operatorname{dim}({\rm ker}(\mu_{{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}}))=h^{0}(K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}-(D_{t}-(G\cap D_{t}))\leq{\rm max}\{0,g-(4g-4-d-t)+1-(t-1)\}={\rm max}\{0,-3g+6+d\}=0.

the latter equality because
d\leq 3g-7
by assumptions.
Since
\Sigma\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
, by semicontinuity it therefore follows that also
{\rm ker}(\mu_{V_{2}})=\{0\}
, for
[V_{2}]\in\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
general, where
\mu_{V_{2}}
the restriction to
V_{2}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})
of the multiplication map
\mu=\mu_{K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t},\;K_{C}}
as above.

Taking into account what proved above, namely that:

\bullet

N^{\prime}-D_{t}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}-D_{t}
has the same degree and cohomology of
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general as in Claims
4.35
,
4.36
and that the same occurs for
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N^{\prime}-D_{t})\simeq H^{1}(N^{\prime}-D_{t}-K_{C})\simeq H^{0}(2K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t})^{\vee}=H^{0}(K_{C}+D_{t}+g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t})^{\vee}
and
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},N)\simeq H^{1}(N-K_{C})=H^{0}(2K_{C}-N)^{\vee}
,

\bullet

[V_{2}]\in\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
general and
[{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}]\in\Sigma\subset\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
both define sub-linear series of
|K_{C}-N^{\prime}+D_{t}|=|g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}+D_{t}|
for which both maps

\mu_{V_{2}}
and
\mu_{{\rm coker}(\widetilde{\partial_{w}})^{\vee}}
are injective,

then one can reproduce verbatim the same proof as in Claim
4.36
and deduces that the locus
\Sigma\subseteq\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
is contained in the image,
via the map
pr_{1}
, of the relevant incidence variety
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
dominating
\widetilde{\mathbb{G}}
, as in the proof therein. This implies that
w
belongs to the unique irreducible component
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N^{\prime}-D_{t})
arising as the image via the generically finite map
pr_{2}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{J}}
. Therefore when
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general specializes to

N^{\prime}-D_{t}=K_{C}-g^{1}_{4g-4-d-t}-D_{t}\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
, by uniqueness reasons the component
\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N)
specializes to the component
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{2}^{*}}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N^{\prime}-D_{t})
which proves that the pair
(N^{\prime}-D_{t},[w])
belongs to the closure of
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
as in (
4.59
), so the proof of Claim
4.39
is completed.
∎

To sum-up, from Claim
4.39
, the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{w}
as in Claim
4.38
corresponds to a
special point
in
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
. Combining this with the stability of
\mathcal{F}_{w}
from Claim
4.38
, for
v\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
general the corresponding bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
is stable, so the proof of Claim
4.37
is completed.
∎

From Claim
4.37
, we can therefore consider a natural (rational) modular map

\begin{array}[]{ccc}(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U_{C}(d)\\
(N,[v])&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{v}],\end{array}

which, by previous construction, is s.t.
{\rm im}(\pi)\subset B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
. In the next results, we will show that the general fiber of
\pi
has dimension
2
, deducing that
\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\pi))=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
, and finally, considering the closure of
{\rm im}(\pi)
in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
, it will give rise to the regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
as mentioned.

Claim 4.40
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
be the closure in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
of the locus
{\rm im}(\pi)
as above. Then
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
is uniruled, of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3})=\rho^{k_{3}}_{d}=10g-18-3d
.

Proof of Claim
4.40
.

The fact that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
is uniruled directly follows from the fact that
{\rm im}(\pi)
, which is dense in it, is dominated by
(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
which is uniruled over
S
by construction.

To prove the assertion on the dimension of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, since from (
4.60
) we have
\operatorname{dim}\left((\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}\right)=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+2
, we then need to show that
[\mathcal{F}_{v}]\in{\rm im}(\pi)
general is s.t.
\operatorname{dim}(\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}]))=2
. Let therefore
[\mathcal{F}_{v}]\in{\rm im}(\pi)
be general, namely this bundle arises from a general point
[v]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}\subseteq\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N))
, with
N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
general. Then

\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}])=\left\{(N^{\prime},[v^{\prime}])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}\,|\;\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{v}\right\}
. From determinantal reasons, if
\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{v}
one has in particular that
N\simeq N^{\prime}
, which therefore implies that
[v],[v^{\prime}]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(\omega_{C},N))
.

Let
\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathcal{F}_{v}
be the isomorphism between the two bundles and consider the induced diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccccl}0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{v^{\prime}}&\to&\omega_{C}&\to 0\\
&&&\downarrow^{\varphi}&&&\\
0\to&N&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&\mathcal{F}_{v}&\to&\omega_{C}&\to 0.\end{array}

If
[v]=[v^{\prime}]
, then
\varphi=\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
(since
\mathcal{F}_{v}
is simple, being stable) so the two maps
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}=\lambda\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
determine two non-zero global sections
s_{1}\neq s_{2}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes N^{\vee})
.
Since
\mathcal{F}_{v}
is of rank
2
, with speciality
3
and with determinant
\det(\mathcal{F}_{v})=\omega_{C}\otimes N
, by Serre duality we get
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes N^{\vee})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=3
. Therefore, if
\Gamma\subset F_{v}=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{v})
denotes a canonical section of the surface scroll
F_{v}
, corresponding to the quotient
\mathcal{F}_{v}\to\!\!\to\omega_{C}
,
(\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}])
contains the linear system
|\Gamma|
which is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{2}
by (
1.14
). Finally, the case
[v]\neq[v^{\prime}]\in\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2}
cannot occur: indeed, for any inclusion
\iota_{1}
as above, there exist an inclusion
\iota_{2}
and a
\lambda=\lambda(\iota_{1},\iota_{2})\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
such that
\varphi\circ\iota_{1}=\lambda\iota_{2}
, otherwise we would have
\operatorname{dim}(|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|)>2
, a contradiction. Thus
the two global sections would be linearly dependent, a contradiction by
[
12
, Lemma 4.5]
. Therefore
\operatorname{dim}(\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{v}])=2
and the proof is complete.
∎

Claim 4.41
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
general as above, the corresponding bundle
\mathcal{F}
admits as quotient line bundles also
\omega_{C}(-p)
, for
p\in C
general.

If we denote by
\Gamma
and
\Gamma_{p}
sections in the surface scroll
F:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F})
which respectively correspond to the quotient line bundles of
\omega_{C}
and
\omega_{C}(-p)
of
\mathcal{F}
, with
p\in C
general, then:

(i)

\Gamma
is not linearly isolated (not li) on
F
, as it moves in a
2
-dimensional linear system, and
\omega_{C}
is not of minimal degree among possible special quotients of
\mathcal{F}
. In particular
\mathcal{F}
is neither rigidly presented (not rp) nor rigidly specially presented (not rsp) via the canonical quotient
\omega_{C}
;

(ii)

\Gamma_{p}
is neither algebraically isolated (not ai) nor algebraically specially isolated (not asi) on
F
, i.e.

\mathcal{F}
is neither rigidly presented (not rp) nor rigidly specially presented (not rsp) via
\omega_{C}(-p)
. Moreover, the line bundle
\omega_{C}(-p)
is not of minimal degree among possible special quotients of
\mathcal{F}
.

Proof of Claim
4.41
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
general we know that the canonical linear system
|\Gamma|
on the associated surface scroll
F
is such that
\operatorname{dim}(|\Gamma|)=2
. Therefore
\Gamma
is not (li) on
F
and
\mathcal{F}
is neither (rp) nor (rsp) via
\omega_{C}
.

Similarly as in the proof of Claim
4.23
, one can show that
F
contains a special section
\Gamma_{p}
of degree
2g-3
as in the statement, namely that the bundle
\mathcal{F}
admits also as a quotient
\omega_{C}(-p)
, in particular
\omega_{C}
is not of minimal degree among possible special quotients of
\mathcal{F}
, and
(i)
is proved. Moreover, for determinantal reasons, one deduces that
\mathcal{F}
fits also in the exact sequence
0\to N(p)\to\mathcal{F}\to\omega_{C}(-p)\to 0,
where
N(p)\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+3}(C)
is general by the generality of
N
. From (
1.12
), one has
\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F}\simeq K_{C}-2p-N
which is therefore general too, of degree
4g-6-d\geq g+1
, where the inequality follows from the bounds on
d
; thus
K_{C}-2p-N
is non-special, effective, with
h^{0}(K_{C}-2p-N)=h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F})\geq 2
. This implies that
[\Gamma_{p}]
is a smooth point of the Hilbert scheme
{\rm Div}_{F}^{1,2g-3}
, which is of dimension at least
2
at
[\Gamma_{p}]
; therefore
\Gamma_{p}
is not (ai) and
\mathcal{F}
is not (rp) via
\omega_{C}(-p)
.

Since the previous arguments hold true for
p\in C
general, then there exists an open dense subset
U\subseteq C
which maps not constantly to
{\rm Div}_{F}^{1,2g-3}
as a parameter space of the algebraic family of sections
\{\Gamma_{p}\}_{p\in U}
; this implies that a section
\Gamma_{p}
, corresponding to
\omega_{C}(-p)
with
p\in C
general, is not (asi) on
F
and so
\mathcal{F}
is not (rsp) via
\omega_{C}(-p)
. Since
h^{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F})\geq 2
, one can finally complete the proof of
(ii)
by Proposition
1.7
-(2-ii).
∎

To complete the proof of Proposition
4.34
, we are left with proving the following result.

Claim 4.42
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
general as above, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective. In particular,
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
is an irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
which is regular and uniruled.

Proof of Claim
4.42
.

Uniruledness and stated dimension of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
has already been proved in Claim
4.40
. To prove
regularity
, i.e. generic smoothness, we show that for
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
general the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective.
Recall that such a
\mathcal{F}
corresponds to a general pair
(N,[v])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
, i.e.
\mathcal{F}
fits in:

(4.64)

0\to N\to\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{v}\to\omega_{C}\to 0\ \ \ \mbox{ with }h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3\mbox{ and }N\in{\rm Pic}^{d-2g+2}\mbox{ general}.

Since
K_{C}-N
is also general, of degree
4g-4-d\geq g+3
, the line bundle
K_{C}-N
is non-special. We set
g^{r}_{g+r}:=K_{C}-N
and
\mathcal{E}_{v}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\vee}
, where
h^{0}(K_{C}-N)=r+1.
From the dual sequence of (
4.64
) it follows that

(4.65)

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\to\mathcal{E}_{v}\to g^{r}_{g+r}\to 0\ \ \ \mbox{ with }h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})=3.

Since
\mathcal{F}
corresponds to
(N,[v])\in(\widehat{\mathcal{W}^{*}}_{2})^{Tot}
general, one has that

(4.66)

0\to H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})\to H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})\to V_{2}\to 0\ \ \ \mbox{ with }h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})=3.

for a general
[V_{2}]\in\mathbb{G}:=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(K_{C}-N))=\mathbb{G}(2,H^{0}(g^{r}_{g+r}))
. Considering that a non-special line bundle is base-point-free, one has that
K_{C}-N
is base-point-free and so it is
V_{2}
general as above.

From this, we can show that the bundle
\mathcal{E}_{v}=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\vee}
is globally generated. Indeed, by the exact sequence (
4.65
), for an arbitrary
p\in C
one has the following diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}(-p)&\rightarrow&\mathcal{E}_{v}(-p)&\rightarrow&g^{r}_{g+r}(-p)&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{C}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{E}_{v}&\rightarrow&g^{r}_{g+r}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{p}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{E}_{v}|_{p}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{p}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&\end{array}

which gives rise to

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C}(-p))&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v}(-p))&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}&{\rm im}(\alpha)&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{C})&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})&\longrightarrow&V_{2}&\rightarrow&0,\end{array}

where
{\rm im}(\alpha)\subset H^{0}(g^{r}_{g+r}(-p))
.
One notices that
{\rm im}(\alpha)
is a subspace of
V_{2}
and so
\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\alpha))\leq 2
. On the one hand,
V_{2}
gives rise to a base-point-free linear pencil of
g^{r}_{g+r}
, whereas
{\rm im}(\alpha)
gives rise to a sub-series of
g^{r}_{g+r}(-p)\subset g^{r}_{g+r}
. This implies that
{\rm im}(\alpha)
is a proper subspace of
V_{2}
, whence
\operatorname{dim}({\rm im}(\alpha))\leq 1
. Therefore one gets
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v}(-p))\leq 1
, equivalently
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v}(-p))\leq h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})-2
so the bundle
\mathcal{E}_{v}=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\vee}
is globally generated.

From the global generation of
\mathcal{E}_{v}
and the facts that

h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})=3
and
\det(\mathcal{E}_{v})=g^{r}_{g+r}
as above, one gets therefore

0\to\det(\mathcal{E}_{v})^{\vee}=-g^{r}_{g+r}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})\to\mathcal{E}_{v}\to 0
whence, tensoring this exact sequence by
\mathcal{F}_{v}
, one gets

0\to\mathcal{F}_{v}(-g^{r}_{g+r})\to\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{v})\to\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{E}_{v}\to 0.

Taking cohomology, together with
\mathcal{E}_{v}=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\vee}
, yields that

(4.67)

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}(-g^{r}_{g+r}))\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{v}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{v}^{\vee})\,,

whence
\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{v}})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}(-g^{r}_{g+r}))
. The exact sequence (
4.64
) gives rise to

0\to N-g^{r}_{g+r}\to\mathcal{F}_{v}(-g^{r}_{g+r})\to K_{C}-g^{r}_{g+r}\to 0.

Since
N
is general in
\operatorname{Pic}^{d-2g+2}(C)
with
d-2g+2\leq g-5
and
K_{C}-N=g^{r}_{g+r}
it follows that
h^{0}(N)=0
and
h^{0}(K_{C}-g^{r}_{g+r})=0
, which yields

h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{v}(-g^{r}_{g+r}))=0
.
As a consequence, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{v}}
is injective.
∎

The previous arguments conclude the proof of Proposition
4.34
.
∎

\boxed{3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6:\;\mbox{existence of a regular component}}
Here we treat the left cases which are possible for a
regular
component to exist, namely we focus on
3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
. As already done in Proposition
4.22
-
(ii)
and
(iii)
, to let the constructions easier, we shall perform slight modifications to presentation of bundles
of
second type
. Precisely, we will use kernel line-bundles
N
arising from a slight modification of case
(2-c)
of Proposition
4.13
.

Before going into the details of the construction of
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, we first need the following preliminary result.

Lemma 4.43
.

Let
g\geq 9
and
4\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
be integers and let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
. Let
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
be general in such a component, as in Lemma
4.2
, with
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
and
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t})=3
(in particular
\rho(g,2,t)=3t-2g-6\geq 0
).

Consider
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subset{\rm Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
as in (
3.1
) and assume there exists an irreducible and positive dimensional locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}
contained in
\mathcal{W}_{2}
, whose general point
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
is such that
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=2
. Let
\mathcal{F}_{u}
be the rank-
2
vector bundle arising from such a general
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
, i.e. fitting in an exact sequence of the form:

(4.68)

(u)\ \ \ 0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}\to 0.

Let
F_{u}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
denote the surface scroll associated to
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. Then:

(i)

the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits also in an exact sequence of the form:

(u_{p})\ \ \ \ \ 0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-p\to 0,

where
p\in C
general;

(ii)

for
p\in C
general, the section
\Gamma_{p}
corresponding to the quotient line bundle
K_{C}-p
of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is linearly isolated in
F_{u}
;

(iii)

for
p\in C
general, the sections
\Gamma_{p}
are all algebraically equivalent on
F_{u}
, belonging to a same
1
-dimensional, reduced and irreducible component of
{\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}}
;

(iv)

for
p\in C
general,
K_{C}-p
is of minimal degree among effective and special quotients of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
;

(v)

\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable for either
d\geq 3g-6
, when
\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
, or
d\geq 4g-3-4\nu
, when
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
.

Proof.

(i) The proof is similar to that in Proposition
4.6
. Indeed, the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits in the exact sequence (
4.68
) and, from the assumptions on
\mathcal{U}_{2}
, the associated coboundary map
H^{0}(K_{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{u}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\to H^{1}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
has corank two. This is equivalent to
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
thus, by Serre duality and
\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})\simeq 2K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}
, one has
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}-(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}))=3
. If we set
F_{u}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
and if
\Gamma
denotes a canonical section of
F_{u}
corresponding to the quotient line bundle
\omega_{C}
, from (
1.14
) we get that
\operatorname{dim}(|\mathcal{O}_{F_{u}}(\Gamma)|)=2
. Similalry as in the proof of Claim
4.23
, we get that
F_{u}
contains a section
\Gamma_{p}
corresponding to the quotient line bundle
\omega_{C}(-p)
of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and, by determinantal reasons,
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits also in
(u_{p})
as in the statement.

(ii) Let
q\in F_{u}
be a general point, which therefore projects onto the general point
p\in C
via the natural scroll map
F_{u}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}C
, i.e.
\rho(q)=p
. If we consider the linear pencil
|\mathcal{I}_{q/F_{u}}(\Gamma)|
passing through
q
, by Bertini’s theorem its general element is a canonical section, i.e. it is irreducible. To split-off the fiber
f_{p}
, in order to get a section
\Gamma_{p}
, we need to impose an extra base-point on
f_{p}
, which therefore implies that
\Gamma_{p}
is linearly isolated as claimed. Notice further that, from the exact sequence
(u_{p})
and from (
1.14
), this fact is equivalent to
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-K_{C}-p+g^{2}_{t}))=1
; from Serre duality, one has
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}(2K_{C}+p-g^{2}_{t}))=1
and, since from
(u)
one has

\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}
, the previous equality reads also
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(p))=1
.

(iii) From (
1.12
) we have
\mathcal{N}_{{\Gamma_{p}}/{F_{u}}}\simeq(K_{C}-p)-(K_{C}+p-g^{2}_{t})=g^{2}_{t}-2p
. By Lemma
4.2
recall that a general
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
is birationally very ample. Therefore

h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-2p)=1
for
p\in C
general, i.e.
\operatorname{dim}_{[\Gamma_{p}]}({\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}})=1
.
Even if
h^{1}(\mathcal{N}_{\Gamma_{p}/F_{u}})=h^{1}(g^{2}_{t}-2p)=g+2-t\geq 0
, where the equality holds iff
t=g+2
, one has that for
p\in C
general
[\Gamma_{p}]\in{\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}}
is a smooth point of this Hilbert scheme as, from (i), there exists an open dense subset
U\subseteq C
for which,
for any
p\in U\subseteq C
,
\Gamma_{p}
is a section on
F_{u}
since
\mathcal{F}_{u}
admits
\omega_{C}(-p)
as a quotient line bundle for any such
p\in U\subseteq C
. Thus
[\Gamma_{p}]
belongs to
the irreducible,
1
-dimensional locus
\mathfrak{G}_{U}:=\{\Gamma_{p}\}_{p\in U}
which is entirely contained in
{\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}}
. In other words,
\mathfrak{G}_{U}
is a
1
-dimensional, generically smooth component of
{\rm Div}^{1,2g-3}_{F_{u}}
which implies that sections
\Gamma_{p}
, for
p\in U
varying, are all algebraically (but not linearly) equivalent, all of them of speciality
1
.

(iv) Assume by contradiction that, for
p\in C
general,
\omega_{C}(-p)
is not of minimal degree among effective and special quotients of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
. Then there exits
q\in C
for which
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits in

0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p+q\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-p-q\to 0.

This implies that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)+h^{0}(p+q)=h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)+1
, as
C
is not hyperelliptic. As reminded above, a general
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
is birationally very ample. Thus one has
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)=1
, for
p\in C
general, hence, from above,
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq 2
. This contradicts
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
whence, for
p\in C
general,
\omega_{C}(-p)
is of minimal degree among effective and special quotients of
\mathcal{F}_{u}
.

(v) We first assume by contradiction
\mathcal{F}_{u}
to be unstable. This means that there exists a maximal sub-line bundle
M\subset\mathcal{F}_{u}
, with
\mbox{deg}(M)>\frac{d}{2}=\frac{\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F}_{u})}{2}
. Since, from (i),
\mathcal{F}_{u}
fits also in a sequence
(u_{p})
, for
p\in C
general, by determinantal reasons one has the following diagram:

(4.69)

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&&&0&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&M&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\rightarrow&K_{C}-p&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&2K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}-M&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&0&&&&\end{array}

Since
\mbox{deg}(M)>\frac{d}{2}=2g-2-\frac{t}{2}
whereas

\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)=2g-1-t
and since
t>2
, one has therefore
\mbox{deg}(M)>\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
. Hence there is no line-bundle map from
M
to
K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p
, so the composition of the two maps
M\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{u}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u}\to\!\!\!\to K_{C}-p
from (
4.69
) gives a non-zero line-bundle map
M\to K_{C}-p
which, necessarily, has to be injective. Therefore
M=K_{C}-p-E_{e,p}
for some effective divisor
E_{e,p}
of degree
e>0
, otherwise
M
would be isomorphic to
K_{C}-p
and, in such a case,
\mathcal{F}_{u}
would correspond to the zero-vector
\mathcal{F}_{0}
of the extension space, contradicting that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
arises from a general element of a positive dimensional irreducible locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}
. For determinantal reasons, we have therefore the exact sequence:

(4.70)

0\to M=K_{C}-p-E_{e,p}\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p+E_{e,p}\to 0,

which yields
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})+h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-E_{e,p})
. On the one hand, as already mentioned above, a general
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
is birationally very ample, so
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-E_{e,p})\leq 1
, being
p\in C
general and
E_{e,p}
effective. The exact sequence (
4.70
) implies therefore
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})\geq 2
since
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
. In other words,
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{1}_{e+1}(C)
.

We remark that
e+1:=\mbox{deg}(p+E_{e,p})<\frac{t}{2}
, due to
\mbox{deg}(M)>\frac{d}{2}=2g-2-\frac{t}{2}
. Moreover, from the assumptions,
t\leq g+2
so one has
\frac{t}{2}\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
. Since
e+1<\frac{t}{2}\leq\frac{g+2}{2}
, from Lemma
3.22
it follows that
W^{1}_{e+1}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
, namely
|p+E_{e,p}|=g^{1}_{\nu}+B_{e+1-\nu}
, where
B_{e+1-\nu}
denotes an effective divisor of base points of
|p+E_{e,p}|
. This means that
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p}-g^{1}_{\nu})\geq 1
. From Lemma
4.2
, if we consider
the maximal splitting vector associated to
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general, it is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\vec{w}_{2,0})=B(\nu-3,t+1-g-\nu)\oplus B(3,0)
.
Since
g^{1}_{\nu}
corresponds to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
, one sees that

h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-g^{1}_{\nu})=h^{0}(B(\nu-3,t-g-\nu)\oplus B(3,-1))=0

so, a fortiori,

h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-(p+E_{e,p}))=0
, since
|p+E_{e,p}|=g^{1}_{\nu}+B_{e+1-\nu}
. Using this, combined with
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
in the exact sequence (
4.70
), we get that
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})\geq 3
, i.e.
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{e+1}(C)
. Since
\mbox{deg}(p+E_{e,p})<\frac{g+2}{2}
and since
\nu\geq 4
from the assumptions, by classification in Lemma
4.2
we have that

W^{2}_{e+1}(C)=\emptyset
when
\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
(this is because
e+1<\frac{g+2}{2}
and the latter integer is strictly less than all the upper-bounds appearing in the first line of the lists of cases occuring in (iii), (iv), (v), (vi) and (vii) of Lemma
4.2
) so we get contradiction when

d\geq 3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2},

as, from
(u)
, one has
d=4g-4-t
with
t\leq g+2
. When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, from Lemma
4.2
,
W^{2}_{e+1}(C)
is not empty and irreducible consisting only of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
. In particular, one must have

e+1-2\nu\geq 0
, so one has
2\nu\leq e+1<\frac{t}{2}
, which forces
t>4\nu
and so

d=4g-4-t<4g-4-4\nu
. Therefore, we get contradiction for

d\geq 4g-4-4\nu\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}.

Assume now by contradiction that
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is strictly semi-stable, which can occur only when
d
is even. This means that there exists a maximal sub-line bundle
M\subset\mathcal{F}_{u}
, with
\mbox{deg}(M)=\frac{d}{2}=2g-2-\frac{t}{2}
. As above, from diagram (
4.69
) and from
t>2
, we get
\mbox{deg}(M)>\mbox{deg}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
so
there exists some effective divisor
E_{e,p}
of degree
e>0
for which
M=K_{C}-p-E_{e,p}
, because
\mathcal{F}_{u}
arises from a general element of a positive dimensional irreducible locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
.
One gets (
4.70
) from which, with same reasoning as above, one deduces
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{e+1}(C)
. For
e+1=\frac{t}{2}<\frac{g+2}{2}
, as above, we get contradiction for

d\geq 3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}

since, from Lemma
4.2
, one has
W^{2}_{e+1}(C)=\emptyset
.
When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
and
e+1=\frac{t}{2}<\frac{g+2}{2}
, from Lemma
4.2

W^{2}_{e+1}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
so one has
2\nu\leq e+1=\frac{t}{2}
, which forces
t\geq 4\nu
and so
d=4g-4-t\leq 4g-4-4\nu
. Therefore, we get contradiction for

d\geq 4g-3-4\nu\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}.

Finally, if
e+1=\frac{t}{2}=\frac{g+2}{2}
this means that
d=3g-6
, which must be even, so also
g
has to be even. From the first part of the above arguments, one gets
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
which, from Lemma
3.22
, is such that

W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}\cup\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}.

If we are in the first component, i.e.
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
, then
|p+E_{e,p}|=g^{1}_{\nu}+B_{\frac{g+2}{2}-\nu}
and, with the same reasoning as above, one deduces that
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
. In such a case, when

d=3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}

we get contradiction because, from Lemma
4.2
,
W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\emptyset
. When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, once again one has
W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
which forces
\frac{t}{2}=\frac{g+2}{2}\geq 2\nu
, i.e.
\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{4}
, so we get contradiction for

d=3g-6\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}.

If otherwise
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subset W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
, in this case one has
\rho(g,1,\frac{g+2}{2})=0
, i.e.
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}})=0
whereas
g^{2}_{t}=g^{2}_{g+2}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subset W^{2}_{g+2}(C)
is general in the
g
-dimensional component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
, where the statement on the dimension follows from Lemma
4.2
. From (
4.70
) one has

3=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\leq h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))+h^{0}(p+E_{e,p}).

Since
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2})=3
, then
0\leq h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))\leq 2
because, as reminded above,
g^{2}_{g+2}
general is birationally very-ample whereas
p+E_{e,p}
is assumed to be in
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}\subset W^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
. Furthermore
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))=2
cannot occur: otherwise either

g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
which has dimension

0
so, since also
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}}
whereas

\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})=g
, we would get contradiction, or
otherwise
g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p})\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}}
which, from Lemma
3.22
, is such that
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,1}}})=\frac{g+2}{2}-\nu
so once again, taking into account that
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{1,0}}})
which is
0
-dimensional whereas

g^{2}_{g+2}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
varies in dimension
g
, we would get another contradiction. Notice that also
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))=1
cannot occur, otherwise

g^{2}_{g+2}=(p+E_{e,p})+B_{\frac{g+2}{2}}=g^{1}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}+B_{\frac{g+2}{2}}
, where
B_{\frac{g+2}{2}}
an effective base-divisor, another contradiction. Thus the only possibility is
h^{0}(g^{2}_{g+2}-(p+E_{e,p}))=0
so, from the previous inequality, we deduce that
h^{0}(p+E_{e,p})\geq 3
, i.e.
\mathcal{O}_{C}(p+E_{e,p})\in W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)
. From Lemma
4.2
, we get contradiction for

d=3g-6\;\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\;\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}.

When otherwise
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
, from Lemma
4.2

one has
W^{2}_{\frac{g+2}{2}}(C)=\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,2}}}
, which forces

\frac{g+2}{2}-2\nu\geq 0
, i.e.
\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{4}
, therefore, once again, we get contradiction for

d=3g-6\;\;{\rm and}\;\;\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}.

To sum-up (v) is completely proved.
∎

Proposition 4.44
.

Let
C
be a general
\nu
-gonal curve of genus
g
, with
g\geq 9
,
\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
and let
3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
be any integer.

Then,
B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
admits a regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
whose general point
[\mathcal{F}]
corresponds to a rank-
2
stable bundle
\mathcal{F}
of degree
d
and speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F})=3
, which fits into an exact sequence (
4.68
) as in Lemma
4.43
, where
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general as in Lemma
4.2
, where necessarily
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
and where
\mathcal{F}
satisfying all the properties (i)-(v) as in Lemma
4.43
.

Proof.

Notice that, if the bundle
\mathcal{F}
as in the statement fits in the exact sequence (
4.68
) of Lemma
4.43
, then
d=\mbox{deg}(\mathcal{F})=4g-4-t
, Therefore, from the given bounds on
d
one finds that
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
as in the assumptions of Lemma
4.43
. Recall moreover that, from Lemma
4.43
, a general bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
as in the assumptions therein turns out to be stable either for
d\geq 3g-6
, when the gonality was
\frac{g+3}{3}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
, or for
d\geq 4g-3-4\nu
, when
4\leq\nu\leq\frac{g+2}{3}
. Notice that, when
\nu\geq\frac{g+3}{4}
one has
4g-3-4\nu\leq 3g-6
; in particular, for any
\frac{g+3}{4}\leq\nu<\frac{g}{2}
,
\mathcal{F}_{u}
as in Lemma
4.43
turns out to be stable for any
3g-6\leq d\leq\frac{10}{3}g-6
in the range of interest. This explains the assumptions on
\nu
in the above statement.

To construct the component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, following Lemma
4.43
, we therefore need first to produce a positive dimensional irreducible locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
with all properties listed therein. Consider
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general where, from Lemma
4.2
,
\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})=3t-2g-6\geq 0
, the latter inequality following from the bounds on
t
(arising from those on
d
).
Then, consider
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
which is of dimension
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}))=h^{1}(-g^{2}_{t})=h^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})=g-1+t\geq\frac{5}{3}g+1
, from the bounds on
t
. Using notation as in (
1.22
) and in Theorem
3.1
, one has
\ell:=h^{0}(K_{C})=g
and
r:=h^{1}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})=h^{0}(g^{2}_{t})=3
, by generality of
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
. Since
\ell\geq r
and
m\geq\ell+1
, from Theorem
3.1
it follows that
v\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
general gives rise to a vector bundle
\mathcal{F}_{v}
of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{v})=1
, since the corresponding coboundary map
\partial_{v}
is surjective, whereas the locus
\mathcal{W}_{1}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
as in (
3.1
) is not-empty, irreducible, of (
expected
) dimension
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{W}_{1})=m-c(1)=m-(\ell-r+1)=(g-1+t)-(g-3+1)=t+1
(recall (
3.2
)); moreover, for
w\in\mathcal{W}_{1}
general,
{\rm corank}(\partial_{w})=1
, thus, considering
\mathcal{W}_{2}
defined as in (
3.1
), one has necessarily
\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq\mathcal{W}_{1}
. From (
3.2
), the expected codimension of

\mathcal{W}_{2}
in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
, is given by
c(2)=2(\ell-r+2)=2(g-1)=2g-2
. In particular

{\rm expdim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})={\rm max}\{0,\;m-c(2)\}={\rm max}\{0,\;(g-1+t)-(2g-2)\}={\rm max}\{0,\;t-g+1\},

in other words
\mathcal{W}_{2}
is expected to be

\mathcal{W}_{2}=\mathcal{W}_{3}=\{\mathcal{F}_{0}=(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})\oplus K_{C}\}
, for any
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g-1
, and to be of dimension
j\in\{1,2,3\}
for
t=g+j-1
. The next result shows that this is not the case.

Claim 4.45
.

For any
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g+2
and for
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general, the locus

\mathcal{W}_{2}\subsetneq\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
as above is not-empty and it contains an irreducible
component
\mathcal{U}_{2}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
which is uniruled, of
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{U}_{2})=2
and whose general point
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
gives rise to an exact sequence (
4.68
) as in Lemma
4.43
, i.e. with
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=2
. For
\frac{2}{3}g+2\leq t\leq g
, the component
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is superabundant, i.e. it has dimension higher than the expected one, which is
{\rm expdim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})={\rm max}\{0,\;t-g+1\}
as from (
3.2
).

Proof.

Once we construct the component
\mathcal{U}_{2}
, its superabundance will be a consequence of the previoous computations
where we found
{\rm expdim}(\mathcal{W}_{2})={\rm max}\{0,\;t-g+1\}
. We are therefore left to constructing such an irreducible component
\mathcal{U}_{2}
.

Recall that, being
g^{2}_{t}\in\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
general, by Lemma
4.2

g^{2}_{t}
is a complete linear series which is birationally very ample, so there exists an open dense subset
U\subseteq C
such that, for any
p\in U
,
|g^{2}_{t}-p|
is a base-point-free pencil
of degree
t-1
on
C
. Thus, for any
p\in U
, one has that

[V_{2,p}:=H^{0}(C,g^{2}_{t}-p)]\in\mathbb{G}:=\mathbb{G}\big(2,H^{0}(g^{2}_{t})\big)
. Since for
p\neq q\in U
general one has
|g^{2}_{t}-p|\neq|g^{2}_{t}-q|
, this gives rise to an irreducible locus in the Grassmannian
\mathbb{G}
defined as

\mathcal{V}_{2,U}:=\{[V_{2,p}]\}_{p\in U}\subset\mathbb{G}
, where
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{V}_{2,U})=\operatorname{dim}(U)=1
.

For any
[V_{2,p}]\in\mathcal{V}_{2,U}
, consider the map
\mu_{V_{2,p}}:V_{2,p}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})\to H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})
given by the restriction to
V_{2,p}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C})
of the natural multiplication map
\mu_{g^{2}_{t},K_{C}}
. Since
|V_{2,p}|=|g^{2}_{t}-p|
, from the base-point-free pencil trick one has

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{V_{2,p}}))=h^{0}(K_{C}-(g^{2}_{t}-p))=h^{1}(g^{2}_{t}-p)=g+2-t
, therefore,

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\mu_{V_{2,p}}))=2g-(g+2-t)=g+t-2
. From the definition of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
as in (
3.1
), one has

\mathcal{W}_{2}=\bigg\{u\in\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})\simeq H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})^{\vee}\,|\,\exists\;V\subseteq H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}),\;\operatorname{dim}(V)\geq 2,\;\mathrm{im}(\mu_{V})\subseteq\{u=0\}\bigg\}

and since, from computations above, we have
m:=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}))=h^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})=g+t-1
, we deduce that
\mathrm{im}(\mu_{V_{2,p}})
is already a hyperplane in
H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t})
and the locus
\mathcal{V}_{2,U}\subset\mathbb{G}
gives rise to an irreducible,
1
-dimensional
locus
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}))
such that, for

[u]\in\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
general, one has

\mathrm{coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}\simeq V_{2,p}=H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p)
, where
p\in C
general. Considering

\mathcal{U}_{2}\subset\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C},K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
, which is the affine cone over
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
, this gives rise to a
2
-dimensional, irreducible locus in
\mathcal{W}_{2}
such that, for

u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
general,

{\rm coker}(\partial_{u})^{\vee}\simeq V_{2,p}=H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p)\in\mathcal{V}_{2,U}\subset\mathbb{G}
.

We want to prove that
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is actually an irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. Let
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
be any irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
which contains the locus
\mathcal{U}_{2}
constructed above. Since, for
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
general
one has by construction
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u})=2
then, by semicontinuty on
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
and by the fact that
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}\subseteq\mathcal{W}_{2}
(so for
u^{*}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
general one has at least
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u^{*}})\geq 2
), one deduces that
u^{*}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
general has
{\rm corank}(\partial_{u^{*}})=2
. From the properties of the loci
\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
and from Lemma
4.43
,

u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
and
u^{*}\in\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
general give both rise to rank-two vector bundles
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}}
such that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}})=3
and satisfying all the properties (i)-(v) therein. In particular, from Lemma
4.43
-(i), both bundles
\mathcal{F}_{u}
and
\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}}
fit
also in an exact sequence of the form
(u_{p})
, for
p\in C
general. Since

h^{0}(K_{C}-p)=g-1
and
h^{1}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)=h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p)=2
, the fact that
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}})=3
reads, in the vector space
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
, that
u,u^{*}
must be elements in

\left(\mathrm{coker}\left(H^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t}-2p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Phi_{p}^{\vee}}}{{\leftarrow}}H^{0}(K_{C}-p)\otimes H^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p)\right)\right)^{\vee},

in other words they both have to belong to the sub-vector space
\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee})^{\perp}
of
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
. Since, from above,
|g^{2}_{t}-p|
is a base-point-free pencil, then by the base-point-free pencil trick we have

\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee}))=h^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})=h^{1}(g^{2}_{t})=g+2-t
as computed above. Thus,

\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee}))=2(g-1)-(g+2-t)=g+t-4
, whereas

h^{0}(K_{C}+g^{2}_{t}-2p)=\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p))=g+t-3
from (
1.24
) since
K_{C}-p\ncong K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p
. Thus
\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee})^{\perp}\simeq\mathbb{C}
is a
1
-dimensional sub-vector space in
\mathrm{Ext}^{1}(K_{C}-p,K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p)
. Since this holds for
p\in C
general and since, from Lemma
4.43
,
\mathcal{F}_{u^{*}}
admits all quotients
K_{C}-p
, for
p
varying in a suitable open dense subset
U\subseteq C
, this gives rise to a dominant map
\psi
defined as:

U\times\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}:\;\;\;(p,u_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{F}_{u_{p}},

where
u_{p}
is a vector in
\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee})^{\perp}\simeq\mathbb{C}
and where

\mathcal{F}_{u_{p}}
is the corresponding bundle in
\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
. This implies that
\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*})=2
and so that
\mathcal{U}_{2}=\mathcal{U}_{2}^{*}
, i.e. that
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is actually an irreducible component of
\mathcal{W}_{2}
. The previous dominant map
\psi
shows that
\mathcal{U}_{2}
is uniruled.
∎

From Lemma
4.43
-(v) and the bounds on
d
, one has that for
u\in\mathcal{U}_{2}
general the corresponding
bundle
\mathcal{F}_{u}
is stable and of speciality
h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
. To construct the locus
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
and to show that it actually is a component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
, notice that
from Claim
4.45
, for
g^{2}_{t}
varying in a suitable open, dense subset
S\subseteq\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
, we have that
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}:=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{U}_{2})\simeq U\subseteq C
because, for
p\in U
varying,
any
\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee})^{\perp})
becomes a point. Therefore, we get an irreducible projective variety

\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{Tot}
, which is birational to
S\times C
and
of dimension

\operatorname{dim}(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{Tot})=\operatorname{dim}(S)+1=\operatorname{dim}(\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}})+1=(3t-2g-6)+1=3t-2g-5=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}+1,

as it follows from Lemma
4.2
, for the dimension of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
and from the fact that
d=4g-4-t
. Once again from Lemma
4.43
-(v), one has a natural (rational) modular map

(4.71)

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{Tot}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\dashrightarrow}}&U^{s}_{C}(d)\\
u=(p,g^{2}_{t})&\longrightarrow&[\mathcal{F}_{u}],\end{array}

where
[u]=\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{im}(\Phi_{p}^{\vee})^{\perp})
and where

\mathrm{im}(\pi)\subseteq B^{k_{3}}_{d}\cap U_{C}^{s}(d)
.

Claim 4.46
.

The general fiber of
\pi
as in (
4.71
) is
1
-dimensional. In particular,
\operatorname{dim}(\mathrm{im}(\pi))=\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
.

Proof.

Let
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
be general, so
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}_{u}
for
u=(g_{t}^{2},p)\in\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{Tot}
general. Then consider

\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])=

=\left\{u^{\prime}=((g^{2}_{t})^{\prime},p^{\prime})\in\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{2}^{Tot}\,|\;\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}}\simeq\mathcal{F}_{u}\right\}
. Let
u^{\prime}=((g^{2}_{t})^{\prime},p^{\prime})\in\pi^{-1}([\mathcal{F}_{u}])
. Since from the exact sequence (
4.68
) as in Lemma
4.43
one has
2K_{C}-(g^{2}_{t})^{\prime}=\det(\mathcal{F}_{u^{\prime}})\simeq\det(\mathcal{F}_{u})=2K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}
, then one must have
g^{2}_{t}=(g^{2}_{t})^{\prime}
. Therefore
the fiber has to be contained in
U\subseteq C
, but from
Lemma
4.43
-(i), it has to consist
of
U
and the claim is proved.
∎

To conclude that
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
is a dense subset in a regular component
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
we need to compute the Petri map.

Claim 4.47
.

For
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
general, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective. In particular the closure of
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
in
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
, denoted by
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
, gives rise to an irreducible component which is regular.

Proof.

The fact that
\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,3}
is of the expected dimension
\rho_{d}^{k_{3}}
follows from the fact that
\mathrm{im}(\pi)
is a dense subset
in it and from Claim
4.46
. The fact that it is actually a generically smooth, irreducible component of
B_{d}^{k_{3}}\cap U^{s}_{C}(d)
will follow once we show that, for
[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathrm{im}(\pi)
general, the Petri map
\mu_{\mathcal{F}}
is injective. To do so recall that, from Lemma
4.43
,

[\mathcal{F}]\in\mathcal{B}_{\rm reg,\;3}
general fits in exact sequences

(4.72)

(u)\;\;0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}\to 0,

(4.73)

(u_{p})\;\;0\to K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}+p\to\mathcal{F}\to K_{C}-p\to 0,

where
p\in C
general and
g^{2}_{t}
a general element of the component
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
. Set
\mathcal{E}_{u}:=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
. We first notice that
\mathcal{E}_{u}
is globally generated; indeed, from (
4.73
),
\mathcal{E}_{u}
fits in

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(p)\to\mathcal{E}_{u}\to g^{2}_{t}-p\to 0
, where
p\in C
general, so for any arbitrary point
q\neq p
we get

0\to\mathcal{O}_{C}(p-q)\to\mathcal{E}_{u}(-q)\to g^{2}_{t}-p-q\to 0
. Since
g^{2}_{t}
is a general element of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}\subseteq W^{2}_{t}(C)
, by Lemma
4.2

g^{2}_{t}
is birationally very ample, hence
h^{0}(g^{2}_{t}-p-q)=1
for a general
p\in C
. Thus one has
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u}(-q))\leq 1
. Since
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})=h^{1}(\mathcal{F}_{u})=3
, this implies that
\mathcal{E}_{u}
is base-point-free, i.e. that
\mathcal{E}_{u}
is globally generated by its global sections, as claimed.

From the global generation of
\mathcal{E}_{u}
and from
h^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})=3
,
\det(\mathcal{E}_{u})=g^{2}_{t}
one gets therefore:

0\to\det(\mathcal{E}_{u})^{\vee}=-g^{2}_{t}\to\mathcal{O}_{C}\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})\to\mathcal{E}_{u}\to 0;

whence, tensoring by
\mathcal{F}_{u}
, one gets

0\to\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t})\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{E}_{u})\to\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{E}_{u}\to 0,

Taking cohomology, together with
\mathcal{E}_{u}=\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee}
, yields that

(4.74)

0\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))\longrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})\otimes H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}(\omega_{C}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{u}^{\vee})\,,

whence
\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{\mathcal{F}_{u}})\simeq H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))
. Considering (
4.72
) and the exact sequence obtained by tensoring it with
-g^{2}_{t}
gives rise to the following exact diagram

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&0&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&K_{C}-2g^{2}_{t}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t})&\rightarrow&K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&K_{C}-g^{2}_{t}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}&\rightarrow&K_{C}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{F}_{u}|_{g^{2}_{t}}&\rightarrow&\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}}&\rightarrow&0\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&0&&0&&0&&.\end{array}

We notice that
h^{0}(K_{C}-2g^{2}_{t})=0
; indeed we consider the following commutative diagram:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccc}H^{0}(g^{2}_{t})\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{g^{2}_{t}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&&\\[4.30554pt]
\uparrow{\boldsymbol{\iota}}&&\parallel&\\[4.30554pt]
V_{2}\otimes H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mu_{V_{2}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&\end{array}

where
V_{2}
is a general two-dimensional linear subspace of
H^{0}(g^{2}_{t})
, where
\boldsymbol{\iota}
is a natural injection. The map
\mu_{g^{2}_{t}}
is injective, since
g^{2}_{t}
is a general element of
\overline{W^{\vec{w}_{2,0}}}
, therefore by composition also

\mu_{V_{2}}
is injective. Since
\mathbb{P}(V_{2})=|V_{2}|
is a general sub-pencil of the
g^{2}_{t}
, then
|V_{2}|
is a base point free general pencil thus, the base-point-free pencil trick gives

h^{0}(K_{C}-2g^{2}_{t})=\operatorname{dim}(\operatorname{ker}(\mu_{V_{2}}))=0
, as stated.

Thus, passing to cohomology in the previous diagram, we would get:

\begin{array}[]{ccccccccccccccccccccccc}&&&&0&&0&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
&&0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))&\rightarrow&H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\rightarrow&\cdots\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}&H^{0}(K_{C})&\rightarrow&\cdots\\[4.30554pt]
&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\downarrow&&\\[4.30554pt]
0&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{t}&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}|_{g^{2}_{t}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{2t}&\rightarrow&H^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{g^{2}_{t}})\simeq\mathbb{C}^{t}&\rightarrow&\cdots\end{array}

Assume by contradiction that
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))\neq 0
and take any non-zero element
0\neq x\in H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))
; from the composition of injections arising from the previous diagram

H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})\hookrightarrow H^{0}(K_{C}),

the element
x
would identify to a non-zero element
x\in H^{0}(K_{C})
. On the other hand, from the injection in the middle-column
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))\hookrightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
, the element
x
would be an element of
H^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u})
such that
x=\alpha(x)\in H^{0}(K_{C})
, which is a contradiction as
\operatorname{ker}(\alpha)=H^{0}(K_{C}-g^{2}_{t})
from the middle-row. This implies that
h^{0}(\mathcal{F}_{u}(-g^{2}_{t}))=0
, proving Claim
4.47
.
∎

The previous arguments conclude the proof of Proposition
4.44
.
∎
